# Contract Items

Governance scope: the **`grande-sertao-veredas`** repo, Veredas 2 — the successor suite to Veredas 1
(`C:\Source\Repos\Veredas`: its own history, never merged here). Authority: `docs/veredas-plan.md`
wins on intent, this contract on status, `tools/leis` enforces the laws (`docs/questions.md` Q4).
C-001..C-006 are the suite's structural laws, each proven by one test file per law under
`tools/leis/tests/` on synthetic suites (exact violations, by message) plus `real_tree.rs` on the
tree as it is; one file per law, so a change to one law re-stales only its clause. A law changes
only with its clause. From C-007 the clauses are a component's capabilities, one test file per
capability under the component's `tests/`, run against reality (real sockets, the real server),
never a mock; its plan (`docs/<nome>-plan.md`) says what each absorbed from Veredas 1. A tool's
capabilities are clauses too (`tools/alforge`, from C-290), tested under the tool's `tests/`. The
components and the suite-wide rules that need code (among them what the briefing of November 2026 decided, P-015 to P-021) live as
`P-xxx` under **Pending Contract Deltas**, one per component, each citing the plan's section and
the Veredas 1 crates it absorbs; none copies the plan's prose. A member arrives this way: its
`P-xxx` → its `docs/<nome>-plan.md` → its clauses here → `crates/veredas-<nome>` at core's MAJOR →
its simulator in maquinar → `cargo test -p leis` green → reconciled. Runs are recorded under
`.zen/evidence/runs/`, refute verdicts under `.zen/evidence/refutes/`, both committed; what a
refute round found and what it changed is in the clause's evidence note.

## C-001: Members live under crates/ or tools/, each listed by path
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (the plan review of 2026-11-05; docs/veredas-plan.md "Adding a component")
- **Description:** Every `Cargo.toml` under `crates/` (a component) or `tools/` (repo tooling), at any depth, is a workspace member listed by path in `Cargo.toml` or is covered by an `exclude` entry (which covers everything beneath it, as cargo reads it); every member, and every crate found, lives directly under one of those two roots. A listed path with no manifest there (a glob, a typo) is refused; a crate nested in a folder, or at the root folder itself, is refused unless excluded; a `veredas-*` or `orcl-*` crate anywhere the repository holds it or could (by name or by folder, an underscore reading as the hyphen) is a member under a root or nothing, never excluded and never parked elsewhere, so no suite crate dodges the laws by leaving the workspace; a crate elsewhere that is not the suite's is nobody's business. The reader does not walk the disk by a list of folder names: it asks git what the repository holds or could hold (tracked files, and untracked files git does not ignore), so what git would never take is nobody's business and what it would take is seen wherever it sits, a hidden folder, a nested ignore rule un-ignoring `target/`, or a forced add included; a manifest is `Cargo.toml` in any spelling of its case, as a filesystem that ignores case builds it, and a folder holding it under more than one spelling is refused (which one cargo reads would depend on the filesystem; the exact `Cargo.toml` is the text the laws read meanwhile); a submodule, a folder the repository holds whose files it never lists, is a crate by its folder, with its manifest when checked out; a tree that is no repository is refused. A member is a decision taken through the five conditions, never a folder a glob admits.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/leis/tests/l1_roots.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | synthetic suites for each rule, the reader on planted repositories (nested, root-level, hidden and ignored manifests; suite crates elsewhere, under a dot, under an un-ignored target/, forced past the ignore, spelled `cargo.toml`, spelled both ways in one folder, as a checked-out submodule and as a bare submodule pointer), the exclusion as cargo reads it, a suite crate excluded by name, by folder, behind an excluded parent, outside the roots, and under an underscore spelling, a tree without git refused; the real tree
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-001.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-002: A component is named veredas-<nome>, in a folder of its name
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Naming")
- **Description:** A member under `crates/` is `veredas-<nome>`, `<nome>` a lowercase ASCII letter followed by lowercase letters and digits (no hyphen, underscore, accent or space), in the folder `crates/<name>`, matched exactly whatever the filesystem ignores. The language's crates are `orcl-<nome>` by the same rule. A `veredas-*` crate is a component and an `orcl-*` crate is the language's: both live under `crates/`, never `tools/`, whatever follows the prefix, by name or by folder, an underscore reading as the hyphen (as L1 reads a suite crate); a tool is not a component; an extension lives inside its host and is not a member (L1 refuses it listed). That the nome is Portuguese and names the role, and that screens show the accented name, is checked at the component's review, not by the law.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/leis/tests/l2_naming.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | the prefix, the nome's character set, the exact folder, the language's names, a tool, a veredas-* crate under tools/, an extension listed; the real tree
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-002.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-003: SemVer per component from 2.0.0, no shared workspace version
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Versioning")
- **Description:** `[workspace.package]` carries no `version`; every component states its own `version`, valid SemVer with MAJOR ≥ 2 (a pre-release such as `2.0.0-alpha.1` counts), never `version.workspace = true`. A tool under `tools/` versions as it likes. That a new component starts at core's current MAJOR is the plan's rule, checked at the component's review, not by the law.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/leis/tests/l3_versioning.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | the shared version, the inherited version, below 2, not SemVer, absent, a pre-release and a tool; the real tree
- **Status:** verified

## C-004: veredas-core depends on no other member
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-core: the foundation", rules)
- **Description:** `veredas-core` names no other workspace member in any dependency table: normal, dev, build or target-specific, in cargo's hyphen or pre-2024 underscore spelling, a dependency renamed with `package` or inherited from `[workspace.dependencies]` (named by the root entry alone, as cargo ignores a `package` written beside `workspace = true`) included. Its own self dev-dependency (a test feature) is allowed. The foundation builds and tests alone.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/leis/tests/l4_core.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | every table, the self dev-dependency, a target table, a rename in the member, a rename in the workspace table with a member's `package` beside it ignored, the underscore tables; the real tree
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-004.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-005: Dependencies point one way, along the map
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Architecture" and each component's rules; docs/questions.md Q5)
- **Description:** Every edge from a component to another member in its normal or build dependencies, however declared (a path, a `package` rename, a `workspace = true` inheritance), is in `MAP` (`tools/leis/src/lib.rs`): anything may use core; portal uses core alone; maquinar uses core and aranha (its codecs, to emulate the devices aranha scans); aranha uses core; the apps (comissionador, supervisao, abaco, wms) use portal; controlador uses portal, aranha and the `orcl-*` crates; `orcl-*` use each other. A component absent from the map may use core alone, and adding its row is a contract change. A dev-dependency is free (a bench is a fixture), a tool may depend on anything, and a crate that is not a member is not the map's business. The tests carry their own copy of this sentence and walk every pair of the planned suite against it, so a row of `MAP` cannot drift from the clause unnoticed.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/leis/tests/l5_direction.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | the planned suite, app→app, bench→portal, portal→app, a component outside the map, build vs dev, a tool, a non-member, a workspace-table rename, every pair of the planned suite against the clause's copy of the map; the real tree
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-005.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-006: No component's code lands without its plan
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Adding a component", condition 1)
- **Description:** Every component `veredas-<nome>` has `docs/<nome>-plan.md`, not blank (whitespace, the byte-order mark and the zero-width characters U+200B–U+200D and U+2060 are not text), and so does every commit reachable from HEAD that holds a component under `crates/` (`veredas-*` or `orcl-*` by name, a folder, a submodule or a link, present at HEAD or not: a renamed or deleted component's landing is judged too): a law over git's trees, every commit judged (merges, re-additions and a plan deleted then restored included), the plan a regular file of text (a folder or a symlink in its place is no plan; bytes that are not UTF-8 are blank), the earliest offending commit named once per folder, so no component's code ever landed without its plan, whatever the tree says today. The language's crates share `docs/orcl/README.md`; the suite's own `docs/veredas-plan.md` is no component's plan, in history as in the tree; a tool needs no plan; a component not yet committed is the tree law's business; a shallow clone, its history cut, is refused rather than judged. The law answers in a fixed number of git conversations whatever the history's length. The suite runs on every push (`.github/workflows/leis.yml`, full history) and before every commit by the repo's rule. What the plan must say (the one-sentence role nobody covers, its scope and rules) is checked at the component's review, not by the law.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/leis/tests/l6_plans.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | absent, blank, a byte-order mark, a title, the language's shared plan, a tool, a veredas-* crate under tools/ through the laws together, the reader leaving the suite's plan to nobody, throwaway git repositories where the plan comes late, together or blank, where the code lands on a merged branch or in the merge commit itself, is removed and re-added, or its plan is deleted and restored, where the component was renamed or deleted since, where a folder or a symlink stands for the plan, where the plan is UTF-16, where the suite's plan is the only plan, where the component is a submodule or a link, a history of three thousand commits answered within a bound, and where the component is uncommitted, there is no repository, or the clone is shallow; the real tree and its history
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-006.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-007: veredas-core — the foundation
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-core: the foundation"; promoted from P-001)
- **Description:** `crates/veredas-core` holds what more than one component needs (the plan's scope; `docs/core-plan.md` maps what it absorbed from Veredas 1's `veredas-telegram` (its socket, framing and capture only), `veredas-opc` and core, and the phases still ahead: file schemas, logging), content-agnostic (no source or test file names the GENIBELT protocol or cites a vendor document: what a device's telegrams mean is configuration; `docs/questions.md` Q8), as a library that builds no executable: cargo, asked what it would build, names exactly one library target (crate types `lib`/`rlib`) and tests, nothing else. It depends on no other member, its plan came before its code, and it versions on its own from 2.0.0 (L3; it started at 2.0.0-alpha.1). Each capability is a clause of its own below.

- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-007.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-008: The shared types: one value model, a tag, a sample on two clocks, quality
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-core: the foundation", scope; docs/core-plan.md "Scope")
- **Description:** A `Value` is an int, float, text or naive time, with a numeric view for all but text (a time as the epoch milliseconds of its stamp read as UTC, rounded down), and displays as the operator reads it; `SeriesType` names the four; a tag is a `SeriesRef`, a series of one source, shown `source/series`, ordered by source then series (not by the shown text); a `Sample` indexes on its parsed stamp, else on arrival, on the value's millisecond axis; `Quality` is good, uncertain or bad, and only good is good; `now_unix_ms` is the unix clock in milliseconds.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/types.rs | Veredas 1's tests plus the edges: case and ordering of ids, tags ordered apart from their shown text (`plc` before `plc-2`), negative and wide ints, text never parsed, times before the epoch and below the millisecond, NaN, display of each variant and of fractional seconds, int vs float equality, both index paths with negative clocks, the quality labels, arrival bracketed by the system clock
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-008.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-009: One stop/join handle for every source worker
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md "Scope", worker plumbing)
- **Description:** A source worker is a thread ending in `Result<(), SourceError>`, held by a `SourceHandle`: polling it never blocks a live worker, reads finished once the worker has ended, keeps the outcome (its error, or `Panicked` for a panic, whatever the panic's payload) and gives `join` that same outcome without joining twice; stopping raises the worker's own flag, and dropping the handle raises it without waiting. A `SourceError` names an I/O failure with its path.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/source_handle.rs | real threads: a live worker polled without blocking, stop reaching a worker, an error kept across polls and join, join alone, an ended worker finished before any poll, a panic as `Panicked` (a payload whose drop panics too included), a drop that does not wait, every error's text and its source chain
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-009.md
- **Status:** verified


## C-010: The framing engine: any configured layout, frames that never lie about their size
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-core", scope: a framing engine driven by a channel configuration schema; docs/questions.md Q8, Q9; docs/channel-schema.md)
- **Description:** Core holds no frame layout: a `Framing` compiled from a channel file encodes and decodes whatever it describes (optional start and end delimiters; envelope, segment-header and tail fields of 1 to 8 bytes in either byte order, each opaque, or the envelope's sequence, data-area length or segment count, or a segment's body length), a frame with no segments being a keep-alive. Encoding computes every length and count, stamps the sequence given, fills opaque fields from the frame or their configured defaults, and refuses a value its field cannot hold or a frame over the limit, never clamping; decoding returns the frame and the bytes it used, asks for more only while the delimiter, envelope and whole segment headers that have arrived could still be a valid frame, and refuses a frame whose lengths disagree once the envelope or the segment header that shows it has arrived whole (never holding the real frames behind it); every length or count a peer declares is bounded by the frame limit before any arithmetic, so a field 8 bytes wide never wraps, and a data area, body or segment count that can never fit the limit, or a body that leaves no room for the segment headers still to come, is refused at once rather than waited for, as is a data area that its declared count of segments could not fill (each at most its header and the largest body its length field can declare); a refusal says how far to skip to the next occurrence of the start delimiter's first byte (one byte where there is no start delimiter). Hex text parses as two ASCII hex digits per byte; previews and dumps render bodies for an operator.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/telegram_framing.rs | on synthetic layouts read from channel files: the segmented envelope (Veredas 1's codec tests ported: golden frames byte for byte in both orders, every prefix waiting, exact resync distances, inconsistent lengths refused early, the remaining-headers boundary on both sides, a wrong byte order read exactly, the frame limit at its edge both ways), opaque fields and their defaults, the sequence stamp, values that do not fit; a length-prefixed layout with no delimiter or segments; a delimited layout counted by segments; 8-byte envelope lengths, segment lengths and counts at `u64::MAX` and 2^63 refused without wrapping, and each limit (data area, body, count, headers still to come) pinned on both sides with the frame at its edge decoded whole; a data area too large for its count of one-byte-length segments refused at the envelope and after a header, both sides of each bound, real frames behind it never held; a hand-built framing past the ceiling waiting rather than wrapping; hex, previews and dumps
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-010.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-012: One TCP telegram link, held correctly in either role
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md; docs/questions.md Q8, Q9: the rules are the channel file's; docs/channel-schema.md)
- **Description:** A `Channel` listens or dials on its own thread, framing by its channel file and holding the link by that file's rules: with `[sequence]` rules, on connect each side sends a keep-alive stamped `connect`, then stamps `first..=last` at send time, a number taken only by a frame that encodes (a send the encoder refuses takes none), and the receiver skips a frame repeating the last number it saw, keep-alives counting as seen when `keepalive_counts` says so (bar the connect keep-alive's, `connect` lying outside the cycle); a keep-alive, the channel's probe or one its owner sends (any frame the peer will read as one), takes the next number when keep-alives count, and carries the last number stamped, taking none, when they do not, so data frames never come back round to the last one; a keep-alive (a frame with no segments; in a segmentless layout, an empty body) is delivered as a keep-alive event carrying its fields; a keep-alive on connect when `on_connect`; silence for `idle` sends one keep-alive probe per window, never while the peer talks (zero never probes); a dialler retries until the listener appears; replies are unicast by a peer id never reused, so a reply for a departed peer is dropped, never delivered to its successor; frames go out as soon as the socket takes them and every queued command is handled at once, so a peer that reads keeps up with any burst its outbox bound holds, whatever other peers are connected, and the channel keeps reading meanwhile; a peer whose socket takes nothing for the write timeout is dropped, the socket itself asked (never a readiness cache, so a slow but reading peer is never judged deaf), judged without waiting, so it stalls no other peer and no shutdown; garbage is resynced past and counted; a bind failure is reported and the channel still stops.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/telegram_channel.rs | over real loopback TCP: two channels both ways, the sequence and its wrap from 255 to 1, the connect keep-alive's 0 in both roles, a duplicate skipped, a keep-alive between two frames on a raw socket and a collision after 254 probes between two channels, the idle probe counted and silent while the peer talks, the departed peer, a 300-frame burst to a reading peer alone and beside three others, a 3 s stream, a deaf peer fed until the write timeout judges it (with a healthy peer never unread for 400 ms meanwhile, and a new peer served after), stop behind a backlog to four deaf peers, a peer reading 100 KB/s under an 8 MB burst never dropped while most of it is still queued, uncounted probes read off a raw socket carrying the last number, two uncounted channels across a whole cycle of probes never skipping, 254 keep-alives sent by the owner between two frames (uncounted and counted) and 254 empty messages of a segmentless layout between two data messages never costing one, two refused sends between two frames in a 1..=3 cycle (unicast and to all) taking no number, a segmentless layout's empty body delivered as a keep-alive with its field, a late listener, garbage before a frame, a port already taken; every channel configured by a channel file (the segmented envelope with sequence rules, or a bare layout), message ids neutral
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-012.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-013: Message bodies read into named fields by rules
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md; docs/questions.md Q8: encoding and decoding by rules)
- **Description:** A `CaptureSpec`, configuration rather than code (read from TOML text, optional keys taking their defaults, a misspelled key or type refused), reads a message body into named fields: a regex's named groups, binary fields at byte offsets in either order, and repeated patterns as `name1`, `name2`... up to a cap; a field past the body's end does not match and never panics; a binary field wins a name collision; a bad pattern is a warning that drops that one capture. Its inverse is C-027 (`compose`), which writes a body from the same `BinaryField` list; C-028's catalog gives each message one such list for both.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/telegram_capture.rs | Veredas 1's tests, plus every field type's exact value in both orders, fields running past the end by one byte, a repeat's own cap and a repeat without a group, one bad pattern among good captures, a binary field over a repeat, a spec read from TOML with its defaults and equal to the literal one, all seven type names read and wrong spellings refused, a misspelled key refused in each of the three tables (the scanner-request tests left with the GENIBELT path, Q8)
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-013.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-016: A deterministic PCG32
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md, test-fixture plumbing)
- **Description:** `Pcg32` is the reference PCG32 (XSH-RR 64/32): it replays the same sequence from the same seed and stream, and diverges across streams, a stream id taken modulo 2^63 as in the reference; `next_f64` is in [0, 1); `uniform` stays within any finite range at its rounding, never NaN; `int_in` covers both ends of any `i64` range, never overflowing; `flip` gives both faces.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/rng.rs | Veredas 1's tests, plus the reference demo's output for seed 42 stream 54, a thousand mixed draws replayed, streams 0 and 2^63 equal, ranges wider than `i64`, both ends at the extremes, `uniform` over (-f64::MAX, f64::MAX) from a zero draw, `next_f64` exact on the reference draws, 0 from the zero state and under 1 over a million draws
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-016.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-017: The channel file: a telegram channel as versioned, validated plain text
- **Date:** 2026-11-05
- **Source:** user (docs/questions.md Q9, Q10; docs/veredas-plan.md "Versioned files"; P-010)
- **Description:** A telegram channel is configured by a TOML file stating `format = "veredas-channel"` and `version = 1` (`docs/channel-schema.md`): its framing (delimiters, envelope, segment header, tail, byte order, frame limit), its sequence rules, its keep-alive rules and its link rules, every optional key with its documented default. `schema::load` refuses another format, an unknown or missing version, a misspelled key, an unknown role, a size out of 1 to 8, a role in the wrong part or twice, a computed field given any value (0 included), a default too wide for its field, a name used twice, a layout that cannot end, an empty or non-hex delimiter, a frame limit below the fixed bytes or over 16 MiB, a sequence field without `[sequence]` rules (an empty `[sequence]` takes the defaults), sequence rules with no sequence field, out of order, a cycle of one number that skips duplicates, or a `connect` inside the cycle (`docs/questions.md` Q11), a zero write timeout, an outbox smaller than one largest frame, and no peers, naming the place; it never guesses. The worked example in the documentation loads and frames as its prose describes it.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/telegram_schema.rs | the documentation's own example block loaded and checked field by field (envelope, segment header and tail as name, size and role), its 11-byte envelope, 3-byte tail, 16-byte keep-alive and 22-byte data frame checked against the prose that states them; every default pinned, a per-field byte order, every limit accepted at its edge (`connect` just outside the cycle on either side, the frame limit at exactly the fixed bytes, one peer), the keep-alive and sequence keys read away from their defaults, a segmented layout ended by its length, its count or both, the framing byte order reaching the segment header and the tail byte for byte, every width at its top (8-byte fields, the widest value of a byte, a 2-byte cycle to 65535), the header (format, version) refused four ways, misspelled keys in every section (`[sequence]` included), and every invalid layout with its exact message (the fixed bytes counted with a tail, a `connect` too wide for its field, every role in every part that does not take it, a count given a value, a default too wide for a 2-byte field included); every key the reader knows either refused when left out (documented required) or loading with exactly the default the reference states; each of 191 one-line mutations killed by this test alone: of the reader (every check in every part, every role list, every copied field, default and inheritance, every `deny_unknown_fields`, the header compared exactly, multi-byte delimiters) and of the documentation (every number of the worked example and its segment id; every default, floor, limit, range, the format and the version of the key reference; every section, key, role, byte order, role part, required or optional flag and delimiter example it names; each read from the text and compared with the reader)
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-017.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-018: An OPC source compiled: node ids, the polled set, columns, the writable whitelist
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md, phase 3: the OPC UA client layer)
- **Description:** A `PollSpec` is what an OPC source's configuration compiles to: an endpoint, an identity (`Auth`, anonymous or user and password, whose password shows in neither its `label` nor its `Debug`, nor so a spec's), a poll interval, and rules naming tables whose columns are tags and their bit fields. `parse_node_id` reads the standard `ns=<n>;<i|s|g|b>=<value>` text, surrounding whitespace ignored, and refuses blank text (`NodeIdError::Empty`) or any other text (`Malformed`, the input quoted and the expected form shown). `poll_nodes` lists every distinct node once, however it is spelled, first-seen across rules; a rule's columns are each tag's own column (none for a bits-only tag) then its bit fields, each an `Int`; a writable tag is found only by its configured text, exactly but for surrounding whitespace (never by a blank, a prefix, an extension or another spelling of the same node), and only when marked writable, an unknown and a read-only tag answering alike; a write flag is always respected, the same text also configured read-only anywhere not hiding the writable tag (the architect, 2026-11-06).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/opc_spec.rs | every node id form (string, numeric, guid, opaque), with and without a namespace, each decoded to its own identifier kind and value, whitespace trimmed, the text form round-tripping; blank text refused as empty and six malformed spellings with the input quoted, each message exact; the node set deduplicated across and within rules in first-seen order, and empty; different nodes sharing an id value (two namespaces, a numeric and a string `5`) all kept; the same text configured read-only and writable, in either order across two rules and within one, resolving to the writable tag; column names and types with bits in place as ints whatever the parent's type, a bits-only tag and a tag with neither; writable lookup across rules, read-only and unknown alike, whitespace on either side of the request or the configuration; another node by case, a blank, a prefix, an extension at either end (`ns=1;s=cmdX`, `Xns=1;s=cmd`, `ns=2;s=ns=1;s=cmd`), a doubled id, a truncation and `ns=01;s=cmd` (the same node as `ns=1;s=cmd` to the stack) never the tag, for either lookup; tags configured as `ns=0;i=5` and `ns=01;s=cmd` found by that text and not by the spelling the stack prints (`i=5`, `ns=1;s=cmd`), and three spellings of one node (`ns=0;i=5`, `i=5`, `ns=00;i=5`) polled once; the label and the debug forms of an identity and a spec without the password
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-018.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-019: An OPC value into the value model, and bits out of words
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md, phase 3: the OPC UA client layer)
- **Description:** `opc_to_value` maps a `Variant` onto the series type its rule declares: every integer wire type, and a Boolean as 0 or 1, into an `Int` exactly (a UInt64 above `i64::MAX` refused); any integer or float into a `Float` (rounding past 2^53 as any float does); a float never into an `Int` (refused, never truncated); anything into `Text` (strings, localized text, qualified names and status codes as their text, numbers as numbers); only a DateTime into a `Time`, as naive UTC; an empty value refused for every type. `bit_field` reads `width` bits (0 read as 1, more than 64 as 64) from bit `bit` of an integer's own two's-complement pattern at its wire width, so bits past the word read 0 and a negative word shows only its own bits; a field at bit 64 or above is 0; a field too wide for an `Int` is refused, never wrapped; a non-integer is refused wherever the field starts. Each refusal is a `ValueError`; every one but an empty value's names the wire type, whichever of the stack's types it is (none is "unsupported").
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/opc_value.rs | every integer wire type at both edges into int and float; Boolean; empty refused for all four series types; floats exact (an f32 widened exactly, f64::MAX, −0, NaN); text from strings, numbers, booleans, localized text, qualified names and status codes; a DateTime to the millisecond, three other types refused as times and a DateTime refused as a number; a UInt64 at and past `i64::MAX`; text never a number; each of the fifteen non-numeric wire types refused as an int, a float, a time (but DateTime) and for bits, by its own name, the message exact; each of the eleven numeric types refused as a time by its own name; single bits across a word and past its top (16, 20, 63, 64, 255), a Boolean's and a Byte's bits at their width; packed fields, width 0 and past 64, a field over the word's top; negative SByte, Int16, Int32 and Int64 at their own width (no sign extension); a UInt64's top bits, a 64-bit field refused for UInt64 and Int64 while `i64::MAX` fits; non-integers refused for bits at bit 0, 64 and 200; every refusal's exact text
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-019.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-020: One poll into rows and samples
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md, phase 3: the OPC UA client layer)
- **Description:** `map_poll` turns one poll's reads (looked up by node, so a tag shared by rules is read once and lands in each) into one row per rule, positional against its columns, and one sample per mapped value on the poll's clock and sequence. A tag read without a value (none, or an empty variant) is a hole in each of its columns, reported once per tag in each rule that maps it (on its own column, else its first bit field) with the server's status, or "no value" for a good status; a value that does not fit its series or bit field is a hole reported on that column; a hole yields no sample; a node absent from the poll is a hole with nothing to report. `map_poll_dedup` keeps a `store_on_change` rule's poll only when its values (holes included, a NaN equal to a NaN) differ from the last kept, else on every `HEARTBEAT_POLLS`-th quiet poll; it skips that rule alone and still reports its failures; without the memory or the flag every poll is kept. `render_line` is `rule column=value ...`, `?` for a hole, always one line (a control character or a Unicode line or paragraph separator read as a space).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/opc_map.rs | a row with bits expanded and its samples field by field; a bad tag with bits a hole in three columns reported once, the rest intact, no sample for holes; a bad bits-only word reported on its first bit; a good status with no value, and with an empty variant on a tag with bits and on a bits-only word, each once; a bad tag mapped by two rules reported in each; an absent node; a type mismatch's exact text; bits of a float reported per bit while its own column maps; a shared tag landing in two rules; the rendered line with a hole and a text holding CR, LF, tab, NEL and both Unicode separators on one line, and a rule with no tags; the heartbeat at exactly every 60th quiet poll, a transition kept, no memory and no flag keeping all; a float stuck at NaN, and a steady 21.5, each kept once then at the heartbeat, a number after either and NaN again each a transition; a rule that gained a trailing column, its first values unchanged, kept; one rule skipped while another keeps, its failure still reported; a hole appearing and healing as transitions; the memory following a spec that gains a rule
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-020.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-021: The address-space browser
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md, phase 3: the OPC UA client layer)
- **Description:** A `Browser` opens a connection of its own, on its own thread, and answers one request at a time. A node's children: every hierarchical reference kind (so HasComponent subtrees appear), keyed by the node asked about, continuation points followed to the end (at most 64 pages, the first counted, then refused), each child once however often it is referenced, ordered by display name, with its node id text, names, whether it is a variable, whether it is worth expanding to find tags (`worth_expanding`: an object, a view, a variable or a node of no stated class; a method or a type is a leaf even with children), and a variable's DataType with the series type a built-in maps to. A node's attributes: rows in a fixed order, the node id first, an attribute the node lacks skipped, any other refusal shown, each in words. A browse result with a bad status is a refusal naming that status, never an empty folder, the first page and every continuation meeting the one check; a malformed node id fails its request alone. The connection is bounded by the connect timeout and every call by the browse timeout; a failed connection is reported and nothing follows; dropping the browser returns within half a second whatever it is doing (connecting, resolving a host name, browsing a stalled server), its CloseSession bounded rather than awaited and its thread run on `run_on_own_runtime`, which never waits for a blocking task left on the runtime. The renderers are exact: every built-in type name with its series mapping, access-level bits, value rank and node class in words, a value brief of at most 160 characters then `…`, on one line.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/opc_browse.rs | numeric ids 0 to 30 mapped or not, custom and string ids not; every renderer's exact text (all 25 built-in names, access levels with unnamed bits, each value rank and node class, mapped, unmapped and custom data types); the refusal's exact text for a browse and a continuation, good-and-empty and uncertain not refused; the brief of each scalar kind, arrays at and past four items, cut past 160 characters but not at 160, by characters for multi-byte text, control characters and Unicode separators as spaces. Against the demo plant over real OPC UA: Connected first; the root ordered by name; the five tags with names, flags, series type and DataType exactly; a HasComponent subtree three levels deep and the Server object listed; `worth_expanding` for all nine node classes; a method of the Server object neither a variable nor a container though it has arguments, its objects containers; the standard BaseObjectType a leaf though it has subtypes; a view in the Views folder a container holding what it organizes; sampling-interval rows of 100 ms and indeterminate; the null node refused by the server as a named refusal; an unknown node empty on this lax stack, its attribute rows each its refusal; a child linked twice listed once; a malformed and a blank id failing as node-id errors while the next request is served; a 1 200-child folder whole, in order, each typed (two pages, chunked DataType reads); on a server serving one reference per page, 64 children across 64 pages whole and typed, 65 refused after 64 pages with the exact text, the next request served; a variable's attribute rows exactly, a writable tag's access, an object's rows; an unreachable endpoint failing with nothing after it and a prompt drop; a silent endpoint failing at the 10 s connect timeout; through a frozen relay, a browse timing out at 15 s, then a drop mid-browse under 500 ms; a drop mid-connect, and while a host name resolves, under 500 ms (biting where a failed lookup is slow); a 10 s blocking task left on `run_on_own_runtime` not holding its thread: the call returns while the task still runs, and its shutdown (from the future's last await to the call's return) takes under 500 ms (on any resolver; neither the runtime's build nor the thread's start counted)
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-021.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-022: The poll worker: reconnection, polls on two clocks, and gated writes
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md, phase 3: the OPC UA client layer)
- **Description:** `spawn` runs one source's worker on its own thread (a zero interval refused) and returns its `SourceHandle` and an `OpcHandle`. The worker reports Connecting, Connected, or Retrying with a typed reason and its wait (`retry_delays`: 500 ms, doubling, held at 30 s, starting over after every session); a connection attempt is bounded by 10 s; a poll reads every distinct node in chunks of at most 500, each call bounded by 10 s, and `tag_reads` turns each answer into one `TagRead` per node in order (a short answer padded `BadNoData`); a poll is emitted with the plant-local index and the unix arrival clock; a read or write call that fails or times out, or a lost connection, ends the session and reconnects; a stop ends the worker within a fraction of a second whether connecting (a host name still resolving included: the thread runs on `run_on_own_runtime`, which never waits for a blocking task left on the runtime), polling, serving or waiting, its CloseSession bounded at 100 ms; a consumer gone ends the worker. A `TagRead` carries a value only with a good status and a non-empty variant, and its quality is good only then, uncertain when the server says so, bad otherwise. Writes go only through `write_tags`: the master switch first (`Disarmed`), then every tag against the spec's whitelist (`NotWritable`, unknown and read-only alike, a batch refused whole), then the worker checks the switch again just before the wire; a request whose caller stopped waiting before the worker reached it (its own timeout dropped the future) is never sent. A `WriteError` says what the plant may have seen: `Disarmed`, `NotWritable` and `NotSent` (no session, answered at once and never fired later; or still queued when the worker ended) mean nothing reached it; `Unconfirmed` (the call failed or timed out, or the worker stopped mid-call) means it may have, now or when a stalled link recovers. A call the server takes returns its per-write statuses in order. The switch is shared and live.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/opc_worker.rs | on a stopped worker: each gate's exact refusal and their order (disarmed before the whitelist, unknown and read-only alike, a batch refused whichever tag is bad, a permitted write reaching the closed channel as not sent, an empty batch, the switch flipping live and through a clone), writable types, a zero interval refused, quality for each status and value; `tag_reads` on an answer short by one, with no status, with a value beside bad and uncertain statuses, with an empty variant, and longer than the request; the delays 500 ms to 30 s and held. Against the demo plant: Connecting then Connected; the first poll in node order and good; typed rows with bits; a change picked up; a prompt stop; a write landing server-side while polling goes on, a pulse's second half, per-write statuses with a read-only node refused in place, an on-demand read with a bad node; a disarmed switch leaving the plant untouched, armed live, disarmed again, a tag outside the whitelist refused though the server would take it; a write queued behind the 400 ms slow tag refused by a disarm made while it waited (the plant untouched), the same write landing when the switch stays armed; a read in flight and a write queued at a stop answered dropped and not sent, the plant untouched; a write queued behind the slow tag and abandoned by its caller's 50 ms timeout never landing, the same write awaited landing; a stop while a host name resolves under 500 ms (biting where a failed lookup is slow); a 10 s blocking task left on `run_on_own_runtime` not holding its thread: the call returns while the task still runs, and its shutdown (from the future's last await to the call's return) takes under 500 ms (on any resolver; neither the runtime's build nor the thread's start counted); a bad node a reported hole while polling goes on; an unreachable server retried at 500, 1 000 and 2 000 ms with a typed reason, a stop mid-backoff under 1 s; the waits starting over at 500 ms after a session; a silent server given up on at 10 s, a stop mid-connect under 500 ms; through a frozen relay, a poll timing out into Retrying with the read's reason within 12 s, a stop mid-poll under 400 ms, a write unconfirmed at 10 s with the session dropped, a stop mid-write under 400 ms answered unconfirmed; writes and reads during an outage refused under 500 ms and never landing after the plant returns, a write landing then; a killed plant noticed and its restart found; 1 200 tags, past the server's 800-node cap, polled and read on demand in order; a right password polling, a wrong one retrying and never connected; a dropped consumer ending the worker; the plant index one local offset from the unix arrival, on a machine set to UTC (Unix) by running again in a zone 5 h 30 east
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-022.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-023: What the OPC UA stack does, pinned against a real server
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/core-plan.md, phase 3: `capabilities.rs`, run first after a version bump)
- **Description:** `async-opcua` 0.19, pinned exactly, behaves as the OPC layer assumes, checked against the in-process demo plant over real OPC UA: anonymous and username/password sessions, a wrong password or an unknown user refused promptly; a batch Read answers one result per node in request order, a good one with no bad status, an unknown node as a bad status rather than a failed call; a Write answers one status per write in order and lands in the address space, a read-only node refused in its place; a Browse lists children with node id, display name, class and type definition, with or without a class filter; a Read past the server's per-call cap fails whole with the server's own status (`BadTooManyOperations`), one at the cap does not, and the session survives; a Read of more than 1 000 nodes fails to decode (`BadDecodingError`) whatever the cap, the stack's array limit, which the worker's chunk stays under; a browse page of 1 000 references (the browser's) decodes, and one past the limit kills the connection; a Browse returns at most the references asked for, and at most the server's own page, with a continuation point that BrowseNext follows to the end, and a released point is gone; a status code's severity is its top two bits (0b11 reading as bad, a good code with sub-status bits still good) and it displays as its name; a session closed with CloseSession frees its slot (the stop waits 100 ms for the answer, which an idle loopback meets and a loaded host may miss, the slot then the server's to time out: a limit no test here holds), and one dropped without it keeps the slot; the attributes the browser reads come as the types its renderers match on, and one a node lacks as `BadAttributeIdInvalid`. The demo plant (feature `demo-server`) serves the node set the tests name, its read cap under its bulk folder and the array limit, a page and a session limit of its own when configured, and a slow tag that takes its stated time.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/opc_capabilities.rs | anonymous connect and a three-node read in order with timestamps, mapped through the value layer (float, bits, text); an unknown node's exact status beside a good one; the right credentials reading, a wrong password and an unknown user each refused within 10 s; a two-node write landing server-side, a pulse, a read-only node refused in place beside a good write; the five children with their metadata and the folder under Objects, and again with class mask 0; 801 nodes refused as `BadTooManyOperations` with the exact text, 800 read with the last value right, the session still usable; 1 001 nodes refused as `BadDecodingError` with the exact text, the stack's limit pinned at 1 000 and the chunk under it; 1 200 children paged 500 at a time across exactly two BrowseNext rounds; a released point answering Good then `BadContinuationPointInvalid`; a server paging one reference when 1 000 are asked; a full 1 000-reference page decoded, the page size asserted at the limit; on a server holding one session, three sessions in turn each closed with CloseSession answered, then one dropped unclosed leaving the next refused `BadTooManySessions`; on a server paging 5 000, 1 000 references decoded and 1 001 closing the connection; severity for good, uncertain, bad, the reserved bits, GoodClamped, GoodLocalOverride and four good codes with info bits, five names; a variable's DataType, NodeClass, AccessLevel, UserAccessLevel, ValueRank, MinimumSamplingInterval and BrowseName in their wire types, its EventNotifier `BadAttributeIdInvalid` with no value; the slow tag between 400 ms and 2.4 s
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-023.md

a binary of another name, two binaries, `[[example]]`, `examples/`, a `crate-type` naming `bin`, a `cdylib`, an empty `crate-type`, a bench, a build script and a package with a binary but no library each refused; every file of the crate, whatever its extension, the manifest included, and every file rustc read for the crate, taken from the dep-info of a nested `cargo build --all-targets --all-features` and a `cargo check --release` of the same into the test's own target dir (the folder above the test cargo marks as its own, `CACHEDIR.TAG`), read wherever cargo's layout puts a build unit (every `.d` under the profile's folder of the target and build directories `cargo metadata` reports for that target dir, `<profile>/deps/` or a unit's folder under `<profile>/build/`), a dep-info chosen by its crate root being one of the package's targets as cargo lists them, every target required to have one in each profile (pinned on a planted crate built for real whose targets pull in twenty-five outside files, each holding a term, by every form: each include macro with parentheses, brackets or braces, a plain, raw and hashed-raw string, `concat!` of literals with and without `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` or `env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME")` in any spacing, a comment between the macro and its argument, an include through a macro variable and through a `use ... as` alias, `#[path]` with inner whitespace, inside `cfg_attr`, after a comment holding an unclosed `#[`, and inside an inline module, a nested include, a `[[test]]` whose `path` lies outside the crate folder, a file behind a feature that is off by default, this platform's file of a `cfg(windows)`/`cfg(unix)` pair, a file behind `cfg(debug_assertions)` and one behind its negation; a module never declared, an include under `cfg(any())`, one under `cfg(doc)` and the other platform's file not read; the dep-info line forms), `cargo test --doc` reporting no doctest, read whole, normalised to letters and digits across line breaks, for the vendor's name and protocol terms and for vendor-document and section citations, the reader checked on planted spellings (a fixture's comment included); ## C-025: Log emitters: lines from a template into files a plant would write
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-maquinar", scope: log emitters; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope")
- **Description:** An emitter (`emit::spawn`, from an `EmitterConfig` read from TOML with every optional key defaulted and a misspelled key refused) writes, on its interval, a line rendered from a template (`{ts}` by a chrono format, a bad format rendered as a marked message and never a panic; `{name}` a value drawn within its range, an inverted range swapped, to its decimals; anything else as typed) into files named by a pattern of chrono specifiers and `{n}` (a pattern without `{n}` names index 1 bare and later indices `_<n>` before an extension, never inside a dotted date), rotating to the next index after `max_lines` lines (0 never) and back to index 1 on a new day, one clock deciding the file and stamping the line (the `Writer` writes one line at the instant it is given; the worker gives it the wall clock); a name this run has not itself opened is taken by creating the file new, never by opening one that exists (the first, a count, a day, a folder that was away and came back, another writer racing for the same name), so the check and the claim are one step and no run ever appends to a file this, an earlier or a concurrent run wrote, whatever gaps the family has; a name that exists is skipped, and when every name tried exists (`MAX_SKIPS`, 10 000, lowered by a test) nothing is written, no file shows open and the status says so; a file is never opened by name: after a failed write the file is left (`release`, which a bench may also call to rotate on demand) and the next line takes a fresh name, so a file lost, replaced or taken by another writer meanwhile is never appended to; a line and its ending are one write; CRLF, flushed per line, UTF-8 or UTF-16LE with a byte-order mark once on a fresh file; a missing folder is created, one that cannot be is reported with its path and retried until it can; an empty pattern, one that is no format, one rendering a character no file name holds, a name ending in a dot or a space (which the system would drop), or a name the system reserves (`CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`...) is refused by name before anything is written; the status reads lines total and in file, the current file and the last error, typed; a seed replays the same values; a stop lands within a slice of 100 ms. `family_shape` gives the regex (a `date` group from the first specifier to the last one reached through single literal separators (carrying a year and no specifier beyond it, or the pattern has no shape: a date the reader could not read back whole), an `idx` group where the first `{n}` stands, a second `{n}` matched as digits, or, for a pattern without `{n}`, optional where the index is inserted; only regex metacharacters escaped, any other character standing for itself) and the date format holding exactly what the group covers, separators included, so every name the pattern renders matches and its date parses back; none for a pattern with no date, an uncommon specifier, another placeholder or a forbidden character.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/emitters.rs | Veredas 1's tests plus the edges: rendering (a lone percent, a stray close brace, an empty placeholder, a name twice, a negative value at zero decimals), draws replayed by seed and within range at their decimals, every naming rule (`{n}` anywhere and twice, index 0, an extension that is not one, trimmed whitespace) and every refusal by name with its text (every forbidden character, a tab, a trailing dot, four reserved names and two that only look reserved), the rotor's first file as a rotation, the same day late, the index and count after each step, a skip, a bad pattern leaving it in place, index 0 read as 1, the family shape for dotted and dashed dates, two-digit fields, every metacharacter escaped and a non-ASCII literal not, a second `{n}`, and ten patterns matched against the names they render at three indices with the index group right (absent for a bare index 1) and the date parsing back; against a real directory: rotation at three lines with the names in order and the status equal to the disk, a seeded pair writing the same lines, a restart growing no earlier file and taking the next index (with and without count rotation, and past a gap in the family with every old file byte-identical), the writer driven across midnight (a 23:59 line in that day's file, a 00:00 line in the next day's, an existing file of the new day skipped), a folder that comes back holding an earlier run's file of the very name the rotor had moved to (untouched, the next name taken, the following line landing in the open file), a released file gone meanwhile (the next line in a fresh name, two more staying in it), a released name taken by a second writer (never shared, the first writer on to the next name), a foreign file under the next name skipped and a fresh UTF-16 file with its own byte-order mark, a release with nothing open changing nothing, on Windows a write made to fail by another handle's lock releasing the file, every name existing under a lowered limit (nothing written, the typed error, every file untouched, the next name taken once the limit allows, and on a count rotation the status showing no file open), six writers released together by a barrier over twenty rounds each taking a file of its own with one whole line, a folder created and a blocked one reported with its action and path then written by the same emitter once unblocked, the BOM once and the text valid UTF-16, a bad pattern writing nothing, a stop within a slice plus 300 ms at a minute's interval, the TOML defaults and three misspellings refused; the hourly shape exact, a date split in two runs refused as no shape, a separator inside the date kept
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-025.md
- **Status:** verified


## C-026: The OPC UA server: a configured tag list on a real endpoint, with reactions
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-maquinar", scope: a configurable OPC UA server; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope")
- **Description:** `opc::start` serves an `OpcConfig` (read from TOML with every optional key defaulted and a misspelled key refused) as a real OPC UA server on its own thread and runtime: bound first and advertising the port it got (0 lets the machine choose), anonymous and, when configured, one user by name and password (shown in no debug form; a wrong password or an unknown user refused), its tags under one folder in a namespace of its own (never the application uri's), each an `int`, `float`, `bool` or `text` with its browse name, its access (read, write-only, read-write), its initial value parsed per type (empty is the type's zero; nonsense refused by name before anything binds), and optionally a draw within a range on an interval (each due an interval after the last was due, never after the tick that served it, so the tick adds nothing to an interval of a tick or more; at most one draw comes per tick, so an interval below the 20 ms tick is refused by name, and the host's timer held at a millisecond while the server runs (on Windows the process opted out of power throttling's timer coarsening, then `timeBeginPeriod(1)`, paired with its release when the guard drops), so the tick is 20 ms on every platform; a float to its decimals; an int one of the whole numbers from the ceiling of `low` to the floor of `high`, a range holding none or reaching outside an int refused; a bool a flip; text has none; `low` above `high` refused for any type), or instead a ramp for plant motion (an int or a float stepping by its step on an interval scheduled as a draw is (due an interval after the last was due, a stall skipping what it missed), from what the tag holds (a client's write, a plant-side set, a reaction's step), wrapped by the range's width while past its high, with places counted in units of its last place so that it reaches its high exactly and never steps back, a value outside the range starting again at its low; its range (finite, its width too), step (at most the width, counted in units of its places), places (named by a float ramp, none for an int's; the range and the step whole numbers of them at any magnitude, at most 2^51 units so that they count exactly), initial value and interval refused by name when they do not fit, a tag never both drawn and ramped, and a ramp's step never a change to a reaction watching the tag); a client's write of the tag's wire type lands in the address space where the server side reads it (`get`, `snapshot`, write-only tags included) and a read-only or write-only access is honoured; a `set` from the plant side is what a client reads next; the stack's own validation would take a scalar of another wire type into any tag, so the bench checks the type itself on every writable tag and answers `BadTypeMismatch`, as a real controller does (a write with an index range `BadIndexRangeInvalid`), storing nothing, counting it and naming the tag and the variant in its status, a plant-side `set` of the wrong type counted and dropped alike; a right write followed at once by a wrong one keeps the right one; a reaction fires when a watched tag becomes its `equals` value, or changes at all without one, a change being what a client wrote or the plant side set between two ticks (a float holding NaN no change from NaN, so a NaN is one change however long it stays) (a tick every 20 ms as asked of the runtime's timer, which the bench holds at a millisecond, so 20 ms on both platforms (`docs/questions.md` Q13), whatever its body took, the body reading the watched tags and applying what is due; the server's own draws and the steps of any reaction, its own or another's, are never a change, so a chain of reactions and a drawn watched tag fire nothing), and sets a target tag to each step's value at its delay (steps falling due in one tick land in the order of their delays); the stack's read cap and session cap are knobs; a port already taken fails with its address, never hangs; stopping or dropping the handle closes every connection and frees the port for the next start; a relay (core's) puts a freezable link in front of a server on `127.0.0.1`; a duplicate or empty id, a bad value, a draw on text or with an empty interval or range, a reaction on an unknown tag, with a bad trigger or step value or no steps, an empty bind or user name, or a zero cap is refused with its place named. The status counts draws and reactions fired.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/opc_server.rs | driven by the same client library portal uses, raw and through core's own poll worker: every type read with its initial value (zero for empty text and int), the names given and defaulted, the namespace index asked, an unknown node refused, the snapshot in order and rendered, a plant-side set read next; draws of a float, an int and a bool moving inside their range at their decimals while a still tag stays, counted, an int over 3.5..=5.5 taking 4 and 5 only and one at the bottom of an int taking its two values, a 100 ms tag drawing 19 to 21 times in 2 s on either platform and a 20 ms tag on every tick (at least 70 and at most 102 in 2 s on both platforms, a bound against the quantum and not a pace: a 31 ms tick gives 64, a loaded host's late wake-ups gave 81 on a shared CI runner, two draws a tick would give 103 or more; the bench holding the host's timer at a millisecond while it runs), an interval of 19 ms refused by name and 20 accepted; a ramp's steps exact through its high and past it, a step of the whole width, a value outside its range, ten rounded tenths making one, 0..=0.3 by 0.1 at one place reaching 0.3, a value between places counted from its nearest, every high and step at one place from 0.1 to 10 (from 0 and from -1) against the same ramp counted in whole tenths, ten unit steps at fifteen places up to 2.25 each the next whole count, every high and step at two places from 0.01 to 0.6 (from 0 and from -0.6) against the same ramp counted in whole hundredths, an int ramp crossing zero and at the edges of an int (2147483637..=2147483647 by 7, the whole of an int by i32::MAX, a range at its lowest) stepping exactly; the next due time an interval after the last was due, a stall skipping what it missed, and on a server a stall of the service loop (`OpcHandle::stall`, held for its whole length) begun just after a step of a 500 ms ramp followed by the one step it owed at once and the next on the old phase some 250 ms after (not 500), the draws' missed ones not caught up; the restart at the low at places too; an int ramp without places stepping 60 times exactly through its wraps; every range from -0.6 to 0.6 at one and two places stepping by its whole width accepted and by a unit more refused, and steps of the whole width coming back to the high where the floats fall short (0.1..=0.3 by 0.2); a float ramp read over the wire each the ramp's next of the one before, inside its range, and an int ramp's and a float ramp's every step read server-side exactly the next of the one before (the float's wrapping), a still tag still; a client's write, a plant-side set and a reaction's step (to 0, off the ramp's own cycle) each moved on from by exactly one step, one outside the range restarting at exactly the low; a reaction watching a ramped tag never firing on its steps, nor one waiting on the ramp's high though the ramp reaches it, both firing on a plant-side set (to the high, for the second); an int and a float 100 ms ramp stepping 38 to 42 times in 2 s beside a 100 ms draw drawing 19 to 21 (each counted apart) and a 20 ms ramp 70 to 102, as draws are; a ramp with a draw, on a bool or text, with a range (an infinite low, an infinite width), step, places (a step, a low and a high off them, at small and large magnitudes, a high and a low past 2^51 units at fifteen places (2.26, -2.26, -9) where 2.25 and -2.25 are accepted, and a step past them between a low and a high inside (-2.25..=2.25 by 4.5), a step past the width in units (0.1..=0.3 by 0.3), a float ramp naming no places (0..=10, 1e16..=1e16+2)), an int range outside an int, initial value or interval that does not fit, each refused with its text; the ramp read from TOML with its defaults, a misspelled or missing key refused; a read-write tag written and read back server-side, a read-only tag refusing and keeping its value, a write-only tag refusing a read and showing its write; the handshake reaction (100 answered 99 within 200 ms and 1 after 300 to 700 ms, a 5 ignored, fired again on the next 100), a change-triggered reaction into another tag (the same value again not a change, a plant-side set a change), a drawn watched tag firing nothing, nor a watcher waiting on a drawn bool's true, and a chain of two reactions stopping at the first, its link landing 100 ms after its firing, a watcher waiting on the first's value too (the second and that watcher firing on a plant-side set); a watcher waiting on the handshake's delayed 1 never firing; steps listed against their delays landing in the order of their delays (15 ms then 5 ms within a tick, 300 ms then 200 ms over a stall, the later delay's value staying); a client's NaN and a plant-side NaN into a watched float each one firing over a second, a NaN into a ramped tag one before its restart at the low; an Int32 into a float, a String into an int, a Double into a bool, an Int32 into a text, an Empty into an int, a Float into a write-only int and a Float into a float each refused `BadTypeMismatch` with the server side unchanged at once (`get` and the snapshot, plainly, no waiting), counted with the tag and the variant named, a Double then an Int32 keeping the Double, the watched float's `equals` not fired by the stray Int32 and fired by a Double, a plant-side set of the wrong type counted and dropped, a ranged write refused, a text as text landing; core's worker Connected then five polls in node order with the draws reaching it, a gated write landing, the bench stopped noticed as Retrying; the configured user reading, anonymous still accepted, a wrong password and an unknown user refused under 10 s, the password in no debug form; four nodes refused over a cap of three with the stack's text and three read at it, a second session refused over a cap of one; the relay's url, a read through it, a frozen link answering nothing within 1 s, thawed and recovered, a server on another host refused a relay by name; a taken port's typed error with its address under 5 s; stop and drop each freeing the port; every value parse and refusal with its text; every configuration refusal with its exact message and the port left free; ## C-027: Message bodies written from named fields by rules
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Adding a component", 2: generalisation first; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Rules": what portal would also need lands in core; docs/questions.md Q8: encoding by rules)
- **Description:** A `ComposeSpec`, configuration rather than code (read from TOML, optional keys taking their defaults, a misspelled key or type refused), writes named values into a message body, the inverse of `CaptureSpec` (C-013) and sharing its `BinaryField` and `FieldType`: a text template with `{name}` placeholders (the name read trimmed; `{{` and `}}` literal braces; an unclosed brace, a stray `}`, an empty placeholder or a `{` inside one refused where the spec compiles, by character), then binary fields at byte offsets in either order (an integer from decimal text, a sign only where its type takes one, refused when it does not parse or fit; text padded with NUL to its length, refused when longer or holding a NUL; hex parsed and held to its length), the body grown with the pad byte to reach a field, padded to `len` and refused beyond it; a text or hex field without a length, two fields sharing a byte, a `len` over 16 MiB, and a template whose literal text or a field that cannot fit `len`, else the 16 MiB ceiling, are refused where the spec compiles (an offset that would wrap included), so every message of a spec that loads can be written with values that fit; a value missing for any placeholder or field is a refusal; a field written over a placeholder's value is refused when rendered (the value would not read back), over literal text it overwrites it; no body grows past 16 MiB, what the values bring past it refused before any allocation. `names` lists what a body needs, once each, in order. - **Date:** 2026-11-06

- **Description:** A channel's messages are configured by a TOML file stating `format = "veredas-catalog"` and `version = 1` (`docs/catalog-schema.md`): the frame field that carries a message's id (`id_field`), the byte order of bodies, and each message's name, id, fixed length and pad byte, its binary fields (one list, written by compose and read by capture), its text template, its payload pattern and its repeats. `catalog::load` refuses another format, an unknown or missing version, a misspelled key in any table, an empty `id_field`, a name (of a message, a field, a repeat) that is empty, repeated or not exact (names are compared as written, no surrounding whitespace), a repeated id, a name any two readers would write (a field named as a payload group or a repeat's numbered name, a repeat's numbered name that is a payload group's or another repeat's; decided by parsing the name, never by listing a repeat's names, so `max` may be in the millions), a repeat with `max` 0, a field ending past the body's length or past 16 MiB or beyond any body, and everything compose refuses (a text or hex field without a length, an overlap, a length over 16 MiB, a template or a field that cannot fit the length), a bad pattern and a bad template, naming the place; it never guesses. A loaded catalog finds a message by name or id, answers whether a message reads a name (a binary field, a payload group, or a repeat's numbered name, by the same parsing rule) so a file read against it (C-030) never re-derives its names, and, where it meets a channel file, checks that its ids can ride the framing before a channel that speaks it (C-029; portal's, P-002) binds: the `id_field` a field of the segment header (of the envelope in a layout without segments) that the framing leaves to the sender (an opaque field, never the sequence, a length or a count) and wide enough for every id, and no message whose body is always empty (a length of 0, or no rules at all) in a layout without segments, where the wire carries one as a keep-alive, each refused naming the field, its role or the message; it puts an id where that framing takes it and reads the ids a frame carries, per segment or from the envelope; writes a body from values (`encode`: the id and the body in the catalog's byte order, a missing value refused; a message with a payload or a repeat and nothing to write refused rather than written empty) and reads one (`decode`: the message and its fields, nothing for an unknown id, a body the rules refuse, or a body of another length than a fixed one; a body padded to a length is read with its padding). - **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/telegram_catalog.rs | the documentation's own example block loaded and checked message by message (names, ids, the announcement's five fields by name, offset, length and type, one field list both ways), the prose's numbers (the body lengths, the code's length, the byte order) read from the text and held against the loaded example, its ids tied to the channel schema's example's `id` field, an announcement written and read back, the answer's golden bytes read from the prose and matched by `encode` and `decode` and its three fields by name, offset, length and type, the outcome's text read from the prose and matched both ways and its pattern anchored at both ends with digits required, an unknown name and id, a short and a long fixed body, an unanchored text refused, a missing value through the catalog, the big-endian answer;
