## C-029: The bench channel: one link in either role, speaking a catalog by name
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-maquinar", scope: TCP telegram channels; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope": telegram channels, "Rules": owns its sockets, speaks the real device's wire)
- **Description:** `channel::start` runs one TCP telegram link on a thread of its own, in either role (listening on a bound address, or dialling and retrying), framed and held by core's channel file (C-017) and core's channel (C-012), so a consumer cannot tell the bench from the device, and speaking the messages of a catalog (C-028) by name: the catalog's `id_field` (C-028) must ride the channel file's framing, as core's catalog checks it (`Catalog::check_framing`: a field of the segment header, of the envelope without segments, that the channel does not fill in itself and wide enough for every id; no message writing an empty body without segments, a keep-alive on the wire), else the channel refuses to start with the catalog's reason; a body that renders empty in a layout without segments is a note, never a frame; its own cyclic messages go out every `every_ms` to every peer, each due an interval after the last was due, their fields rendered in order from the sources a plain channel has (a constant, a range, a counter, a cycle, a template of the earlier fields, the clock; `from` and `register` refused at start, as are a message the catalog lacks and a name the message writes that no field renders, each by place); `every_ms` 0 is on demand only, and `send_now` builds any message of the catalog from given values, to one peer or to everyone; what cannot be built (a message the catalog lacks, a value missing or not fitting, a frame core's framing refuses: a body over the frame limit) is a monitor note and the status's last error, never a frame and never counted as sent; each message a received frame carries is decoded by id and body: decoded, it is counted by name and shown with its fields, its body's preview and its peer; an id the catalog lacks is counted as unknown and shown by id; a body the rules refuse is counted as undecodable and shown by the message's name; the status mirrors core's wire status, the connected peers, the newest 100 monitor lines (messages in and out, connections and disconnections, refusals, the controller's notes) and the counts sent and received by name; a port already taken or a peer never there shows in the wire's state and the last error, the channel staying until stopped; stop and drop end the link and free the port; a controller's knob on a channel without one is refused by name, and a knob is answered only once the status shows its effect. The controller that may ride a channel is C-031.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/channels.rs | a listener and a dialer over loopback with the channel schema's worked file and the controller schema's worked catalog: the listener listening, then one peer each side with core's state words and no error, the connection noted with the peer's id and address; a status line every 100 ms decoded by name on the other side at least five times, its counter counting, its constant and its peer shown, its body previewed as text, the time of day with milliseconds, sent to everyone, core's own counters beside (no message the other way, at most the keep-alive on connect); a message at `every_ms` 0 never sent on its own, `send_now` of a command to everyone and of a heartbeat to one peer, each decoded with its fields and previewed as hex, the sender's monitor showing the peer it went to; an unknown message, a missing value, a value that does not fit and a body the segment's two-byte length field cannot declare each a note and the last error, read one by one, and no frame, the long body not counted (the count of status lines sent equal to the monitor's lines out in one snapshot, no line here or at the peer carrying the long value); the monitor at 130 lines keeping the newest 100, newest first; stop seen by the other side as a disconnection and the port free, the dialer still running; a layout without segments (the worked file with the id in its envelope and no segment header): a command and a status line decoded by name each way with nothing unknown or undecodable, a body that renders empty a note and the last error with nothing counted and no keep-alive at the peer while the same message with a value is sent and read by name, an `id_field` not in the envelope and one that is the envelope's sequence field refused by name and role; a sender whose catalog holds an id the receiver's lacks and defines the command two bytes longer: the receiver counting one unknown id and one undecodable body and showing each by id or by name with its bytes, core's counter seeing all three frames, the decodable one counted by name; refused at start: an `id_field` not in the framing, one that is the segment header's length field, and a catalog with an id of 300 over a one-byte id field (the same field with the worked catalog accepted), a cyclic message the catalog lacks, a name no field writes, `from` and `register` in a plain channel, each with its place; a taken port's state `failed` and the error naming the port, the channel alive until stopped; a dialer with nobody to dial `retrying` with no peers; a knob on a channel without a controller refused
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-029.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-030: The controller file: a controller's behaviour as versioned rules against a catalog
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Versioned files"; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope": a simulated controller as a rules file; docs/questions.md Q8, Q10)
- **Description:** What the bench's controller does on its wire is a TOML file stating `format = "veredas-controller"` and `version = 1` (`docs/controller-schema.md`), read by `controller::load` against the catalog its messages come from (C-028): a seed; registers (named integers); destinations with named one-bit state bits, a resting word, initial words and blocking bits in precedence order, each with the outcome fields it forces and whether the item comes round again; the items announced (the message, the interval, the key field, a gate register, the lap, the fields, named faults with a share and the fields each sets, the reply awaited with its key and destination fields and dispositions by a field's value, the outcome with its travel time, fields and weighted mix); answers (on a message, send another after a delay, fields from the incoming message); commands (a message's field value setting registers); cyclic messages (every interval, per destination or not, fields from the registers); and every field's one source (a constant, a range to decimals, a counter, a cycle, a template, a context field, a register, the clock, or a decision by first-match rules, a weighted spread, a reject value and a sticky key). The reader refuses, naming the place, another format or version, a misspelled key in any of its eighteen tables and every malformed rule the reference's closing paragraph lists (a field with no source or two, an empty or non-finite range, a counter ending before it starts, an empty cycle, a bad template, a source its place does not take, a register that does not exist, a decision with a bad pattern or nothing to decide with, a message name the catalog lacks, a name the message writes that no field renders, an interval or lap of 0, a key not announced, a fault over 100 or setting a field not announced, a reply key or destination the reply does not read (asked of the catalog: a binary field, a payload group, a repeat's numbered name), an outcome without a reply or without the reply's destination field when there are destinations, a weight not finite or negative, a command with nothing to set or a register that does not exist, a destination list empty or repeating, a bit that is not one bit or named twice, an unnamed bit, an initial destination not in the list, a bit blocking twice); it never guesses. The worked example (a catalog of six messages, the machine's rules, the unit's rules) loads as its prose describes; every key statement of the reference is the reader's, and the test quotes each statement about loading beside its pin (C-031's quotes those about running) and holds the reference's text (the Verification). A field a disposition, a rule, a command or a `sticky` key names is looked up when the message comes, checked at load only for being empty; a forced field (a blocking bit's, a mixed outcome's) must be one the outcome renders; an `initial` key is the destination's number exactly as it prints; a range at 0 decimals must hold a whole number.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/controller_rules.rs | the three blocks of the documentation loaded (the catalog's six ids, three read from the prose), the machine's rules checked against the prose's numbers (the interval, one item in ten, the lap, the travel time, the mix's weights, the heartbeat's and the status line's intervals, the dispositions' flags, destination 4 full) and rule by rule (registers, destinations, bits, blocking in order with precedence pinned on a word with both bits, the items' fields, key and gate, the fault's fields, the reply's key and destination field, the outcome's fields, the commands' settings, the cyclic messages, what it sends and receives), the unit's likewise, its decision read back from the loaded rules (the rule, the spread's three shares, the reject 9, the sticky key) and a field with another source deciding nothing; the reference's eighteen tables' key lists equal to the reader's own (from its refusal of a misspelled key placed in each table through `load`), each key left out of a full file refused where the reference says required and loading with exactly the stated default otherwise (the top level's tables as sections, a register table nothing reads, a reply's destination required with an outcome by destination and not without, bits nothing names), each one-source key loading alone and refused with none or with another, the actions, the empty pattern and the sticky ceiling the reference names being the reader's; the header refused four ways with the body judged after it, the emptiest file loading; every refusal of the closing paragraph with its exact message (fifty cases, `from` and `register` in a plain channel through `Fields::compile`), `decide` refused in an outcome and in a cyclic message and `from` of the destination or the state in a cyclic message that is not per destination, the bounds at their included ends loading (a range of one value, fifteen decimals, a counter of one value, a fault at 100, a spread and a mix weight of 0), a field's decimals reaching its draw, a padded name refused in every place a file names one (a register, a bit, an `initial` key, a field, a fault, an outcome), a reply keyed by a repeat's numbered name accepted and by the repeat's own name or one past its max refused, a text reply keyed by a payload group accepted and keyed or sent to another name refused, the excluded ends next to the included ones (a range of 2 to 1, a counter from 3 to 2) refused, a range of 1.5 to 1.7 refused at 0 decimals and loading at 1, an `initial` key `01` or `+1` refused by text and one key twice refused by TOML, an empty disposition, rule, command or sticky field refused by place and one the message lacks loading, a forced field no outcome renders refused for a blocking bit and a mixed outcome, the full file's decision read back with a rule's value apart from its reject; ## C-031: The simulated controller: pure and tick-driven, then the whole conversation over a socket
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-maquinar", scope: a full simulated controller; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope": a simulated controller, generalised from Veredas 1's mscsim and trajetorias' automated replies, no vendor named)
- **Description:** `Controller::new(rules, seed)` runs a controller file (C-030) as a pure engine: `tick(elapsed, peers)` advances its clock and returns what is due, `on_message(name, fields, peer)` takes a decoded message and returns what it sends now or later, with a note for the monitor on everything it decides; no socket, no sleep, no wall clock but the one a `now` field asks for, and a seed (the bench's, else the file's) repeating a run exactly, its draws on a stream per purpose (the items, the outcomes, the cyclic messages, the answers), so an item's fields depend on its place alone, whatever a tick carried between two items. It announces an item every interval while a peer is connected and the gate register is not 0, each due an interval after the last was due whatever the tick (a pass announces every item due since the last pass, at most twenty, a longer stall skipping the rest and keeping the phase; when announcing resumes past a due time the first item comes at once), its fields rendered in order (a counter counting, a cycle cycling, a template reading the earlier fields, a range drawn), each fault setting its fields on its share of items, drawn item by item; it awaits the reply keyed by the item's key (trimmed), takes the reply's fields into the item (trimmed), counts a reply for no item and a destination it does not have (the reply's destination field naming none of its destinations, or no number; a reply with no destination field names none; the item still travels), and acts on the first disposition whose field equals: requeue (round again after the lap, its laps counted, its fields kept, held like a new item while no peer is connected or the gate is closed), park (ended, asked about no more), else deliver (travel for the outcome's time, then the outcome to the peer that answered, its fields from the item and the reply, its result forced by the first blocking entry whose bit is set in the destination's word, with the fields it sets and its requeue, else drawn from the mix by weight, else delivered with nothing forced; without an outcome the item ends delivered at once); it answers a message with the answer's fields rendered from the message, to the peer it came from, at once or after the delay, a field that cannot render (no decision, a name not there) refusing the answer, counted; a decision remembers its key while the value is not blocked, takes the first matching rule, draws over the spread avoiding what is blocked, else the reject value, which is never remembered; it obeys a command whose field value matches a setting, setting its registers, and notes one that matches nothing; it sends each cyclic message every interval, peers or none, one per destination with the destination and its word when asked; its status holds the registers, every destination's word, the items awaiting and the age of the oldest, the newest 200 ended items with how each ended, the counters (announced, answered, requeued, parked, unknown answers, invalid destinations, ended by how, answers sent and refused, commands, cyclic sent, faults by name), the interval and each fault's share; the knobs set the interval (0 refused; the next item within the new interval), a fault's share (over 100 or unknown refused), a destination's word, one bit of one or every destination, a register, and announce an item now, each refused by name on what the rules do not have. Riding a bench channel (C-029), the whole conversation runs over a real socket: the machine of the worked example on one end, the unit on the other, the knobs turned live.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/controller.rs | the worked example's machine driven in 10 ms ticks with no sleep: nothing announced without a peer while the heartbeats go out; the first item at once then ten more in a second, their fields exact (the counter, the cycle, the flag, the template, the weight within its range), the status's counts and the oldest unanswered age; the answer for item 1 from peer 5 with a padded key, its note with the latency, the outcome 500 ms later to peer 5 with the item's and the reply's fields and a result from the mix, the history entry with its destination, peer, laps, latency and how it ended; a reply for no item and a destination not in the list each counted and noted, a reply with no destination field counted as nothing with the item travelling to `-`; a message nobody reacts to; the mix drawn by weight (a thousand deliveries 850 to 950 sorted and 50 to 150 lost, the ended counts agreeing; a weight of 0 in front never drawn and the shares 3 to 1 holding over four hundred); requeue bringing the item round after 2000 ms as lap 2 with its fields kept (held while no peer is there, announced the moment one is), park ending it for good, the first matching disposition deciding, none delivering, an item without an outcome ending delivered at once; destination 4 full forcing FULL over the rendered field and requeueing, the bit cleared drawing from the mix, a fault forcing FAULT without a requeue, both bits set deciding by precedence, every chute full and back, every knob's refusal by name, a mix entry that requeues and a mix with no weight ending delivered; ten ticks of 100 ms and one tick of 1000 ms rendering the same eleven weights under one seed, the first item's outcome landing between items in the first and after them in the second; a command stopping the announcements while the heartbeats go on per destination with each word, starting them again at once, setting the speed the status line reads at 5000 ms, one matching nothing and one without the field, the register knob, a cyclic message every 250 ms peers or none with a counter wrapping after its last, the clock, a cycle and a template as sources; the unit answering to the peer at once with the item copied and a destination from the spread, the same code deciding the same twenty times, an empty or blank code taking its rule's 0, the shares over four hundred codes (1 about twice 2 and 3), destination 1 marked full never drawn and a remembered 1 decided afresh, every chute full giving the reject 9 to a read code and a remembered one while an empty code keeps its rule, the chutes back drawing again for a new code and for the one rejected while full, the code remembered before the full period keeping its chute, an answer refused for want of a decision or a name, counted, a delayed answer going out after its delay; the fault's share (about one in ten of a thousand, every fault field set), turned to all and to none, the rate of 100 ms items over 10 s on ticks of 10, 30, 70 and 250 ms alike and of 20 ms items on a 31 ms tick, a stall of ten intervals announcing the ten and one of fifty announcing twenty, the phase kept, one item at once after an idle with no peer, the interval turned live, announce now, two seeds repeating and a third differing, the history capped at 200; then over a real loopback socket, the machine listening and the unit dialling with the channel schema's worked file: items announced, answered within 500 ms, outcomes reported back and read by the unit with their text, heartbeats per destination, the counts of both ends agreeing to within what is in flight, the monitors showing each message's fields, every chute full forcing FULL outcomes and requeues seen by the unit then every chute back, the register stopping the announcements and a command sent by the unit starting them again, noted, the no-read fault turned to all with the unit rejecting to 0, the knobs refused by name and all refused on a channel without a controller, the machine stopped and the unit seeing it go, the port free; the reference's sentences about running quoted beside their pins (C-030's copy holds the text)
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-031.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-032: Controllable pressure: the rate, the faults, every chute full and the no-reads, live on the wire
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-maquinar", scope: controllable pressure; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope": controllable pressure)
- **Description:** The pressure a bench puts on its consumer is turned from the bench channel's handle while the conversation runs, and holds on the wire: the announcement rate is the interval's whatever the host's tick (every item due since the last pass announced, each due an interval after the last was due, so a tick longer than the interval does not cap the rate), changed live by `set_interval` and forced by `announce_now` (one item, no burst); a named fault's share of items is `set_fault`'s, drawn item by item, each faulted item carrying the fault's fields (a no-read's flag and empty code) so the other side treats it as its rules say (the unit rejecting it to destination 0, which the machine counts as an invalid destination); `set_bit_everywhere` marks every chute full, so every outcome at a chute is forced FULL and every item comes round again after the lap, and clears them, so the outcomes are drawn again (an item answered while full may still end FULL once); a peer gone stops the announcements, and the oldest unanswered item ages in the status; a register is set as a command would set it.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/pressure.rs | the worked example's machine and unit over loopback, measured on the unit's count of announcements received: 17 to 23 in 2 s at 100 ms, 34 to 46 at 50 ms after `set_interval`, 85 to 115 at 20 ms (below the Windows tick), 4 to 6 at 400 ms, exactly one more on `announce_now` at an interval of a minute and none after; the no-read share at 50 % over a thousand items at 10 ms (44 to 56 by the machine's own count, 35 to 65 among at least 200 announcements the unit showed while the fault was on, gathered by item as its last hundred lines slid, each with its flag and empty code), the unit rejecting to 0 and, once every item is answered, the machine's invalid destinations risen by exactly the no-reads; every chute full: every word's bit set, five more FULL outcomes and requeues within 5 s and the five newest outcomes at a chute the unit shows all FULL, then every chute back with the words cleared, the newest outcomes at a chute drawn again and at most two more FULL from items already answered, an item on its second lap; the unit stopped: the machine's peers gone, nothing more announced over 600 ms and the oldest unanswered item 500 ms older; the register knob
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-032.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-033: The bench file and the executable: one file for the whole plant, run from the command line
- **Date:** 2026-11-06
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-maquinar": a library with an executable on top; docs/maquinar-plan.md "Scope": the bench file, "Rules": a library first, one executable on top; docs/questions.md Q12)
- **Description:** One TOML file stating `format = "veredas-bench"` and `version = 1` (`docs/bench-schema.md`) lists the plant's parts by name: log emitters (C-025), OPC UA servers (C-026), Modbus servers (C-110), EtherNet/IP adapters (C-111), PROFINET devices (C-112), EtherCAT segments (C-113), scenes (C-114, a scene file beside the bench file), their models (C-115) and telegram channels (C-029), each channel pointing at the device's own files beside it (core's channel file, the catalog, the controller rules file). `bench::load` reads it and every referenced file and checks each part as its own module checks it, refusing by place another format or version, a misspelled key in any of its thirty-one tables (the top level among them), a part without a name, with another part's name or with a dot or a control character in it (a model names a point `<part>.<point>`; a name is one line of the plan and the status), a bad file name or timestamp format, a value range empty or not finite or over 15 decimals, what the OPC UA or the Modbus server refuses, a model whose scene, belt, sensor, mover or `<part>.<point>` is not there or whose point is of the other kind or written by two models, a referenced file that cannot be read or does not load, an id field not riding in the framing, a rules file not loading against the catalog, a message the catalog lacks or whose fields leave a name unwritten or use a source a plain channel lacks, before anything binds, opens or spawns; `Bench::start` runs every part behind one handle, emitters first, then servers (OPC UA, then Modbus, then EtherNet/IP), then PROFINET devices, then EtherCAT segments, then scenes, then their models, then channels, each kind in the file's order (TOML keeps no order across kinds; an emitter without a seed drawing from the bench's seed plus its index, each channel and its controller from the bench's seed (absent, a channel from the clock and a controller from its rules file's seed, else the clock), an OPC UA server and a PROFINET device without a seed of its own given none (each takes the clock), the output folder created), a part that cannot start (a listening channel whose port is taken among them) stopping the ones before it, every one of them joined, and named, with every part's status by name in that order, one line per part, a channel or a server by name for the knobs, and stop ending every part. The executable `veredas-maquinar <bench.toml>` runs a bench file until stdin closes or says `stop` (any other line, UTF-8 or not, read past), or on Unix a SIGTERM or a SIGINT comes (its handlers installed before the bench file is read), printing every part's line at the start (a server's with the address it got) and once a second, exit 0; `--check` reads, checks and prints the plan without starting anything; `--version` prints `veredas-maquinar <version>` and exits 0, nothing read; a refused file or a part that cannot start is exit 2 with the reason on stderr; a usage error (no file, a second file, an unknown option: any argument beginning with a dash but `-` alone) exit 1 with the usage. The worked example loads, starts with every part doing its work and runs under the executable; every key statement of the reference is the reader's, and the test quotes each statement beside its pin and holds the reference's text (the Verification). A desktop UI was Q12; the architect decided (2026-11-07) that the library and this executable are the bench.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/bench_file.rs | the worked example's folder written from the references (the channel file, the catalog, the two rules files, the bench file with a free port): loaded with the prose's numbers (the line interval, the rotation, the draw's range and interval, the step's delay, the port the reference names) and its four-line plan exact; started: the emitter's lines in a daily file under `logs` with codes and weights in their ranges and no error, the server advertising a chosen port, drawing twice within 5 s, a speed within its range read back and a reaction fired by a set, the machine with its peer answering three items and the unit's own status line received, one status line per part in the file's order with their words, unknown names none, stopped with the port free and the file whole; the reference's thirty-one tables' key lists equal to the reader's own (the scene, its models, a drive, a conveyor, a sensor model, an actuator and a pose among them, a scene's file and its models reaching the plan) (from a misspelled key placed in each through `load`), each key left out refused where required (the header's two with their own words) and loading with the stated default otherwise (the top level's parts as sections, a server's tags with its reactions), the type, access, encoding and role words the reader's own variant lists; the header refused four ways with the body judged after it, the emptiest bench loading and running with no part; every refusal of the closing paragraph with its exact message (a nameless and a padded name, a name taken across kinds, an empty and an unwritable file pattern, a bad timestamp format, a nameless value, an empty and an infinite range, decimals over 15, the server's own refusal and its empty bind each with the part named, an empty host, a channel file missing or of another format with its path, a catalog whose id field does not ride, a rules file naming a message the catalog lacks with its path, a message the catalog lacks, a name unwritten, `from` in a plain channel); every key of a Modbus server reaching its configuration with a value of its own; a Modbus server by name, none for a name no part has, each one's address its own line's; a Modbus server's refusal named with its part; OPC UA servers started before Modbus servers whatever the file's order (both on taken ports, the OPC UA one named) and a Modbus server started before the channels (a channel after it that fails never dialling); `--version` its name and version, a file named beside it unread; a SIGTERM and a SIGINT each ending a run whose stdin stays open with `stopped`, exit 0 and the port free, and each one sent before a byte of the bench file (a pipe) was read ending the run the same way; the executable's output with `stop` already in its stdin exactly the header, the plan, one status line per part with the port each server got (not 0), then `stopped`, and a client reading a point through the port the start's line printed; a server on a taken port refused at start by name with the emitter started before it stopped and joined before the error came back (its file's state, a first line or none yet when the bind failed before the thread's first tick as a loaded runner showed, unchanged over two and a half of its intervals), a server started before a failing one with its port free the moment the error is back (joined, not told), a channel listed after a failing server never dialling the socket it would have dialled, a folder that cannot be created refused by place; a file listing a server above an emitter above a channel started and shown as emitter, server, channel; the same bench twice with two emitters without seeds writing the same lines run for run and different lines from each other, the machine's first two items, read once both stand in the status (announced, and no longer travelling), drawn alike for one bench seed and differently for another, a channel's own drawn field, four lines kept by a peer, alike for one bench seed and differently for another; the executable: `--check` printing the four-part plan with nothing bound or written, a refused file exit 2 with the place on stderr, a missing file exit 2, no file and an unknown option exit 1 with the usage, each on stderr, a run printing the parts' lines with the emitter writing, the machine announcing and the unit connected, stopped by `stop` on stdin with exit 0 and `stopped` last, the port free and the log file there, and a second run stopped by stdin closing alike; the reference's text equal, paragraph by paragraph, to the copy the test holds; round 3's pins: a channel taking an emitter's name, a nameless channel, a scene and a models part taking a Modbus server's, two scenes of one name and a name with a dot refused by their text; an adapter on a taken port named before a device on no interface, a device before a segment, a channel after a failing adapter never dialling; an emitter writing before a failing server in some of 20 runs; the plan of every kind in the start order, the emitter's line whole; scenes and models shown after a Modbus server and before a channel, and in a private network an adapter, a PROFINET device, an EtherCAT segment and a scene shown in that order; a catalog missing, a catalog that does not load and a controller missing named by place and path; a listening channel on a taken port refused at start with the server before it freed, and the executable exit 2 naming it; a server on a taken port exit 2 naming it; two files exit 1 with the usage; a Modbus server before a failing adapter joined, its port free; the status lines a second apart (four gaps each under 1.9 s, their mean 0.8 to 1.4 s, round 4); round 4's pins: a NUL in an emitter's and a channel's name, a newline and a tab in a server's refused by their text at load, by `--check` and by a run, exit 2 with nothing printed; a line on stdin that is not UTF-8 read past, the run ended by `stop` after it with exit 0; `-h`, `-V`, `-c` and `--help` each exit 1 with the usage; two emitters without seeds under bench seed 7 writing what emitters of seeds 7 and 8 write at their places, an emitter's own seed writing the same under bench seeds 7, 8 and none, two runs without a seed different, a fixed seed the same at one place whatever is before it and other at another; the worked example's machine drawing alike with no bench seed and with 7 (its rules file's seed 7), otherwise with 8, and differently run for run with no seed in either

## C-034: veredas-portal — the gateway
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal: the gateway"; decisions of 2026-11-05 on Q8; promoted from P-002)
- **Description:** `crates/veredas-portal` is the one point where the suite exchanges data with the outside world (the plan's scope; `docs/portal-plan.md` maps what it absorbs from Veredas 1's `veredas-core` (the data plane), `veredas-store`, `veredas-server` and `veredas-desktop`, what it leaves there, and the phases: the data plane, telegram channels by rules, OPC sources, the session store, the server, the project file with P-010, the shell as `docs/questions.md` Q14). Content-agnostic like core and the bench (no file of the crate names the vendor protocol core's telegram layer came from or cites a vendor document; Q8), and a library first: cargo, asked what it would build, names exactly one library target (crate types `lib`/`rlib`), at most one binary named `veredas-portal`, and tests, nothing else; its light build (no default features: the data plane, the channels, the OPC sources and the project file, nothing stored and nothing served; no consumer named today, controlador embedding the full build, P-019) carries in its normal and build dependency graph no database (`duckdb`, `libduckdb`), no server (`axum`, `hyper`), no desktop UI crate (`eframe`, `egui`, `winit`) and no member of the suite but core (its tests dev-depend on the bench, which plays the plant: a dev-dependency is a fixture, left out of the walk). It depends on core alone (L5), its plan came before its code (L6), and it versions on its own from 2.0.0 (L3; it started at 2.0.0-alpha.1). Each capability is a clause of its own below.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/component.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | `cargo metadata --no-deps` on the gateway, and on planted packages: a plain library, one with a `src/main.rs` and one with a `[[bin]]` of the package's name accepted; a binary of another name, two binaries, `[[example]]`, `examples/`, a `crate-type` naming `bin`, a `cdylib`, an empty `crate-type`, a bench, a build script and a package with a binary but no library each refused; every file of the crate, whatever its extension, the manifest included, read whole, normalised to letters and digits across line breaks, for the vendor's name and protocol terms and for vendor-document and section citations, the reader checked on planted spellings; the dependency graph of the gateway built alone, asked of `cargo tree -p` without the default features on every platform (`cargo metadata` resolves the whole workspace, so supervisão's use of the default features would unify into it), following normal and build edges and never a dev edge (the bench absent from it, core and the stack present), empty of the banned names, the ban list pinned on planted names, and the walk on planted packages (a banned crate as a normal dependency and as a build dependency seen, as a dev dependency not, as both seen, a clean package); the laws on the real tree with the gateway in it (name, version, core alone, plan in its history)

## C-035: The file tail: one growing file followed through truncation and replacement, text decoded whatever its encoding
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal", scope: the data plane, tailing; docs/portal-plan.md "Scope")
- **Description:** A `FileTailSource` follows one file at a path `tail -F` style on its own thread, poll-driven (a filesystem watcher wakes the poll early on a local path; a share is polled alone, a UNC path or a mapped network drive on Windows, while a mount on Linux reads as local until P-013), emitting every completed line as a `LineEvent` tagged with its source, a dense per-source sequence from 0, the arrival clock, and the stamp a `TimestampSpec` reads from the line (none when it does not parse; the line still flows), a line's time index being the parsed stamp read as UTC, else its arrival: CRLF stripped, a bare carriage return kept, an empty line a line, a partial line held until its newline; started at the end it skips what exists and the torn fragment a mid-line end leaves, started at the beginning it reads the file whole; a file absent at attach is read whole when it appears; an in-place truncation (the file seen shorter than what was read; a rewrite back at or past the read offset by the next poll is read as a continuation, the limit of every poll-based tail) flushes the held partial and rereads from the top; a replacement or a deletion drains the old file to a stable end (its torn last line flushed, never lost; one found shorter than what was read, truncated then replaced between two looks, reread from its top to its stable end first) and follows whatever appears at the path from its start; a stop flushes the final partial and the worker ends cleanly; a writer faster than the reader never pins the reader and loses no line; a tail no watcher serves (the folder not there yet, the watcher off or unobtainable) polls at its interval, never spinning, and reads the file when it comes; a start returns once the worker has looked at its path (present, attached where the start says; present but not openable for the moment, a writer holding it or a share's passing error, awaited: when it opens, the same file (its identity, a length not shrunk) is read from where it stood at the look, so what was written to it meanwhile is read and its history stays history, and a file replaced meanwhile is read whole, as is one whose identity the look could not read, denied even its attributes, or one the look could read nothing of, not even its length (its folder denying the listing, a share's passing error), duplicates over loss; absent, awaited and read whole when it appears; within five seconds at most, the watcher armed after the signal so its arming never eats the bound), so a line written after the start is never taken as history when the look completes within the bound. Text is decoded as UTF-8 (a byte-order mark or none), UTF-16 in either order (a mark, or the zero pattern of ASCII-heavy text without one) or CP1252 (the fallback), decided by the first bytes that tell (a mark or the UTF-16 pattern in the first bytes, else the first byte beyond ASCII, so a file that opens with plain ASCII lines streams them and is not committed before its first accented character, however far in; a tail attached at the end reads the file's head to decide, tells a UTF-16 line feed from a torn line, takes a file holding its mark alone as a clean start, and moves an attach that falls inside a UTF-16 code unit back to the unit's boundary, dropping the fragment it skips before the decoder sees it while the head left the encoding undecided, so an attach inside a UTF-8 sequence of an ASCII-headed file never commits CP1252; a head under four bytes that is no complete mark is too little to decide from, and its bytes vote with what follows them as a start from the beginning does, a torn mark being no text yet and a clean start, and what it held being text from before the attach unless it proves a mark, skipped with the torn line it opens; a NUL beside a line feed in the first bytes waits for the UTF-16 pattern rather than decide on the line feed) and committed for the file's lifetime, a multi-byte sequence or code unit split across appends decoded whole, a torn final byte (one that starts a UTF-8 sequence the stream ends before completing) a replacement character, a byte no sequence starts the CP1252 character it is. Reading never blocks the plant's writer: every handle is a plain open with full sharing, never a memory map, so the owner appends, truncates, renames and deletes while the tail reads. A `SourceSet` merges every source's stream into one receiver, tagged and sequenced per source, that disconnects only after every source is stopped; a source is removed alone, a worker's liveness and failure are reported without blocking, a dropped consumer ends a worker cleanly, and a stop reaches a busy worker.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/tail_file.rs | over real files in a scratch folder: appends in order with the sequence, the source, the arrival bracketed by the clock and the index equal to the arrival; CRLF stripped, a bare CR kept, an empty line; a partial held then completed; a start at the end skipping history and the torn fragment, and emitting the next line of a file ending in a newline; truncation flushing the partial and rereading; a replacement draining the torn last line then reading the new file; a deleted file drained and the reappearing one read from its start; a late file read whole; a stop flushing the final partial with the worker's clean result; 20 000 lines from a flat-out writer arriving once each in order; UTF-8 with and without a mark, UTF-16 in both orders with a mark and little-endian without, CP1252, a UTF-8 sequence and a UTF-16 code unit split across appends, a torn final byte as U+FFFD; a timestamp rule stamping two lines and leaving a continuation line unstamped with its sequence and its arrival index; the owner appending three times by open-append-close, truncating, renaming and deleting while a running tail reads, every owner operation succeeding and every line the owner wrote reaching the tail; UNC paths remote and a drive local (on Windows, where those shapes exist), a relative path classified without a panic; the merged stream of two list sources tagged and sequenced, the receiver connected until stop-all and disconnected after, a stop reaching an endless worker and a dropped consumer ending one, remove stopping one of two with the other still producing, the reports of a live, an ended and a failed worker with the failure's exact text kept across remove, and three files tailed at once with 900 lines merged, each tagged by content and dense per source; a UTF-16 file in either order tailed from its end, ending on a line feed (the first new line emitted) and mid-line (the torn line's completion skipped); an empty file then a header then CP1252 decoded as CP1252 (and committed), an ASCII opening then UTF-8 decoded whole, a tail attached at the end of an ASCII file decoding the CP1252 line appended; three tails no watcher serves (a parent folder not there yet, the watcher off, the watcher asked for on a missing folder) costing under 1.5 s of the process's CPU over 3 s and each reading its file when the folder and the files come; a file holding a UTF-16 (either order) or a UTF-8 mark alone, tailed from its end, emitting the two lines then written; a UTF-16 file in either order cut one byte into a code unit, inside a line (the torn line's completion skipped, the next two whole lines first) and at a line's start (that line whole, then the next); an ASCII-opened file ending on a torn UTF-8 lead flushed as U+FFFD at the stop, and on a byte no sequence starts as its CP1252 character; an ASCII head over 4 KiB ending inside a two-byte UTF-8 character, tailed from its end, the continuation byte then two accented lines appended, both decoded as UTF-8; a file holding one byte of a UTF-16 mark in either order, or two of the UTF-8 mark, tailed from its end and the rest appended, the two lines then written whole; one UTF-16 character without a mark, in either order, its line skipped and the next two whole; a CP1252 thorn and a UTF-8 character opening like a mark, held at the attach, never emitted; a UTF-16 file without a mark opening on a bare line feed, UTF-16 from its end and from its beginning alike; a short head the stop reaches undecided emitting nothing; thirty tails started at the end of a file and appended to right after the start returned, each reading the line; a file absent at the start, created with a line right after, read whole; a file held against every open at the start and released after, started at the end, emitting the line appended then and none of its history (where a lock can be held); a file held at the start and appended to during the hold emitting the line appended during the hold and the one after, none of its history; a file replaced by a rename during the hold read whole, with the line appended after; a file denied to its reader at the start, no identity readable, replaced by a rename and the replacement read whole with the line appended after (where an access-control list can deny, Windows); a file unreadable by its mode at the start and released, the line appended then read and none of its history, and one replaced by a rename during the hold read whole (Unix, not as root); a file truncated to a torn line, renamed away and replaced by a new file between two polls (the watcher off), the torn line then the new file's line; a file denied to its reader in a folder denied its listing at the start, replaced by a rename, the replacement read whole with the line appended after (Windows); a file in a folder denying search at the start, the folder opened again and the file replaced before the next look, the replacement read whole with the line appended after (Unix, not as root)

## C-036: Log families: a rotating family followed through its rotations with no line lost or duplicated
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal", scope: log families; docs/portal-plan.md "Scope"; proven against the bench's emitters, C-025)
- **Description:** A `FamilySpec` is a regex over file names, matched whole (a relative `/`-separated path when recursive, bounded in depth, a folder of a member's name no member), with two reserved groups: `date`, parsed with its chrono format (a date alone midnight; a year alone its first day, a month its first, an hour without its minutes the start of the hour, twelve-hour or twenty-four, padded or not; a token that fails its parse degrades to none and the file stays a member), and `idx`, parsed as a number (so `_10` follows `_2`); `resolve` lists the members in rotation order (the key, then the modification time, then the name, a keyless member first) with the active file last and marked: the newest by key in `NewestByOrder`, the file of the constant name in `Fixed` (absent, no file is active); a date group without its format or a format without the group, and a bad pattern, are refused by name; an unreadable folder is an I/O error naming it. A `FamilyTailSource` follows the family on its own thread through its rotations with zero lines lost, zero duplicated and the order kept, whatever the writer does: a clean rotation, a straggler line landing late in the retired file (drained to a stable end before the handoff), torn split writes, generations born inside one rescan (every generation between the one being read and the newest consumed, oldest first, before the newest is read), a fixed-name active renamed into the pattern (recognised by identity, never by position, so the file just opened is never read twice; the fixed path checked again after every listing, so a burst of rotations between two looks is never taken for members dropped in from outside and lost; a sweep that meets the file the cursor just opened, retired while the sweep ran, ends there, so a generation that retired after it waits for the cursor's own lines and the order holds through a second rotation inside the sweep); a family born after attach is all new content, every birth-window generation included, in both modes; a family that predates the session is followed from its active file alone, from its beginning or its end as configured, its retirees history; a family's start returns once its folder has been listed (or found absent) or its backfill located, so a line written right after it is never history, and a fixed-name active not yet born at the start is followed as a family born after the start, the retirees listed then being the history, so a generation that retires before the active is opened is read, never history; a family is followed by its keys: a pattern with neither a `date` nor an `idx` group is listed (by modification time, then name) but refused for following, by text, a pre-existing family on which no key parses is refused by text, and members whose keys tie are followed by name, the lower name history (the listing breaks a tie by time), so the keys must separate generations and a writer that reuses one is not followed. The bench's emitter (C-025) writes the files a plant writes, rotating on a line count into dated, indexed names, in UTF-8 or UTF-16 with its mark; its `family_shape` is the spec that resolves them, in rotation order with the date read back (a year alone as its first day, a month as its first, an hour without its minutes as the start of the hour; a pattern whose date is not one run of specifiers carrying a year has no shape), and the tail attached before the first file emits exactly the lines on disk, in order, once.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/tail_family.rs | resolving: unpadded indices numerically with the newest active and the key read, dated names chronologically and date-plus-index by both, a full date-time token and a date alone, fixed mode with the active last and marked and without it, non-members and a whole-name match and a folder of a member's name excluded, an unparseable date token first with a `NONE` key, a recursive scan with relative names ordered across subfolders and the flat spec seeing the flat folder, a pattern confined to a subtree, the same name in two folders kept apart, both spec refusals with their exact text and a bad pattern, keyless members ordered deterministically, a missing folder as an I/O error naming it; following, against a writer the test drives after an attach sentinel: indexed clean rotation of 5 000 lines, straggler and split writes of 3 000, bursts of 3 000 at a 250-byte cap under an 80 ms rescan, fixed-name clean rotation of 2 000, a straggler into the renamed file of 1 500, fixed-name bursts of 2 000, each with at least three generations, every line exactly once in order with a dense sequence; a family born after attach captured whole in both modes; a pre-existing family followed from the active file's beginning (the active file's lines then the new one) and from its end (the new line alone); against the bench's emitter: a UTF-8 emitter rotating every 40 lines and a UTF-16 emitter every 25, each followed from before its first file, the lines received equal to every line of every file on disk in family order and none more, the sequence dense; and the emitter's family shape (its regex and date format exact) resolving its files with the index counting from 1, the date today, the newest active, and every retired file holding exactly the rotation count; twenty families attached at the end and appended to right after the start returned, each reading the line; a keyless pattern listed by modification time then name (the same time, by name) and refused for following with its exact text, no worker started; the emitter's hourly shape exact, the next hour's key over every index of the hour before, its files listed in order, the next hour's first file followed from the end of the active, and a date split in two runs having no shape; a fixed-name family whose active is absent at the start returning at once and reading the active from its start when it comes; a pre-existing family on which no key parses ending its worker with the exact text; a reused index followed by name with the lower name never read; a twelve-hour hour read as the hour; a fixed active born after the start and rotated twice before the worker opens it, every generation read in order, five rounds with the watcher off; a family of one empty member, in either mode, planning nothing and falling through to live, the two lines then written read and the worker ending cleanly; a start between two files landing on the next file's first stamped line past two unstamped opening lines; ## C-038: Capture rules: a line read into typed series by a regex's named groups, the stamp parsed by ordered formats
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal", scope: regex capture; docs/portal-plan.md "Scope")
- **Description:** A `CaptureRule` (a name, a `regex` pattern with named groups, a scope of one source or every source, a type per group, an optional key group) turns a matching `LineEvent` into one `Sample` per named group with a value, every sample carrying the source, the series (the group's name), the key captured from the key group (none when absent), the line's stamp, its sequence and its arrival; a group is text unless typed int (decimal, trimmed), float (what the parser takes, trimmed) or time (the source's chrono formats in order, a date alone midnight), and a value that refuses its type is counted and never kills the line; the key group is no series; an optional group absent on the line emits nothing and counts nothing; the key is carried even when every value failed; a line outside the scope or not matching yields nothing. Construction refuses a bad pattern (lookaround included: one engine, `regex` syntax alone, as C-013 reads a message body by rules), a pattern with no named groups, and a key group the pattern lacks, naming the rule. `named_groups` lists a pattern's named groups in order, none for a bad or groupless pattern. A `TimestampSpec` locates a line's stamp by a pattern (its `ts` group, else the whole match) and parses it with ordered chrono formats (a fraction, a date alone as midnight), refusing an empty format list and a bad pattern; a `TimestampExtractor` keeps the format that last succeeded preferred (tried first; when it misses, the configured order with it skipped, never a rotation from it, so a format that never succeeded is not preferred over an earlier one), so an ambiguous stamp resolves the same way for a whole stream, while the stateless spec tries the configured order every time.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/capture.rs | a four-group rule with a key: the series in pattern order with their types (the key not among them, an untyped group text), three samples each carrying the key, the sequence, the arrival, the stamp and the source, with the exact values; a scoped rule on another source and a miss yielding nothing; two refusals counted (a text and a fraction into an int) beside the value that landed, trimmed signed and exponent numbers, an empty int refused and a NaN float taken; time groups parsing with two formats in order, a date alone at midnight, and no formats a counted failure with the line still matched; an absent optional group emitting nothing and a present one its text; the key carried with every value failed, and none when its group is absent; `named_groups` in order, an unnamed group not listed, empty for a bad and a groupless pattern; construction refused for a bad pattern (naming the rule, the engine's text), no groups, an unnamed group alone, and an unknown key group, each error's exact text, and a lookaround pattern refused as bad; the timestamp rule: a named group inside noise, a miss and garbage, the whole-match fallback, two formats in order, a fraction, a date alone and an impossible date, the extractor's preference moving to the format that parsed a 13th month and staying, the stateless spec unmoved, an empty format list refused with its text and a bad pattern as a pattern error; three formats with the second preferred, a stamp the preferred one misses read by the first configured format (as the stateless spec reads it) and that format preferred after

## C-039: A portal telegram channel: a device by its channel file, its catalog and reply rules, every message a typed row, every reply gated

- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal", rules: a device spoken over TCP is a telegram channel configured by encoding and decoding rules, its data stored like any other protocol's; docs/questions.md Q8; docs/portal-plan.md "Scope"; proven against the bench channel, C-029, its controller, C-031, and its pressure, C-032)
- **Description:** `channel::spawn` runs one TCP link to a device on its own thread, in either role, configured by core's channel file (C-017) and catalog (C-028) and by the gateway's reply rules, never by code: before anything binds or dials it asks the catalog whether its ids ride the framing (`Catalog::check_framing`, refused with the catalog's reason), compiles every reply rule against the catalog (the messages named must exist, every name the sent message writes given exactly once by a constant or by a field the answered message reads as the catalog says, a field with no source or two refused) and shapes every message's table (one column per binary field, typed by the catalog, an integer field an `Int`, text and hex `Text`; the payload's groups and a repeat's numbered names `Text`, a repeat wider than the table cap refused), each refusal naming the channel, the rule and the place. Running, it reads every frame's messages by the catalog's placing (`messages_of`, `decode`) and emits each as an event carrying the peer, the plant-local index clock, the unix arrival clock, the message's name and id, its fields and the raw frame, which decodes again; an id the catalog lacks or a body its rules refuse is an `Unreadable` event, counted apart; a keep-alive is reported; the link's state (waiting, linked with its peers, trouble with the wire's reason, stopped) is reported as it changes; a `MessageTable::row` turns the fields into a row positional against the columns, a missing name or a non-number in an integer column a hole. A reply rule met by its message composes the reply by the catalog (`encode`, the body placed by `frame`) and sends it to the peer the message came from, only while the master write switch (core's `WriteGate`, shared with every write path of the session) is on: off, the reply is withheld and counted, nothing composed, nothing sent, the device waiting on every item, and arming the switch answers the next message, live; a reply that cannot render or that the catalog refuses is refused and counted, never sent. The handle's `send` is the second write path of the same gate: the switch first (`Disarmed`, nothing composed), then a message the catalog lacks, a value missing or not fitting, an empty body in a layout without segments, a frame the framing refuses, a peer not connected or no peer, each a typed refusal with nothing on the wire; a message the catalog writes reaches the device by name, to one peer or to all. The status counts messages received and sent by name, unknown ids, undecodable bodies, replies sent, withheld and refused, and keeps the last refusal's text, a disarmed send's included; a peer gone between the look and the write, within one tick, is the wire's own error, the send counted; a stop is prompt and frees the port. Its reply rules are the gateway's own, not a controller file (C-030): the same act as a controller file's `[[answers]]` (a message answered by another sent back to its peer, every name it writes given), narrowed to a constant or a copied field and gated by the switch, each side checking its names through the catalog's `names` and `reads` (C-028).

Against the bench's machine, started from a bench file in a scratch folder over loopback: armed, the link waiting then linked, at least five announcements as rows exact to the rules file (the counter from 1, the lane cycling, the flag and the template or the no-read fault's flag and empty code, the weight in range), every one answered by the rule and every outcome back to destination 1 with a result of the mix, heartbeats for the four destinations with destination 4's full bit, every message's arrival within the clock and its index offset by the local zone, every raw frame decoding whole, the machine's own count agreeing (answered at least five, no unknown answer, no invalid destination), the status's counts exact, a stop within 1.5 s seen by the machine as its peer gone; disarmed, at least five announcements read and every reply withheld with no outcome, the machine answered nothing and waiting on five, the wire having sent the connect keep-alive alone, then the switch armed live and the next item answered with its outcome, then disarmed again and nothing more answered; the handle's send disarmed refused with the machine's register untouched, an unknown message, a missing value and a value too wide refused by the catalog's text with no command counted, an unknown peer refused, a stop command obeyed (the register 0, the announcements stopped, one command counted) and a command to one peer by id setting the speed, the last refusal's text kept, a send after the stop reporting the worker gone; the no-read fault at 100 % showing in every following row and a 20 ms interval giving at least 30 rows a second; a plant catalog with an extra id and a longer command: one unknown id and one refused body, each with its bytes, beside a status line read, the counts exact; ## C-041: The session store: every protocol's rows alike in one embedded database, views in one namespace, windows, pages, snapshots and CSV

- **Description:** `store::DuckStore` opens a fresh session database (feature `store`), in memory or in a file whose folder is created (a database already at the path refused by name: a session store is fresh, never a second session's rows beside the first's; an open refused after the file is made, by a view or a table's name, leaves nothing at its path, so the same open, its cause fixed, opens), with a memory limit and its spill beside it, and holds every protocol's data alike: one wide base table per rule, shaped by `BaseTableSpec` from a capture rule's series, an OPC rule's columns or a telegram message's table without distinction (the fixed columns `source`, `key`, `index_ms`, `ts`, `arrival_ms`, `seq`, then one typed column per value), a rule's rows appended positionally with holes, and the narrow `samples` table for bare producers; a base table name empty, quoted, taken (whatever the case) or `samples`, and a column named as a fixed one, are refused by name; user-defined tables are SQL views applied with a fixpoint so they resolve in any order, a definition that never resolves refused with its name and the engine's message, and a definition one SELECT, handed to the engine's `query()` so a second statement in its text is refused, at validation and when applied, and nothing in it runs, validated without running it. Every column reads on the narrow surface as a series of its source (the type as declared, the count of values with holes counting nothing, the distinct keys holding a value, the last value, the last point by stamp then sequence as a window orders them (two keys of one poll at one stamp), as the suite displays it, never as the engine prints it, a time with its microseconds; a column two tables of one source share one series, counted together, the last value the later), a numeric window inclusive at both ends and time-ordered whatever the append order, filtered to one key (an unkeyed row the empty key), text yielding nothing and a time its epoch milliseconds; a series declared with two types across rules is counted as a conflict, never silent. The table surface lists every table and view with its columns (an exact row count for a table, none for a view; a view that cannot be described now, a linked file gone between a removal and its replacement, listed with no columns and hiding no other table), pages a table's rows newest first by its time column, every other column breaking a tie so no row repeats or is skipped across pages (two rows identical in every cell aside), with every cell typed as JSON (a timestamp, a date and a time of day as milliseconds, a decimal a number, an interval an ISO 8601 duration with its sign on the seconds, a blob hex, a hole null, a nested value alone as the engine's debug text), windowed inclusive at both ends, offset and bounded by the page cap, its total and its rows read in one transaction so both are one moment of the store (a row the ingest commits between the two in neither; a view, which reads again at every statement whatever it reads, its window read once into a table of the transaction's own, so a line a logger adds to a file a view reads between the two is in neither), the transaction ended whatever the page's outcome, a failure or a panic inside it (a time of day in nanoseconds, which the engine's driver panics on, the caller's to catch, `docs/questions.md` Q130) leaving the connection to read the next page, and charts one numeric column of a table or view keyed or not (a key asked of a table with no key column refused as a name the catalog lacks), every name checked against the catalog so no text reaches the SQL unquoted; a snapshot writes a standalone database file any tool reads, tables as data and views as views, never over an existing file (refused by name before anything is attached; a refused snapshot leaves nothing attached, and the next one writes); a table or view exports to CSV with a header and the whole database to a folder with its schema; a CSV the operator keeps is read live through a view (its edits, a replacement and a new column seen on the next query, the file never held open), the engine's sniffer its first reader, the padded reader (on one thread, so a quoted line break is read whole) taking any file the sniffer would read ragged (a collapsed delimiter, a short row, a long row: any outcome but a regular table, read off the file itself over the first 2 048 rows, a blank line no row, in the sniffer's delimiter, quote and escape (the quote either of its two readings saw, the file as it is or asked to pad, none when neither saw one; ## C-042: What the embedded database does, pinned against the engine itself
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/portal-plan.md "Scope": the session store; core's C-023 for the pattern: a pinned stack's behaviour pinned by a test run first after a bump)
- **Description:** `duckdb` 1.10504.0, pinned exactly, behaves as the store assumes, checked against the engine in a test of its own so that a version bump that moves the engine fails on the assumption that broke, by name: an appender's rows reach a cloned connection on another thread once flushed; an unsigned 64-bit value and a microsecond timestamp round-trip and `epoch_ms` converts; a nearest-preceding `ASOF` join takes one row per probe within a tolerance written as a plain conjunct; `time_bucket` snaps to an absolute grid; `DESCRIBE (select)` yields the columns without running the query and names a bad reference; the catalog functions list tables, views and columns with `NOT internal` leaving the system's out; `duckdb_memory()` reports a figure and a plan is inspectable; `COPY FROM DATABASE` snapshots tables as data and views as views into a file a fresh connection reads; a file-backed database takes a memory limit and a temp directory and reopens with its rows; its committed rows stand in a write-ahead log beside it (`<file>.wal`), which `CHECKPOINT` folds into the file (no log after it, the close's own checkpoint turned off, and the rows read back by a fresh connection), the file three 4 KiB headers and whole blocks, growing by whole blocks (the size a record counts, C-091); a connection behind a mutex is shared across threads; a view over `read_csv_auto` re-reads the file on every query, never holds it open, and re-expands its columns; and the engine reports the pinned version; and, the read side the store relies on: the value variants the engine hands back for a date, a time, a decimal, an interval, a huge integer and a timestamp; the listing's aggregate (a count, a distinct count filtered, a max over a case, an `arg_max`) over a union skipping null cells and typing the last value; a positional tie-break after the time column ordering every column with nulls last under `DESC`; an empty table copied to a header alone; `EXPORT DATABASE` writing the schema with its views and one file per table under a sanitised name inside the folder; the CSV reader's options typing a decimal comma and, on a ragged file, padding a short row, widening the schema by a numbered column for a long row within the first 2 048 lines (the header included) and refusing the read with the line named for one past them, whatever the sample size; a view binding when it is created (a reference to a table not there yet refused with the catalog's text and no view left behind, a bad column with the binder's text, the retry succeeding once the reference exists), which the store's fixpoint over view definitions rests on; the appender taking the store's typed cells (a text, a null, a microsecond timestamp, a double, nulls in typed columns) through a slice of `ToSql` and reading them back exactly; the sniffer's outcomes on a marked, a header-only, an empty and a missing file, and on a ragged file (a short row collapsing it to one column; a long row on any line up to 2 047 with a short row after it collapsing the file to one column, numeric or text alike, whatever the file's length; a run of long rows ending the file and starting on line 2 048 or before kept alone of all its rows (runs on lines 2 047 to 2 049 and 2 048 to 2 050 kept whole), its first row read as the header, duplicate names made unique, when every column the run gives is text or one of its cells cannot be read as its column's type (the types from the rows after it, or from the row itself when it is alone), and kept as data otherwise (as probed: a run of numeric rows or of rows mixing text and numbers kept whole under engine-named columns, a single mixed row kept, a run of all-text rows or a numeric row before a text one losing its first row to the header, a single all-text row keeping none); a long row on line 2 048 with a short row after it, a single long row ending the file on line 2 049, and long rows from line 2 049 up to line 20 480, the engine's default sample size, refusing the sniff; one past line 20 480 refusing the read with the line named); a copy into a foreign database refused by the engine on the first table it holds and detached after; and, for the padded reader the store forces: on one thread (`parallel=false`) a quoted line break read whole where the parallel reader refuses the read by its line, the window's edge kept (a long last row on line 2 048 widening, on 2 049 refusing the read by its line) and lines ending in a bare carriage return read; the sniffer's dialect: `(empty)` for a quote or an escape it did not see, a `'` quote, `\r` as a line ending, and, asked to pad (`null_padding=true`), the delimiter, quote and escape of a ragged file it otherwise collapses on `|`; a forced reader given a quote reads with it, and left to find its own it may pick none and refuse a file it reads whole with one; it opens a quote only at a field's start, a `"` text with `quote=''` or `"`, and after one leading space it opens its field, after two or a tab none; its window counts rows, a blank line moving it by none; the sniffer prints a tab delimiter as the tab itself; in memory, deleted rows hand their space back (a table appended four million rows keeping the newest 50 000 never above 32 MiB, its later peak no higher than its earlier, where one deleting nothing passes 32 MiB within a million); the two sniffs disagree on a ragged file's quote both ways (none as it is and `"` asked to pad, and the reverse); a `"` quote with no escape refuses a doubled quote, which the sniffer reports with its escape `"`.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/store_engine.rs | a thousand appended rows counted by a cloned connection on another thread; a value past 2^62 and a microsecond timestamp read back equal and the timestamp's `epoch_ms`; the `ASOF` pairs exact with the out-of-tolerance one filtered; three bucket anchors exact; `DESCRIBE` columns exact, a cast the run refuses described while the run errors, and the binder's exact words for the bad column; the one table, the one view and the three columns in order; a positive memory figure and a non-empty plan; a snapshot's rows and view read by a fresh connection; the limit's exact text and the temp directory read back and 100 rows reopened; the foreign copy's error text and the file untouched after the detach; the one-thread padded reader: a quoted line break refused on line 2 by the parallel reader and read as three exact rows on one thread, 2 047 rows with one widened cell for a long last row on line 2 048 and the read refused on line 2 049, three exact rows of a bare-carriage-return file; the dialects exact (`,` with no quote, escape `(empty)` and `\n`; `'` for a regular quoted file; `\r`), `|` for the two ragged files unpadded and `,` `'` and `,` `"` `\` padded; the late quoted file: 2 047 rows with one widened cell and its line break whole when given `"`, refused on line 2 049 when left to its own; the inches file's four rows and its `12" long` cell with no quote and with `"`; a long row on line 2 049 after a blank line widening (2 047 rows), on line 2 050 refusing the read by that line; the tab printed as a tab;
