## C-043: The session: a project's sources of the three protocols running as workers, every row into one store alike, the ingest keeping what a shell and the server read
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal": the gateway runs the project; docs/portal-plan.md "Module map": session)
- **Description:** A `Session` starts from a `SessionConfig` (the one a project file yields, `Project::session_config`, C-046): the log sources (a file or a family, each with an optional timestamp rule), the capture rules (each a wide base table named for the rule, its typed groups the columns, its key group the key), the OPC sources (compiled as C-040, each rule a table), the telegram channels (each catalog message a table named for the message, with C-041's rows) and the project's SQL views. Everything is checked before anything spawns (the channels whole, through the channel's own check: the framing, the reply rules, the tables), and refused by name: no source, an empty or a duplicate source id, a rule's scope naming no log source, an unknown group type, a rule or group name outside the name shape of C-040, a table name taken twice across rules, OPC rules, messages and views, whatever the case (naming both owners), a start point in the past into a family without a timestamp rule (nothing to locate it by), and the OPC and channel compile refusals (C-040, C-039). Started, every worker runs on its own thread over the store's (C-041) base tables; a start point in the past reads a file whole and backfills a family (C-037), none follows every log live from its end. A pump thread of its own drains every source's events into wide rows (a captured line, a mapped poll, a read message), flushes them into the store and keeps what a reader needs without a store scan: every series' type, count, distinct keys (capped at 64, flagged), last index and last value; the store's vitals (value cells, bytes, type conflicts); the ingest's counters (lines, polls, messages, rows, failures, store errors, the last error's text); and every source's health on the arrival clock (waiting before anything arrived, live within the idle window, idle past it with the silence, retrying with the reason while an OPC server or a peer is away, ended, or failed with the worker's text; a log's with whether its start's history is in the store, C-096), refreshed every 500 ms. A pass never waits on a reader: the store's memory figure among the vitals is read through the ingest's own connection, so a reader holding the store's query connection (a heavy window, an export) delays no row. It broadcasts every numeric sample as JSON data frames to whoever listens, each sample with its source, series, key, stamp, value and the series' count with it (the count the cache shows once it holds that sample, so a listener orders two samples under one stamp); a slow listener drops frames, nothing backpressures the plant. One master write switch, off unless the project says so, is the gate every OPC handle and channel handle of the session shares: set live, the next write obeys it, and a tag no rule marks writable is refused whole, armed or not. A stop ends every worker first, then the pump, whose last passes drain the channels dry (every row the workers sent before the stop stored, cap or no cap) and publish a last refresh, every source ended or failed with its text and the counters final, joins them, and reports each worker's end.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/session.rs, crates/veredas-portal/src/store/connections_test.rs | against the bench's plant (its OPC UA server and the controller reference's machine over real sockets) and a real log: a session over the three with a start point in the past and a view: the sources listed by kind, 20 stamped lines captured as typed rows keyed by the item and indexed on the stamp (the first and the last exact, 7 for one key), the view's 9 rows, the server's polls as rows with the drawn speed in its bounds and the word as configured, the machine's announcement in its message's table with its source and its integer item, the series cache listing the three sources with the exact type, count, keys, last index and last value, every source's health live with the log's count and last stamp, the vitals and the counters, ten lines appended (a line no rule reads among them) arriving as frames with their exact stamps, values, keys and the series' counts (21 to 30) and as rows, the text state's count 20 with its samples though no frame carries them, a stop ending the three workers cleanly and the machine seeing its peer go, then every numeric series of the log and the server framed (a float and the integers alike), each series framed counting one up from frame to frame to the cache's count and a keyless sample framed with no key (the machine announces before anyone listens, so its item is checked through the cache, not a frame), the cache's type, count, keys, last index and last value for the log's temperature, the server's speed and word and the machine's item as the store holds them, and the state's count 30; the refusals' exact texts (no source, a duplicate id, a blank id, an unknown scope, an unknown type, a table name taken by a rule and a message, a backfill without a timestamp rule); the switch off by default refusing an OPC write and a channel send before anything reaches the plant (the word unchanged, the machine running), armed live writing the word (seen on the server) and stopping the machine by the command, a polled tag not marked writable refused whole, disarmed live refusing both again with the word kept; a quiet log read as waiting, then live after a line with its count, then idle with its silence past the window, the last stamp kept; a rule name outside the shape, a view named like a base table and two views of one name refused with their exact texts; a 60 000-line file read whole with the pump held on its refresh while the stop begins, every row stored and counted and the source ended after the stop; a family on which no key parses failed at once with its text, the stop returning the failure and the health keeping it after; seventy keys on one series listed as 64 and capped, the server and the peer away before the start read as retrying with the reason, a listener that never reads lagged once more than 256 frames went out while every line kept reaching the store (the server and the peer dialled on ports held bound and listening for nobody, never ports a stopped bench freed); the pump's passes over three seconds while an export of the rows into a named pipe nobody reads holds the store's query connection, and while four readers take it in back-to-back pages, each at least half its passes over three quiet seconds, the five lines written during the hold stored during it, the export then ending with a header and the forty rows it saw (Unix, where the pipe is a FIFO); in the crate, the memory figure read with both readers' connections held (the query's and the snapshot's), the same figure as read unheld, the fastest of five reads under 50 ms
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-043.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-044: The server: the live stream and windowed REST over a session, the write surface refused by default on the wire and whitelisted
- **Date:** 2026-11-07
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-portal": serves the data and gates the writes; docs/portal-plan.md "Module map": serve; Veredas 1's server as it was)
- **Description:** Under the `serve` feature, a `router` over one session's `Live` (C-043), served on a bound listener. `GET /events` is a server-sent event stream opening with a `hello` event carrying the session snapshot, then every data frame the ingest broadcasts as a `data` event, a `lag` event carrying the count of frames a slow client missed (its cue to re-window; the stream goes on, nothing lost unsaid), a keep-alive comment every five seconds holding an idle connection. `GET /api/session` is the snapshot from the ingest's caches, never a store query: the session's name, start and started, whether it still runs (false once its stop has published the end), the store axis now and the arrival now, the sources by kind, every source's health as a label with its detail, count, last arrival, last stamp and whether its start's history is in the store (C-096), the vitals, the counters, the switch's position. `GET /api/series` is the listing from the cache, typed and counted, sorted by source then series. `GET /api/series/data` is a numeric window of one series (`from` and `to` inclusive, an optional `key`) downsampled server-side by largest-triangle-three-buckets to a point budget clamped to 10..=5000 (1000 by default), the endpoints always kept, with the raw total and whether it was downsampled. `GET /api/tables` is the store's catalog with kinds, columns and exact row counts; `GET /api/table/data` a windowed page of typed rows with its schema, total and truncation, or with `y` one column charted like a series window; an unknown table is 404 with its name. The write surface is a whitelist over the handles' own gates (C-040, C-039), never a passthrough: `GET /api/opc/read` reads one polled tag on demand (no switch needed; 404 for a tag not in the session); `POST /api/opc/write` and `POST /api/channels/send` answer 403 with the gate's text while the switch is off, before a byte of the body is read; armed, a body that is not the shape is 400 in the same error form, one over 64 KiB 413 in it (a tag's value or a message's fields never fill that), a tag no rule marks writable, an unknown OPC source or an unknown channel is 403 by name, a value that does not fit the tag's type 400 by name, an unknown message or a body the catalog refuses 400, a write the plant never saw 503 (a server away; a peer not there, or a channel's worker gone or silent, a channel's wire carrying no confirmation of its own), an OPC write it may have seen 504. The switch is armed in process alone: no endpoint sets it. Every query is a pure function of its parameters over the store, so the server holds no state per client.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/serve.rs | over a real socket with a raw TCP client, against the bench's plant: the snapshot's running flag, name, start, the two clocks, the sources by kind, the switch off, every source's health live with the log's count and last stamp, the vitals and the counters; the listing with the exact type, count, keys and last index of a series and its sort order; a window of 40 points downsampled to 10 with the exact endpoints, a window of 10 inside it passed through whole, a key narrowing it to 13; the catalog with the base tables, the samples table and the view, a table's exact row count and columns; a page of 5 rows of 40 truncated, the view's 19, a column charted to 10 points, an unknown table's 404 text; a tag read on demand with its value, and an unknown tag's 404 text; the write surface: an OPC write and a channel send refused 403 by default with the gate's text and nothing at the plant (the word unchanged, the machine running, no command counted), the switch armed in process shown by the snapshot, a polled tag not writable and an unknown source refused 403 by name, a value that does not fit 400 by name, the write landing on the server, an unknown message and a missing value refused 400 by the catalog, the command stopping the machine, the switch off again refusing both with the word kept; the stream's hello (a 200, the event-stream type, the running snapshot by name) then five lines appended arriving as data events whose samples carry the exact stamps and values; the budget 1000 by default and clamped to 10 and 5000 (purely) and three points asked giving ten over the socket, the 504 of a write unconfirmed (purely); disarmed, a value as a number, a send without a message, a body that is no JSON and a 3 MiB body to each route all 403 with the switch's text; armed, the two shapes 400 in the error form, a 3 MiB body 413 in it with the limit named, an unknown channel 403 by name, a peer not there 503 by text; a client that never reads, four hundred large frames pushed with the plant stopped and nothing else flowing: the frames it receives and the counts its lag events carry summing to four hundred, every lag event carrying its count, the frame sent after them read; the server stopped, a fresh stream's hello then two comments within fourteen seconds, the first four to seven seconds after the hello and the next four to seven after it, a write 503 by text; the session stopped, the snapshot not running and the log ended
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-044.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-045: One header reader in core: `format` and `version` read before the body, refused by name, or handed to a migration
- **Date:** 2026-11-08
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Versioned files"; P-010: "the header reader is written out in each, ten lines, until a second component needs core's")
- **Description:** `veredas_core::schema` reads the stamp every plain-text file of the suite opens with, before the body: `check(text, noun, format, version)` for a reader that knows one version, refusing in this order and in these words (the noun the reader's own, as `a catalog file`): not TOML with the parser's reason, no `format`, a `format` that is not a string (`` not a catalog file: `format` is not a string ``: a stamp of the wrong type is no stamp), another format (`` `format` is `x`, not `y` ``), no `version`, a `version` that is not a whole number from 0 to 4294967295 (named the same way), another version (`version N of `x` is not known here (this reader knows M)`); the format is decided whole before the version is looked at, so a file of another format is refused for its format whatever its version says (each key read as the file types it, a whole number of any width the parser offers included, so no value type's limits decide; an integer the parser itself cannot read, past 128 bits, is its reason before any key, as any text it cannot parse), and the text is parsed for its syntax alone, the body included but skipped unread, so a body value the stamp never looks at is the reader's own to judge; and `stamp(text, noun)` for a reader that knows several versions and decides what to do with each (a known older one migrated, an unknown one refused, never guessed at; both keys required and of their type, the format first, a version of 0 read as it stands for the reader to decide). The four readers that wrote the header out by hand (the channel file C-017, the catalog C-028, the controller file C-030, the bench file C-033) the project file (C-046), the supervisão file and the binding file (C-048) and the release manifest (C-290) call it and map its refusals onto their own errors, so every reference's pinned refusal reads as before: their tests are the proof that nothing moved.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/src/schema.rs (unit) | a good header checked and stamped; not TOML with the noun in the text; no `format`, another format, no `version`, another version, each with its exact text; both keys missing and the keys inside a table refused for the format first; another format with a string version, and with versions of 2^64-1, 2^64 and 2^128-1, refused for its format; an integer `format` (one past 64 bits too), and a string, a fractional, a negative, an overflowing, a 64-bit, a 65-bit, a 128-bit and a negative 64-bit `version`, a boolean, an array, an inline table, a date and a nan, each named by its key in the not-TOML form; the largest version a `u32` holds checked; a version of 2^128 refused by the parser with its reason, no key named; a good header before a body that is not TOML refused with the parser's reason and no key named, and before a body value past `i64` checked; the stamp of another format read with both keys required, a version 0 read as it stands, the stamp without `format`, of an empty text, of an integer `format` and of a negative `version` refused the same way | and the readers' own tests unchanged: crates/veredas-core/tests/telegram_schema.rs, crates/veredas-core/tests/telegram_catalog.rs, crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/controller_rules.rs, crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/bench_file.rs
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-045.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-046: The project file: one `veredas-project` TOML the gateway runs, checked whole before anything starts, Veredas 1's folder read into it, every file of the suite held to its stamp
- **Date:** 2026-11-08
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "Versioned files", "veredas-portal"; P-010 promoted; docs/project-schema.md)
- **Description:** A project is one TOML file, `format = "veredas-project"`, `version = 1`, read by core's reader (C-045) before its body, a misspelled key anywhere refused with its name (inside an inline table too: `start`, `store` and `active` are read key by key, a key missing from one and a shape that is neither word nor table named as well). It names the session; its `[session]` says where the logs start (`midnight`, a date-time, a date, `{ last_hours = N }`, or live when absent), whether it records to the drive (`record = { dir, max_mb, every_s }`, C-091, refused beside a store on the drive), where the store lives (`ram`; `{ window_s = N }`, in memory kept as a window of N seconds, N at least 1, with no file and no memory ceiling beside it, C-090; `{ disk = "<file>", memory_limit_mb }`, a file pinned by name, refused when one already stands there, C-041; or `{ dir = "<folder>", memory_limit_mb }`, a file of each run's own in that folder, named from the project's name and the session's start to the second, so every run leaves its own, `docs/questions.md` Q16; the two forms in one table refused naming both), whether writes are armed (off when absent) and the idle window; its `[server]` where to serve, or nothing; its `[secrets]` where the sealed values sit (`file`, beside the project, `secrets.toml` when absent, committed with it) and which environment variable holds the key that opens them (`key_env`), required once a source names a `secret`, refused by name otherwise and when its file or variable is blank, `docs/questions.md` Q15; its `[[log]]` sources are a `file` or a family (`dir` and `pattern`, with `date_format`, `active` and `recursive`), never both nor neither, each with an optional timestamp rule; its `[[rule]]` capture rules, `[[opc]]` sources (C-040's declarations) and `[[channel]]` channels (their channel file and catalog read from beside the project, their role `listen` with `bind` or `connect` with `host`, their reply rules checked against the catalog) and `[[table]]` views complete it. `project::load` resolves every relative path against the file's folder, compiles every rule once (a timestamp rule, a family spec, a capture rule, an OPC spec without its secret: the password is an entry of the secrets file, which the session opens when it starts), loads every referenced file, claims every table name once across the rules, the OPC rules, the catalogs' messages and the views (naming both owners when taken twice), requires every source id once and at least one source, and refuses everything else by name, so a project that loads is a session that can start (`Project::session_config`), bar what only the running pieces can tell (a view's SQL, which the store resolves when it opens, C-041; the secrets file and the key the environment holds when the session starts: the session opens the file in memory before it compiles a table and refuses by name, never by value, the variable unset or not a key, a file that is not a secrets file (by its path), a file the key does not open, and a source whose entry is not there, a file not there yet holding no entry so that an imported project's first run is refused by the entry its source names); the session (C-043) claims the same names, compiles the same tables and runs the channel's check again, through the same functions. A start in the past with a family that has no timestamp rule, a group name outside the name shape and a reply rule the channel's check refuses are refused too, by name. The executable `veredas-portal <project.toml>` runs it, serving when the project says so (on the address it bound, printed, the port the system's when asked for 0), printing every source's health once a second until stdin closes, then ending well; `--check` reads, checks and prints the plan without starting anything (neither the key nor the secrets file needed); `--new-key` prints a fresh key, 64 hexadecimal characters, and nothing else; `--secret <project.toml> <entry>` seals the value on stdin (to its end, the final line break dropped, LF or CRLF and nothing else: a bare carriage return and a line break inside kept) as that entry of the project's secrets file (its name trimmed), ChaCha20-Poly1305 under the key the project's variable holds with a fresh nonce each time and the entry's name bound in, the file created stamped `veredas-secrets` version 1 when none is there, printing the entry's name and the file's path and never the value, and refusing by name a project without `[secrets]`, a variable unset or not a key, a key that does not open the entries already there (nothing written then), and a file that is not a secrets file (every refusal of the file names its path, the stamp's with the suite's header text after it), a stray argument or an option given twice or in a name's place a usage error; `--version` printing its name and version, a run ended by closing stdin or, on Unix, by a SIGTERM or a SIGINT alike (exit 0, nothing on stderr); `--from-v1 <folder>` reads a Veredas 1 project folder into this file on stdout and names on stderr what it did not carry (a disabled part, a telegram source without a catalog, an OPC source with no rule to map its tags, a rule whose scope names a log not carried, a view the engine refuses over the tables the file creates (the import loads the file as the gateway does, compiles its tables through the session's own compilation with a blank for every secret, and opens an in-memory store over them: a table nothing creates, a column no table has, whatever the shape of the SQL, is the engine's to say, and the view goes with the engine's reason) and a view over one of those, while a view over a file the engine cannot read where the import runs (a CSV on the plant's machine) is carried as written and named, and so is a view that reads it, directly or through other views, whatever their order in the file (the engine names every view it refuses at once, and the import judges them together, every set-aside view standing in as an empty table of its name so that whatever else a reader names is the engine's to bind: a reader that names a table nothing creates beside a set-aside view falls for that table whichever it names first, and a reader of a column only the file can show is carried and named as over a file not here, while a fault the import can judge in full, a column a table the file creates does not have, drops the reader with the reason and what reads it falls after; a reader refused for naming a view set aside, deferred, or set aside by its own reading in the same round is judged again against the stand-ins in the next (a cycle of readers falls, each with its reason), and one whose error names a set-aside view that already stands in, through a schema the stand-in is not in, falls with the reason, so every round decides a view and the judging ends), a security mode never implemented whatever shape its setting took, a certificate authentication never implemented, a store mode Veredas 1 never had, a memory limit on an in-memory store, the auto-export, the rules' skew-monitor threshold, the screens, the grep, the command sequencer), a credential-store password becoming a `secret` entry named for its source as a plain word (letters, digits, `_`, `-` and `.`, every other character `_`, every leading `-` `_` too so the tool never reads it as an option, a word already taken given `_2`, `_3`, so the call needs no shell's quoting; a source not carried names no entry and takes no word), the note giving the call that seals it, and the `[secrets]` table written with the suite's variable `VEREDAS_KEY`, an absolute start in either of Veredas 1's spellings (a `T` or a space between) carried in this file's own, one that is no date-time, or one with an offset (a date-time, but not on the plant's clock), named with the session starting live, a start of zero hours carried as one and named, a CSV-linked table a view over the engine's CSV reader, and a float-typed tag with bits an int word (bits are carved out of an int word, C-040), each named; it never guesses, and the file it prints loads, checked before it is printed: a folder that leaves nothing loadable is refused with the reason and the notes, never handed over as a file the gateway would then refuse. Law L7 (`tools/leis`): every TOML file of the repository that is not a tool's own (a cargo manifest, the test runner's `.config/nextest.toml`, the linter's `clippy.toml` or `.clippy.toml` at any depth, `docs/questions.md` Q225; `.toml` in any case), and every TOML block of a reference in `docs/` (a fence of three or more backticks or tildes whose info string's first word is `toml` in any case, whatever follows it, inside blockquotes and list items nested in any order as well (a list mark followed by a space or a tab), closed by a fence of its kind at least as long with nothing else on the line at the same quote depth, by a line with fewer quote marks than it was opened under (the blockquote ends there, as CommonMark has it), or by the end of the text; a reference's `.md` in any case), states a `veredas-*` `format` and a `version` from 1. The declarations the session reads (a log source, a timestamp rule, a capture rule) live in the project module, so the light build (no store, no server) still reads a project.

## C-047: veredas-supervisao — the web interface

No two sessions of one supervisão file open one database file: a store pinned to one file, or a store of each run's own or a record in one folder by projects whose names portal writes as one file name (named from the project's name as portal writes it, letters, digits, `-` and `_` kept and every other character `_`, and the host's one start), refused when the file loads, naming both sessions, the place read as one however its path is written (made absolute, then read part by part as the drive reads it, a part that is a link followed to where it leads whether or not that stands yet, its target read part by part in turn, forty links at most (a path that meets one more opens nowhere, as the drive refuses it, and is compared with none), a `..` the parent of what is read so far, written or in a link's target; on Windows without case, by Unicode's lowercase). ## C-051: The 2D layout: the SVG bound by object ID, live

a fill and a tag painted by their fill alone, never stroked), with its mark and word beside it when it is not normal (a marker's below it, moving with it), the view's fit taking in everything the page writes beside the drawing (a label, a mark and word or a value at its edge never cut by the drawing's frame nor covered, the page's own controls standing clear of the drawing's box at every window size, the frame growing from the last fit and never shrinking while the view stays fitted; a zoom (the buttons' or the wheel's) or a pan the person's until Fit, a press of the mouse's primary button a pan only once the pointer has gone 4 px from where it went down (in any direction), the pan ending with the press wherever the button is let go and on any move with the button no longer held (its release lost to a native menu, say), a click picking what its press went down on (a marker slid from under a still pointer, a release a pixel past an element's edge) and clearing the selection only when the press went down on the background, and the click that ends a pan picking nothing and clearing nothing (a finger's drag or pinch on a touch panel is not claimed; a change of the screen's scaling with the window's CSS size unchanged, a window moved between monitors, is a limit: no resize is observed, and Chromium's emulation moves no text box with the scale factor); the page's own controls clear of the drawing whatever lines they wrap to (the drawing's padding follows their heights); everything the page places by an element's box (a marker's place and its mark and word among them) measured anew whenever the view's scale changes, a fit, a zoom or the window, since Chromium lays a text out at the size it is drawn and its box moves with the scale; a marker its layer hides measured as it is drawn, the layer's hiding lifted while the boxes are read), levels filling up the screen within the element's outline by the element's own fill rule (a shape with a hole, `evenodd` as Inkscape's Combine writes it, filled around its hole; an element with no area, a line or a straight path or polyline or a group of them (a group of each kind of shape the reader's `DRAWN` lets an object draw on that can have no area: a path, a line, a polyline and a polygon; ## C-052: The dashboards: every panel kind computed in Rust from the session's store and drawn
- **Date:** 2026-11-09
- **Source:** user (docs/design/supervisao/ "Dashboard objects"; docs/binding-schema.md "A panel")
- **Description:** Each panel of a binding's dashboards is computed from the session's store into what the page draws (a value tile in four states, its sub-line the rule that named its state as the feed picked it (one over another series naming that series and its key, its comparison bare), a number a person wrote (a rule's, a target, a trend's limit, a bars' target, a cells' spread) said in full, as the binding writes it (the shortest text that reads back as the binding's number: `4.50` said `4.5`, `1e3` and `1_000` said `1000`), by the host and drawn as the host wrote it, a trend with its limit and breaches, bars per clock-aligned bucket (a limit: a width that divides a day from midnight, another from the epoch, a week from a Thursday) against a target with the running one marked, each from 0 up to its value or down to it, state over time (a run one state, its class and its word together; the state holding at the window's start drawn from it; a rule written out over the object's own series its own; an object whose rule reads another series refused on a timeline, Q24), every chart of an object bound to one key reading that key's samples alone, a state grid with the count of its cells not normal, levels against ticks, a table with inline bars (a value's magnitude against the column's largest, a column with rules keeping its state and word beside its bar) and state words, a time written as the store's clock reads it (a timestamp of any unit or zone to the second, a date as its day, a time of day with its zone or without to the second, the day's end (24:00:00) as itself (a limit: portal turns a stamp finer than a millisecond into milliseconds toward 1970, so a time before 1970 within a millisecond of its second is written at the next second; a limit: the engine's driver hands over a time of day with its zone as its local time alone, so its offset is not read; a time past the clock's reach, beyond the year 262 143 either way, the engine's infinities among them, is written as its milliseconds): every date and time type the engine lists but the interval, which portal writes as text; a limit: a time of day in nanoseconds, which the engine's driver cannot read, leaves its panel saying so, until portal reads it as a TIME (Q130, ruled 2026-11-25)), an integer cell in its own digits however large, a column read by its engine type (in a numeric one the digits of an integer past 64 bits a number, its places written as zeros (none, and no point, at 0 places, up to the reader's 6), its bar and its rules reading it, a rule through the double nearest it, a limit: a threshold is a double too; in any other a string text, sorted as text; a boolean 1 or 0 to its rules, its bar and its sort, written true or false), charts in cells (a value column that is no number refused in the panel's place) with a median, its sparkline, a distribution on one scale, σ and n, each row of the window counted once while rows arrive, read in order by its stamp, source and sequence and told from another by every column (so a view fanning a stored row into several keeps each; a view as its table must carry `index_ms`, `source` and `seq`; a limit: a view's rows alike in every column are one), a number key by its full value (an integer however large, its keys in their exact order) and a time key as the clock reads it, the keys in the key column's own order by its engine type (each NUMERIC type the engine lists, a time by the clock, a boolean false before true, any other type as text, an ENUM's labels among them, a text column's digits as text), a row with no key or no value charted in no row and counted beside the window, a table's sort exact for whole numbers (never through a double), the first rule that holds naming a state on a timeline and in a table as on a tile (limits: a DECIMAL is read as the double nearest it, portal's conversion, and a UHUGEINT as the engine's driver hands it over, a double), capped said only when the cap left a row of the window unread; a panel that cannot be computed (refused or panicking) saying why in its place, the others drawn; a limit: the windows, buckets and runs sit on the store's axis, the plant's wall clock read as UTC (C-041), so across a change of the plant's clock an hour repeats or is skipped, and midnight is the wall clock's) and the page draws it, computing nothing but its drawing: positions, and its axes' labels from the window and values the host gave it (a trend's four values and five times, its axis from the lowest to the highest of its points and its limit, 0 to 1 with neither; bars' top and bottom values and 0 between them, the axis from the lowest to the highest of 0, the values and the target, 0 to 1 when all are 0; buckets' starts and target, a timeline's start), its hover texts (a bar's, a run's, a grid cell's) as the host gave them, a value missing drawn as missing on every surface the host sends one to (a tile's figure, a grid cell's, a level's fill and figure), a sample that is no finite number (NaN, an infinity) no value, left out of every chart and every bucket, each chart's left edge room enough for its longest value label, a rule's word on a tile's badge as written, every text as written (none the stylesheet generates or changes: the page's own headings typed in capitals, a label, a column name and σ as written, Q28; and its white space as written on every text a panel draws, a title, a label, a word, a value, a unit, an id, a limit, a target, a note, a key, a heading, a cell and a refusal's reason, two spaces, a leading or a trailing one, a tab and a line break never collapsed; a trailing space is kept and hung at its line's end, its width the line's, not measured, a limit; a hover text (a bar's and a run's `title`, a grid cell's) is held as written in its attribute and drawn by the browser's own tooltip, not read, a limit; a chart's labels, the bars' target among them, keeping theirs too), each state painted otherwise than every other wherever a panel draws one, by computed values (a tile whole, its badge, figure, sub-line and sparkline with it, each object read as the page drew it in its own state and the probe swapped on the one with the most parts, a tile's twin of one point drawing its dot in every state; a state's paint told apart per object whole, not per part, the clause's reading; a grid cell; a level's fill and its figure; a timeline's run and its word now; a table's cell), none in the accent's four colours or its band of hue but the neutral series the accent draws (a normal sparkline, a table's inline bar; Q22), a window of one point drawn as a dot on a sparkline as on a trend, and an answer for a dashboard no longer shown dropped (another dashboard's panels never standing under a tab: until its answer comes, the grid says the dashboard is asked), a refusal of it alike, a refusal on time said in the dashboard's place, the five-second refresh (each ask five seconds after the last, measured) never asking while an answer is still out, and giving an ask up after ten seconds with no answer, said in the dashboard's place, the next tick asking again. It reads the store through C-041's queries (numeric windows, pages of rows) and adds the panels' computation and drawing; the store itself is C-041's. Limits: a store kept as a window (C-090) lets rows go by their arrival while the cells are read, and a row let go from the stamp a page ended on makes the next page read past as many rows still held there (it reads by place among the rows at a stamp), so near the window's edge a cells panel may count fewer rows than the store holds; and an object reading a text series is refused on every chart when the binding loads (C-048), a controller's state word (C-092) among them.

a post is given no deadline of the page's own, so one whose answer neither comes nor fails is said nothing, its button busy, until the browser fails it; the answers live in the page's memory, so a reload, another session's page or another browser says none of them, and so does its guard: one of those can press an action whose post is out, and portal takes both posts; ## C-070: The controller's state text: one writer, one reader, the table of reasons
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026; docs/controlador-plan.md "The state"); promoted in part from P-018
- **Description:** `veredas_core::state` holds a controller's state as structured data (layer 1 with at most one reason of its own state, the start-up phase, the conditions, the maintenance requests) beside the table of reason, phase and maintenance numbers and words as the plan states them. Its one writer spells a state as one line (`docs/state-text.md`); its one reader accepts exactly the texts the writer writes and refuses every other spelling by name and byte, so a text read and written back is the same characters and a state written and read back is the same state (its maintenance requests in the table's order). Each value has one spelling, a TOML value TOML's own parser reads the same; health is derived as the plan says. The readings the plan left open are `docs/questions.md` Q100's.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/state_text.rs | the plan's table and seven examples, read and written back byte for byte; every reason, phase and condition in its order, an absent one unwritten; health; every Unicode character in a string, spelled as the list says and read the same by `toml`; every term of a seeded corpus of 4,000 states read by `toml` as the state holds it; round trips both ways; every one-character deletion, insertion and replacement in 600 of its texts either refused or written back unchanged; spellings the writer never writes refused by name, the messages pinned
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-070.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-071: The Modbus/TCP client: every function of a device's data model, bounded, every answer checked
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: the four fieldbus masters in core, the Modbus TCP client on existing crates; docs/controlador-plan.md "Networks and devices"); promoted in part from P-018
- **Description:** `veredas_core::fieldbus::modbus::Client` (behind core's feature `modbus`, off by default) speaks ten public functions of a Modbus/TCP device's data model (the four reads, a coil or a register written alone or several at once, a register masked, registers read and written in one request; Read FIFO Queue, 0x18, is not spoken) in the standard's frames. It refuses a request outside the standard's bounds before anything is sent, bounds connecting and every answer in time, and takes an answer only when it answers its request (transaction, unit, function, quantity, a write's echo); otherwise it closes the connection, so a late answer is never read as another request's, and the next call connects again. A device's exception keeps the connection. A write says what the device may have done: never sent, not done (an exception the standard gives for a request not carried out: an illegal function, address or value, a device busy, a gateway with no path), or unconfirmed (no answer taken, or an exception that does not say it was not done: a device failure, an acknowledge, a gateway's target silent, a code the standard does not name). Met against the bench's Modbus server (C-110) from the test-only tool `tools/mestres`, as `docs/questions.md` Q101 takes it (`docs/core-plan.md`, "The fieldbus masters").
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/modbus_client.rs, tools/mestres/tests/modbus.rs | over real TCP, a peer recording the bytes: every function's request and answer the specification's own example frames, byte for byte; exceptions kept apart and the connection kept; every exception code a byte holds (0 to 255) on each of the six writes, refused only for the standard's five "not carried out" codes and otherwise unconfirmed, and on each of the four reads always the read's no, one connection throughout; the feature off by default, as cargo reads the manifest; a silent device ended at the bound for a read and unconfirmed for a write, the connection closed and the next call reconnecting; a late answer never read as the next; a closed and a cut link; nineteen ways an answer does not answer (transaction, unit, function, quantity of words and of bytes of bits, every echo, a coil neither on nor off, and an exception for another transaction, unit or function on a write and on a read), each closing the connection; every bound of every function with a quantity (none, one past its limit, a range past address 65535) refused before a connection with its exact reason, and every limit taken at the last addresses; a refused port and a full listen queue bounded; drop and disconnect closing; the unit carried; against the bench's Modbus server, a device written apart from the client: every function reading what the plant side set and writing what it then holds (the mask's arithmetic, a read-write's overlap, the largest register requests at a table's end), the server's own record of each request the specification's bytes with the transaction counting from 0, an address past a table and a unit not played answered as the device's no with the connection kept and nothing written, a write to that unit unconfirmed by its gateway's exception, a slow device landing a write the client calls unconfirmed and a silent one not landing it, a restarted device ending the call and the next connecting again, a stopped one never connected
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-071.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-150: orcl-std — ORCL's standard control library, carried from Veredas 1
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "From Veredas 1 crates to Veredas 2"; docs/orcl/README.md); promoted from P-003 (the ORCL crates)
- **Description:** `crates/orcl-std`, version `2.0.0-alpha.1`, is the library every ORCL program builds on, as Veredas 1 left it (`docs/orcl/README.md`, "What it absorbs from Veredas 1"): `no_std`, `unsafe` forbidden, the panic lints denied. The scan model: a `Scan` carries the scan's time, read once, its period and its cycle number; a `Program` scans copyable inputs into copyable outputs (a program whose inputs or outputs are not `Copy` does not compile, whatever other traits they have, and nothing beyond `Copy` and `Default` is asked, neither crossing threads nor living for ever) and declares safe outputs, all-default unless it says otherwise. Every timer takes its time from the scan's time, never from its cycle number or its period: whenever it measures, its elapsed time is the scan's time less its start, so a scan whose time goes back (which the runtime's own clocks never give) measures less and one before the start nothing, and nothing panics, a preset time as long as `Duration` allows included; each timer is judged on every scan against the `pt` that scan gives, as the counter against its preset, so a `pt` lowered below the elapsed time switches it at once, one raised past it while it measures turns an on-delay off again, and an off-delay that has switched off holds its elapsed time at the `pt` it switched off at; and each block's `q()` reads what its last scan returned. The on-delay `Ton` turns on once its input has been on for `pt`, exactly at `pt`, its elapsed time capped at `pt`, and turns off and restarts from zero with the input; the off-delay `Tof` holds on for `pt` after its input drops, its elapsed time then held at `pt`; the pulse `Tp` gives one pulse of exactly `pt` from a rising edge whatever the input does, ignores a retrigger during it, and once its pulse has ended stays off until the next rising edge, whatever its `pt` or its time then does (one pulse per rising edge, `docs/questions.md` Q229, ruled on 2026-11-25), holding its elapsed time at that scan's `pt` while the input stays on and resetting once it is off, on the scan the pulse ends if the input is off by then. The edges: `RTrig` on for exactly one scan per rising edge, every one of them, a signal already on at the first scan counting as one; `FTrig` on for one scan per falling edge, every one of them, never at start. The up counter `Ctu` counts rising edges, a held input once (a reset released with the input high counts nothing), reset winning over counting, saturating at its top instead of overflowing, `q` on while the count reaches the preset, judged on every scan against the preset that scan is given (a preset of zero or below at once, a reset on that scan or not, a preset raised above the count turning it off, and at its top). `Bits` reads and writes bit `n` of `u8` to `u64`, a bit written with the value it holds staying as it was, an index past the width reading false and writing nothing. Function blocks (timers, edges, the counter) are neither `Copy` nor `Clone`, so scanning a copy does not compile (ORCL's O14). It states the language's own version, ORCL 1, as `LANGUAGE`, a `u16`, a number apart from the crate's SemVer (`docs/questions.md` Q220, ruled on 2026-11-25). The crate's root states `no_std`, `forbid(unsafe_code)` and the six denied lints, and nothing that undoes them; cargo reports it depending on nothing but a dev-dependency for its tests and its library's root as `src/lib.rs`, and no source file brings in another crate, pulls in a file from elsewhere, or builds code apart for its tests outside the unit tests' own modules: read as the compiler reads them, word by word: `cfg` and `cfg_attr` appear in no source but at the five places the crate needs them, and `path`, `include`, `extern` and `env` in none; no source holds a panic source (`unwrap`, `expect` but as a lint attribute, `panic!` and its kin, `assert!` and its kin in the library's code) or debug-only code, no test function stands outside the unit tests' own modules, and those modules hold no `impl`, `trait` or macro that could make the test build differ. The list of types O13 forbids is not carried while law L7 refuses a `clippy.toml`; the architect ruled on 2026-11-25 that the law treat it as a tool's file (`docs/questions.md` Q225), and the list comes once it does.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/orcl-std/src/timers.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/edges.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/counters.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/bits.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/lib.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/scan.rs, crates/orcl-std/src/o14.rs, crates/orcl-std/tests/blocks.rs | `cargo metadata` read for every target of the workspace named `orcl_std` or `blocks`, which must be exactly orcl-std's library (of kind `lib`) at its `src/lib.rs` and its test target (of kind `test`) at its `tests/blocks.rs`, and orcl-std's own targets exactly those two (no build script), then `cargo test -p orcl-std --lib --test blocks`, then `cargo test -p orcl-std --doc`, then, with overflow checks on (`CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_OVERFLOW_CHECKS=true`), `cargo nextest run -p orcl-std --release --test blocks --run-ignored only --no-tests fail -E 'test(=the_counter_counts_to_its_top_and_saturates_there_on_the_build_programs_link)'` (2^31 rising edges, under a second in release; named so that an empty run fails), then `cargo clippy -p orcl-std --all-targets -- -D warnings`, then `cargo build -p orcl-std --target x86_64-unknown-none`, one command (its test targets named and their files checked from outside every target, so a manifest that switched one off or pointed it at another file fails it) whose exit code is the verdict (the panic lints are clippy's, which no other run of the suite calls; the build for a target with no standard library proves `no_std` for every file, the target installed through rustup): Veredas 1's ten tests (each timer's exact switching times, an interrupted on-delay restarting from zero, a retriggered pulse, both edges over a five-scan trace, counting with a held input and a reset, the counter saturating at `i32::MAX`, bits set and cleared, an index past the width); then every timer still in its old state 1 ms before `pt` and switched at it, and switched on the same scan at a `pt` of zero, every timer started and read on scans whose time disagrees with their cycle number, an off-delay holding for `pt` after a second drop, scanned late with its elapsed time capped at `pt` and held there, a pulse triggered for one scan lasting exactly `pt`, its elapsed time held at `pt` while the input stays on, every block's `q()` against its scans, a rising edge from a signal on at the first scan, the reset beating a rising edge in the same scan and a released reset counting nothing, `q` off below the preset, on from it and past it, on at a preset of zero or below, and on at `i32::MAX`, every width's top bit read and written and nothing past it by `with_bit` or `set_bit`, a program's safe outputs all-default and one that declares its own, the crate root compared line by line with what it must hold, so nothing is commented out, allowed back or brought in; a bit written with the value it holds on every width; a reset released low counting the next edge; a pulse of zero length off at once and an edge on the scan a pulse ends starting nothing; six falling edges; every timer held until a preset time of 100.5 ms; the counter's `q()` below its preset; reads far past the width; the crate's version; a pulse ending with its input off reset on that scan and the next edge pulsing again; the counter's `q` following a preset raised and lowered; six rising edges; every file of `src/` read; a program on an `f32`, on a structure that is only `Copy` and `Default`, and on a borrowed value bound to its thread, which compiles; `cargo metadata` reporting no normal, build or target dependency and the library's root `src/lib.rs`; a reset at a preset of zero or below leaving `q` on; a set bit cleared below the top on every width; the counter at `i32::MIN`; every timer switching at a preset time of 100 000 500 ns and not a nanosecond before; every timer's `q()` mid-delay and a fresh off-delay's; an on-delay and an off-delay restarting from their new start after they had switched; the unit tests' interrupted on-delay, retriggered pulse, off-delay reset and held count repeated on the build programs link; a program on a value that is `Copy` and `Default` and nothing else, scanned and asked its safe outputs through a generic runner and directly; an edge's `q()` off again on the scan after its edge, and a pulse's after a pulse that ended with its input off; and `compile_fail` doctests: for each of the six blocks scanning a copy and a clone, and asked for through a `Clone` bound beside a `Default` bound that compiles, and a program with inputs or outputs that are a `Vec`, everything but `Copy`, each beside a twin that compiles, since stable rustdoc does not check the error code a `compile_fail` names, so an unrelated error cannot keep one green; the sources read once as the compiler reads them (one token after another; comments taken out, block comments nested; strings, raw, byte and C strings and characters emptied; lifetimes and labels kept whole; the unit tests' module bounded by its braces), that reading checked on a sample holding each way found to hide code or words from it, and by it: none of `extern`, `debugger_visualizer`, `path`, `include` and its kin, `env`, `option_env`, `unwrap`, `unwrap_err`, `unwrap_unchecked`, `expect_err`, `debug_assertions`, `todo`, `unimplemented`, `unreachable` or `debug_assert` and its kin anywhere; `expect` only as a lint attribute; `panic` only among the root's denied lints; no assertion in the library's code; `cfg_attr` only in the root's `no_std` switch and `cfg` only in each unit tests' module's `cfg(test)`, however spaced, standing on `mod tests`, with no other word starting with `cfg` (`cfg_select!` among them); `test` in the library's code only in that switch; `macro_rules` only for `impl_bits!`, every fragment of which (`$name:kind` with one of Rust's fragment kinds; `$crate` and a bound are none) is a type and which is invoked once on the four widths, whatever its delimiter; a shebang line dropped as rustc drops it, an inner attribute kept; and no `impl`, `trait`, `macro_rules` or `macro_use` in a unit tests' module; every timer's output and elapsed time on scans whose time goes back mid-delay, after switching and before its start, and with its input changing on such a scan; every timer on a period that changes and on a period of zero, switching at `pt`; every timer at the longest preset time, waiting to the end of time, and started past zero; every timer on a `pt` lowered and raised, mid-delay and after switching, raised both below and past its elapsed time, its output, its `q()` and its elapsed time read on each; a `pt` lowered to zero mid-delay and mid-pulse, and an off-delay dropped at a zero `pt` staying off when it is raised again, its input off; a timer's `q()` and elapsed time at a `pt` of zero; a `pt` raised while the timer still measures, an on-delay waiting to it and a pulse lasting to it, its input on and then off; an off-delay lowered to zero mid-delay, off at once and off still when its `pt` is raised; the counter's first three edges counted one by one at a preset of `i32::MAX`; a rising edge's input held four scans and then three giving one pulse each, and a falling edge's held low from the start giving none, then one pulse per drop, `q()` read on each scan; a pulse reset with its input off (dropped mid-pulse, or after a held end) staying off when its `pt` is raised or its time goes back, its elapsed time read mid-pulse with its input off, and Veredas 1's retrigger, held end and new press repeated on the build programs link; a bit read and written beside other set bits on every width; an off-delay with its input held on for 500 ms on and holding nothing elapsed throughout, then on for `pt` after the drop; a reset with the count input low clearing a count of 3; an on-delay that switched on, stepped back before its start, off; two scans at one instant each judged (an on-delay's input off, a pulse's edge); an edge on the scan a pulse ends, then held, starting nothing; a rising edge's signal off at its first scan, no edge; the counter's preset raised, lowered and set to zero, and a reset, while its count input is held high; an edge on a pulse's end scan that comes after `pt` starting nothing; a read at bit 1999; two edges, two counters and two pulses of one kind side by side, each keeping its own memory; a pulse released on the scan it ends, reset then, and one pulse for each of ten rises of a square wave;
