## C-151: orcl-runtime — the engine of a scan: one cyclic task on the clock its host hands it
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-controlador: the softPLC"; docs/controlador-plan.md "Faults"; docs/orcl/README.md "The engine of a scan"); promoted from P-003 (the deterministic scan)
- **Description:** `crates/orcl-runtime`, version `2.0.0-alpha.1`, runs one `Program` as a cyclic task on a clock its host hands in, with no operating-system code: virtual time (`VirtualClock`: still until moved, a wait moving it to the deadline at once, never back, clones sharing one time) or a target's monotonic clock (`MonoClock`: std's `Instant`, never the system time a time setting moves; a wait never returning before its deadline, and its reading keeping the wall's pace, never falling behind it). A task refuses a period of zero by name. Scan `k` is due at `k` periods from the clock's zero, to the nanosecond whatever the period, and starts there, whatever the scans before it took or when the task was first stepped; its inputs are read once at its start, the program is handed that snapshot, the scan's time and its outputs as the last scan left them, and its outputs are written once at its end. A scan ends when its outputs are handed to the plant: its length runs from its start to that moment, the program's own run included, and the time-error rule and the deadlines it skipped are judged at that same moment; the plant's own time to take the outputs is not the scan's. A deadline found passed when the task is stepped again, whether it passed during the plant's write or while the host was away, runs late, its lateness counted, and every other deadline passed by the end of that late scan is skipped and counted, never caught up. A scan longer than its period is a time error, counted and reported with the scan, its outputs still written; a scan of exactly the period is none; the rule measures a scan's length alone, never how late it started. A deadline that passed while a scan ran is skipped and counted, never caught up, and one that falls exactly at the scan's end runs then. A scan longer than twice its period faults the task (a watchdog fault naming the scan's length, at the scan's start): that scan's own outputs are never written, the safe outputs are, and from then on every scan reads its inputs and writes the safe outputs without scanning the program, so the plant's exchange goes on; the faulting scan is a time error, no later one is, nor an overrun, and the fault keeps the cause and the date of the scan that raised it. The rule is measured when a scan ends; a scan that never ends is the host's watchdog's to catch. The statistics count scans, overruns and skipped cycles, and a scan's lateness against its deadline and its length, with their maxima and means. The same program, inputs and times give the same scans. One cyclic task only: several tasks, as the execution model rules them (`docs/controlador/execution-model.md`, `docs/questions.md` Q223 with Q33), are not built yet.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/orcl-runtime/tests/model.rs, crates/orcl-runtime/tests/scan_cycle.rs, crates/orcl-runtime/tests/conveyor.rs, crates/orcl-runtime/tests/mono_clock.rs | `cargo test -p orcl-runtime --test model --test scan_cycle --test conveyor --test mono_clock`. A model of this clause, written apart from the engine in whole nanoseconds, run in lockstep with it over 4000 random sequences of 50 steps, 40 of 2000, 40 more of 2000 whose scans are held to twice the period so the task never faults, and 100 of 2000 held so until a step drawn between the 60th and the 1960th, after a drawn streak of up to 40 time errors, where the task faults (periods from 1 µs to 2 s; host delays, scan lengths and write lengths drawn to land on deadlines, a nanosecond either side of them and at one and two periods, and one step in twenty the host away for up to a million periods, and a wait that has to wait waking past its deadline by a nanosecond, part of a period or more, as a target's does under load), every report, every statistic with its maximum, total and mean, the next cycle and deadline, the clock, the scan the program is handed and every read and write by time and value compared after every step, with a program whose output depends on the output image it is handed and whose safe outputs depend on its own state; a host away 300 periods and then 2^33 periods, the late scan after each run as it is (its cycle, its time, no time error, still running) with its lateness counted, and every deadline it skipped counted; the draws counted to reach every case the clause names at least 20 times (a start late by under a millisecond, a faulted late scan, a scan and a faulting scan ending on a deadline, a faulted scan skipping, a period of a second or more, a slow write across a deadline, a fault past the 1000th scan, after more than 20 time errors, after more than 10 in a row and after 100 skipped deadlines, a running and a faulted scan each late by 1000 periods or more, and a scan started by a wait that woke past its deadline); an 8 ms program on a 10 ms period on a clock whose wait wakes 3 ms and 13 ms late, the scan started then, 8 ms long, no time error and no fault, its lateness 3 and 13 ms, the program handed its start; 70 000 scans of one and a half periods, each a time error and a skip, counted past 16 bits, the mean and longest length exact, the task still running. Then, on virtual time: a zero period refused with its text; 100 scans of 10 ms before 1 s, each on its exact deadline with 3 ms of work, reading what the one before wrote, every read and write logged at its exact time, the statistics exact; a scan of exactly one period; a 15 ms scan skipping one deadline and a 20 ms scan (twice the period, the limit itself) skipping one and ending on the next, which runs at once; the longest scan's length and the mean pinned where lengths differ; a scan of 20 ms and 1 ns faulting, every write that reached the plant listed (one per scan, at its end, the program's own safe outputs, which no default gives, from the faulting scan on), the deadlines it overran skipped and the program scanned no more; a host back late, the scan handed its start and not its deadline, its lateness counted with its maximum, total and mean; a fault dated at a late start; a program that itself runs 15 ms (a time error, its outputs written at its end) or 25 ms (the fault, the faulting scan a time error); a scan started 8 ms late running 5 ms (no time error) and one started 9 ms late running 15 ms (a time error, no fault, its own outputs written); a faulted scan started late reading at its start with its lateness counted; a faulted scan of 25 ms neither a time error nor an overrun, the fault keeping its first date and length; a program that writes its output on even cycles alone, the odd ones writing what it left, and one that writes on a first scan that is a time error, the next writing what it left; a host back 25 ms late (no time error, its lateness counted whole); a scan of 10 ms and 1 ns (a time error); a later faulted scan of 30 ms leaving the fault's 25 ms; the faulting and faulted scans' lengths in the maximum, total and mean; a virtual wait of 10 s taking no time on the wall; periods of 2.5 ms, 500 µs and 333 333 ns each on its deadlines; a first step at 5 ms running scan 0 late and scan 1 at 10 ms; a mean kept to the nanosecond; the faulted scan's 30 ms the longest in the statistics; the program handed exactly the scan the engine reports, late and skipped scans included; periods of 1, 1.5 and 5 s on their deadlines; 2000 scans of 333 333 ns skipping nothing; a first step at 25 ms running scan 0 late and skipping two; a scan started 9 ms late and running 15 ms skipping the two deadlines passed while it ran, its lateness 9 ms, the next scan on its deadline at 40 ms; a late faulting scan a time error and an overrun, its skips judged at its end; cycles past 2^32 each on its deadline; a period of zero refused naming its task (`fast`); a plant whose write takes 1 ms after a scan of exactly 10 ms (no time error, the next deadline run 1 ms late, nothing skipped) and after one of 9.5 ms (the next deadline run at 10.5 ms); with a slow write, the statistics' length and overruns, a 19.5 ms scan no fault and a 25 ms one a fault naming 25 ms; lateness late twice (3 ms maximum, 5 total, 1.25 mean); a host back 25 ms late and a write spanning two deadlines, each running the first late and skipping the others passed by that scan's end, every cycle run or counted; two runs alike; Veredas 1's conveyor program and its five tests, then its 22 s against its demo plant on the engine, every change of state, of the alarm and of the count on its exact scan; on a target's clock, judged against a wall clock started before it as well as against its own reading, waits of 50 µs to 100 ms never early and scans never before their deadlines, every cycle run or counted as skipped; its reading, taken after the wall's and after 100 µs of spinning past a wait, never behind the wall by more than the wall's head start; and a program spinning 5 ms on a 2 ms period measured at 5 ms or more, a time error and a fault; and, read in the source since no test can step the system's clock without privileges, every file of `src/`, its comments aside, none naming `SystemTime` or `UNIX_EPOCH` or taking a file in from elsewhere (`#[path]`, `include!` and its kin), and `MonoClock` started at `Instant::now()` and read as `self.start.elapsed()`, its wait judging the deadline on that reading; the root `no_std` with std brought in by name and no macro imported wholesale, so every use of std is written out (a print to the terminal among them), and std used only at the paths the engine needs (`Rc`, `Instant`, the wait's `thread::sleep`), the rest from `core`, so no thread started and no file, socket, environment or process reached; and, by cargo's own report (`cargo tree`, every target, every feature), the engine's normal and build dependencies exactly orcl-std and its root forbidding unsafe code, since another crate or unsafe code could reach the system's clock or the operating system without naming either
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-151.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-110: The Modbus server: a device's four tables on a real TCP socket
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: bench devices for the four fieldbuses, a Modbus server among them; P-020; docs/maquinar-plan.md "P-020: the bench devices, as they land")
- **Description:** `modbus::start` serves a `ModbusConfig` (TOML, every optional key defaulted, a misspelled key refused; a bench file's `[[modbus]]`, C-033) as a Modbus/TCP server on its own thread and runtime, the bench's own code: four tables sized by the file (at most 65536 addresses; absent, reaching the last point) and named points in them (a bool in a bit table; a `u16`, `i16`, `u32`, `i32` or `f32` in a register table, the two-register ones in either word order), refused by name before anything binds when a point has no name or another's, a type or a word order it cannot take, an address past its table or over another point, or a value that does not parse; it answers function codes 01 to 06, 0F, 10, 16 and 17 as the protocol specification's worked examples answer, byte for byte, each request the moment it is read and in its order (pipelined, split across segments, several connections at once), every other function with exception 01, a length, quantity, byte count or coil value the function does not take with 03 before an address past its table with 02 (a refused request writes nothing), a unit other than the one it plays with 0B, 0 and 255 included (none configured, every unit is the device, as a client addressing a device on TCP directly with 255 expects), a frame of another protocol identifier dropped unanswered and a length no frame has closing the connection; the plant side reads and sets every table by point (typed or as text) and by address, inputs included, each side reading next what the other wrote; its knobs hold every answer for a delay (a connection's pipelined answers one after another; a client that closes during it, a half-close included, taken as gone), withhold every answer not yet handed to its socket (a request read while silent, or an answer waiting out its delay when silence began, even for a moment, never sent later, nor the requests that begin in what was read ahead during that delay, while one read ahead during an earlier delay silence did not touch is judged as it comes; silence set under the lock an answer's count takes, so no write begins once the knob returns; one whose write had begun goes out), and close every open connection (those it serves, those still in its listen queue, one blocked on a client that reads nothing; returning once they are, the server listening on), a connection limit closing one more at once, a client that closed and connects again at once not refused however many connections are busy; an answer counted, and shown in its own exchange, as its write begins, and taken back when the write fails or a drop cuts it (a withheld one, or one whose connection was dropped or whose client closed or reset during its delay whatever it pipelined behind (read ahead up to exactly 64 KiB, a request past it judged by silence when it is read, and what it pipelined neither done nor shown; past 64 KiB its close is seen only by a write that fails: up to two delays, the answer counted, the next request done), counted as none, as unanswered when silence had withheld it, that connection ended and its place under the limit freed at once; the count is of answers handed to the socket: those a drop's reset discards from the socket's buffer stay counted), every withheld answer and every refused connection counted in a status that keeps the last hundred exchanges; a plant-side read or write past a table refused whatever its count; stop and drop close every connection and free the port, a taken port refused naming the address. A client library the bench did not write reads and writes every table through it.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/modbus_server.rs | the configuration's defaults and every key read, a misspelled key and an unknown table or type word refused; values per type with their limits; every refusal of a configuration with its exact message, the largest tables and a point at the last address accepted, and a bad value refused before a taken port is reached; the specification's examples for 01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 0F, 10, 16 and the 02 exception byte for byte with the header checked (transaction and unit echoed, protocol 0, the length), what each write left read on the plant side, 17's write before its read (overlapping too); every exception in the protocol's order at each function's limits (2000 bits, 125 registers, 1968 coils, 123 and 121 registers, a coil value, wrong lengths and byte counts, an empty table, the top address not wrapping) with nothing a refused write names landing; 0B for every other unit and every unit served without one; the points' registers as the wire carries them in both word orders, the sizes taken, a NaN read back; `tokio-modbus` reading every table and type, writing by every write function with the plant side seeing it, and the reverse, its exceptions; three requests in one segment and one split byte by byte answered in order and once, five connections at once; another protocol dropped and a length of 0, 1, 255 or 65535 closing, the 254 a frame can hold read whole; a delay held on every answer (two in turn, two pipelined the second after the first), silence never answered and the next answer the next request's, silence set while an answer waits out its delay withholding it, and silence switched on and off inside the delay withholding it too, neither counted as answered, every connection dropped (one waiting out a 30 s delay too) and a new one served, fifty connections dropped straight after their connect returned (closed, never answered) and one whose client reads nothing dropped within a second, an answer dropped with its connection counted as none and its exchange showing none; every point's value read as text, a NaN as `NaN`; a count past every address refused, never overflowing; the last hundred of 150 exchanges kept; refused writes of coils and registers over values none of them carries, nothing changed; a limit of two closing a third at once; stop freeing the port; the reference's drive bench run through the bench file; round 3's pins: an answer taken back when its client resets during the delay, an answer counted as its write begins on a client that reads nothing and taken back by a drop, what was handed to the socket going out after silence began, 03 before 02 for 0F, 10, 16 and 17, 03 for a wrong length on 01, 02, 06, 10 and 17 and a zero quantity on 10 and 17, a refused 05 writing nothing, the delay holding two exceptions, two interleaved delayed answers each in its own exchange; round 4's pins: a client closing the ordinary way during a 500 ms delay gone within 300 ms with nothing counted, the same with a reset, an exception whose client left counted as none, a reconnecting client under a limit of one not refused, 100 switches to silence with a status reader on another thread letting no answer begin; round 5's pins: a client that pipelines a second request and closes, the same resetting, and one resetting alone during an 800 ms delay, each gone within 300 ms with nothing counted and no exchange showing an answer (round 4's reset within 300 ms too); a client that pipelined and closed during a 3 s delay leaving its place under a limit of one to its reconnection, answered; a half-closing client answered without a delay and given nothing under one, counted once; an answer silence withheld counted unanswered when a drop or the client's close then ends its delay, and a client gone without silence counted as neither; an answer whose write waits on a client that reads nothing taken back when the client resets; a client closing and connecting again at once under a limit of one, 500 times between answers and 300 times during a delay, never refused; round 6's pins: two connections' 500 ms delays waited out side by side (both answers within 900 ms); a close or a reset behind 2, 100 and 5000 pipelined requests seen within 300 ms with nothing counted; 6000 requests behind a delay, across the 64 KiB read ahead, answered once each in their order; a write read ahead while silent never done nor answered after silence ended, a request pipelined before silence came and went withheld too, both counted unanswered, a request after the delay answered; a client at a limit of 100 beside 99 busy connections reconnecting 300 times never refused; four clients reconnecting together at a limit of 4 and drops racing 400 reconnections at a limit of one leaving the counts whole; silence that came and went and then the client left counted unanswered; a frame of another protocol and a length no frame has read ahead judged in their turn; 72 000 bytes behind a delay holding the connection past the delay after its close, its answer counted; a write pipelined behind a delayed request neither done nor shown once its client half-closes or closes, and done without a delay; round 7's pins: a client reconnecting at once 300 times beside 299 busy connections at a limit of 300 never refused, no busy answer lost; a write and a read sent after silence ended and its delay passed answered in order, the write done, answered and unanswered 2 and 2; a request read ahead during an earlier delay silence did not touch answered and its write done when silence came and went in a later delay; 6001 requests sent while silent during an 800 ms delay, the 5462 beginning within 64 KiB withheld and the 539 past it answered once silence ended, the last a write done (on the machine's lock); five connections past a limit of one each closed within 300 ms and counted (on the machine's lock).
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-110.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-152: veredas-controlador — the controller's states and the commands that move them, over one cyclic task
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/controlador-plan.md "Start, stop and power", "The commissioning switch", "Faults", "The state", "Rules"); promoted in part from P-003
- **Description:** `crates/veredas-controlador`, version `2.0.0-alpha.1`, a library, holds a controller running one ORCL program as one cyclic task on `orcl-runtime`'s engine (C-151), its state core's structured `ControllerState` (C-070) and its text core's. It comes up from nothing in STOP; after a power cycle, RUN (and a start-up a power cut interrupted) returns through a warm start with no person, and STOP, HOLD and FAULT return as `STOP power_on(was)`; a runtime that restarted while the machine did not comes up in `FAULT internal`. A start, warm or cold, is a person's (`oficina` or `local`) with the commissioning switch armed, from STOP alone: it shows `STARTUP` with its reason and the phase `load`, and the next cycle ends the start-up and runs the program's first scan, the program from its initial values and its clock from zero, the plant handed the target's time; on a production target without real-time scheduling a start, on command or after a power cycle, leaves `STOP realtime_refused`. A stop is free, from a person or from the program itself, from RUN or STARTUP, to `STOP command(source)`, the program ending at once. A scan longer than twice its period faults the whole controller, `FAULT watchdog(task)`; in FAULT the plant's exchange goes on with the program's safe outputs, a stop and a start are refused, and only a person's acknowledge with the switch armed leaves it, to `STOP acknowledged`, from where a start runs the program again from its initial values. Every refusal says why in its text (the program may only stop, the switch is not armed, the command does nothing in this state, a production target without real time) and changes nothing, but a start refused for want of real time, which leaves `STOP realtime_refused`. While no program runs, stopped or faulted before any program ran, the controller reads the inputs and writes the program's safe outputs on the periods of the target's clock alone and at most once in each, the first after a stop or an acknowledge at the first period not before the next cycle and after the last exchange, the program's or its own. The conditions `sim` and `no_realtime` say how it runs, `overruns` counts the time errors since the start, afresh from the moment a start begins, and a stop keeps the run's count, and the health is core's. Where the plan was silent the readings are `docs/questions.md` Q228's, which the architect accepted on 2026-11-25.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-controlador/tests/model.rs, crates/veredas-controlador/tests/states.rs | `cargo test -p veredas-controlador --test model --test states`. A model of this clause, written apart from the controller, run in lockstep with it over 10 000 random sequences of 60 operations and 2000 of 400 that seldom stop, half of these with slow scans that never fault, each on a task period drawn from 1 to 50 ms (every restart on every kind of target; every command from every source, armed or not, in every state; host steps late, clock jumps of a fraction of a period, onto a period of the target's clock or of seconds, reads stalling by up to three periods and at one and two periods and a nanosecond past them), comparing after every operation the whole state with its conditions and count, the health, the program's scans and clock, every refusal whole, its command and the state it names, and every exchange's time and value; the draws counted to reach every command and fault, an idle exchange held back by a late scan's start, an idle exchange past 4.29 s of the target's clock and a count of overruns past eight at least 100 times. Then, on virtual time, against core's own writer: a zero period refused when the controller comes up; a new controller in `STOP`, its health a function check, three exchanges of the safe outputs at 0, 10 and 20 ms; a cold start arriving at 13 ms shown as `STARTUP cold(id=202) load()`, then `RUN` with health normal, the program's scans at 13, 23 and 33 ms on the target's clock and at 0, 10 and 20 ms on its own; a start refused from the program, with the switch off and from STARTUP, each by its text, the state unchanged; a stop with no switch to `STOP command(id=101, source="oficina")`, the safe outputs from the next period, never twice at one instant, a stop in STOP refused, a stop from STARTUP and one from RUN by the program; a start in RUN refused and the program going on; an acknowledge refused in STARTUP and in RUN; a cold start refused from the program and with the switch off, by its text; every refusal but the real-time one leaving the whole state as it was; `no_realtime` and `sim` each on its own; a power-cycled start-up stepped to `RUN`, and on a production target without real time left in `STOP realtime_refused`; the watchdog naming its task (`conveyor`); a stop keeping the overruns; the exchange while no program runs on the target's periods after a program on its own grid, after a stop between periods, after an acknowledge, after a refused stop and a stop from STARTUP, and after a faulted task's last exchange; a late step while no program runs (a stop handled at 23 ms, a first step at 13 ms, an idle read of 15 ms, a scan of 15 ms before a stop) waiting for the target's next period; a program started on a period other than zero never exchanging twice at its last scan; a warm start after a stop and after an acknowledge running the program from its initial values and its clock from zero; two time errors counted; a start from every kind of STOP; the crash's FAULT refusing a stop and a start; a start after a fault with overruns showing `STARTUP cold(id=202) load()` and a start stopped from STARTUP leaving no count; refusals in the watchdog's FAULT leaving the faulted program and its exchange on its own periods; on a production target without real time, the program's start and a start with the switch off refused for their own reasons first; an acknowledge refused in RUN restarting nothing; a stop from `local`; the program's clock from zero after an acknowledge; the conditions after a power cycle (`STOP power_on(id=102, was="STOP") no_realtime()`, then `RUN no_realtime()`) and after a crash on the simulator; on a production target without real time, STOP, HOLD and FAULT back as `STOP power_on(was)`, a crash as `FAULT internal` with health failure, and a start in it refused by the state; refusals leaving the exchange while no program runs on its periods; a scan stalled 25 ms on a 10 ms period giving `FAULT watchdog(id=501, task="main") overruns(count=1)`, health failure, the exchange going on with the safe outputs, stop, start, the program's acknowledge and an acknowledge with the switch off refused, a person's acknowledge to `STOP acknowledged(id=103) overruns(count=1)`, a second acknowledge refused, then a cold start to `RUN` with the program from its initial values; after a power cycle `RUN` and `STARTUP` back through `STARTUP warm(id=201) load()` to `RUN`, and `STOP`, `HOLD` and `FAULT` as `STOP power_on(id=102, was="...")`; a runtime's own restart as `FAULT internal(id=507)` exchanging the safe outputs until acknowledged; a production target without real time refusing a start with its text, `STOP realtime_refused(id=105)`, after a power cycle too; the simulator as `STOP sim() no_realtime()` and `RUN sim() no_realtime()`, health a function check; a 15 ms scan as `RUN overruns(count=1)`, health normal; a late scan starting on a period of the target's clock and taking no time, then a stop, a faulted task's late exchange on one, then an acknowledge, and a program started off the grid scanning late on one, then a stop, each followed by the idle exchange at the next period, never at the instant just served

## C-153: orcl-interp — a program in the shape of rustc's MIR, run one instruction at a time on memory laid out at load
- **Date:** 2026-11-25
- **Source:** user (docs/orcl/README.md "The interpreter"; docs/orcl/interpreter.md; docs/questions.md Q227, Q223 point 4, Q230); promoted in part from P-003
- **Description:** `crates/orcl-interp`, version `2.0.0-alpha.1`, depending on nothing (`orcl-std` for its tests alone), loads a `Program` (its types, its functions as MIR has them at opt-level 0, the source files its locations name) with a region of memory for each type the host names, and refuses at load, by a reason of its own, a program it cannot lay out or whose instructions do not fit their types, every reason it gives met by a test but five no program can meet (more than 2^32 types, functions, blocks or statements, and a variant's field of what is no enum, which the downcast before it refuses); among them: an index to no type, function, local or block; a type that contains itself with no reference between; an enum's discriminants that clash or fall outside their type; a size past the machine's reach; a function with no block or more parameters than locals; a value of another type than its place, a constant whose slots are not its type's, a field of what is no structure, an index that is no `usize`, a constant index past the array, a comparison into what is no `bool`, a unary operation on what it does not take, a switch's value outside its operand's type, a call with too many or too few arguments, a core operation called with types it does not take; and a function that reaches itself through its calls, its cycle named (`docs/questions.md` Q230, taken meanwhile). A loaded program runs as MIR means it: an entry started with references to the host's regions and values, run one instruction (a statement or a terminator) a step, for a budget of steps, or to its return; `+ - *` wrap, a shift takes its amount modulo the width, `/` and `%` fail on a zero divisor and on the least value over minus one, the checked forms give the wrapped value and whether it overflowed, `!` and a wrapping `-`, Rust's `as` between `bool`, the integers and the floats (a float to an integer toward zero, saturating, NaN as zero), comparisons, the floats' arithmetic, each as the native type gives it; structures, arrays and enum variants built from their parts, an enum's variant writing its own fields and leaving the rest unwritten, its discriminant read and switched on, its fields read as that variant; a reference writing its caller's place and a host's region; calls to the program's functions and to the operations of the closed list (Q227), which give what core's functions give: saturating and wrapping `+ - *`, `checked_shl` and `checked_shr`, `Ord::min` and `max` on every integer type, `Duration::from_millis`, `from_secs`, `saturating_sub`, `min`, `max` and its six comparisons through references, `Option::get_or_insert`, `is_none` and `is_some`, and `Default::default` of a scalar or a `Duration`. A failed assertion, a division's check, an index past the end, code marked unreachable and a scalar an operation reads before anything wrote it end the run with a `ProgramError` naming the function, the block, the statement or the terminator, and the source location; the machine stays at that instruction, every step gives the same error, nothing starts over it until an abort, and memory keeps what was written before it. A host's misuse is a `HostError` (a busy machine, no such function or region, arguments that are not the entry's parameters, slots that are not a value of the region's type). The machine's whole state is data: stopped at any instruction, a run goes on to the same result, memory and count of instructions; a copy of a machine, taken at any instruction or idle, is a machine of its own standing where the original stood, with the same room in every buffer, and goes on alike, allocating nothing; a budget of zero runs nothing and says whether an entry stands, or gives a failed one's error; the same program and arguments give the same memory, result and count every time; and once a program is loaded nothing is allocated while entries run, fail or are stopped midway, in the machine or in a copy of it. The crate's root forbids `unsafe` code and denies clippy's panic lints, and no source allows them back. Cargo reports it depending on nothing, on every target and with every feature. The program file a `Program` travels in is C-154's. Not yet: the front end, the engine of a scan running an interpreted program, and the conformance suite (`docs/orcl/interpreter.md`, "Built in this order").
- **Verification:** automated → crates/orcl-interp/tests/ops.rs, crates/orcl-interp/tests/machine.rs, crates/orcl-interp/tests/load.rs, crates/orcl-interp/tests/core_ops.rs, crates/orcl-interp/tests/blocks.rs, crates/orcl-interp/tests/alloc.rs, crates/orcl-interp/tests/root.rs, crates/orcl-interp/tests/common/mod.rs | `cargo test -p orcl-interp --test ops --test machine --test load --test core_ops --test blocks --test alloc --test root && cargo clippy -p orcl-interp --all-targets -- -D warnings`, one command whose exit code is the verdict: every binary operation on every integer type (and every shift amount on every right-hand type) at its bounds, their neighbours, small values and values drawn at random, and every pair of 8-bit values, against the native type's own operation; the checked forms likewise; `!`, `-` and every cast between integers; `bool`'s operations, and `bool` as every integer type; `-` on `f32`, every cast between the floats, and integers whose nearest float a second rounding would miss; the floats' arithmetic, comparisons and casts at zero, minus zero, the extremes, the infinities and NaN; a loop with its checked additions run for several lengths, its instructions counted, and a call returning its result; an overflow three blocks in, named, the machine standing there; a run stopped after every budget from 1 to 333 steps and a copy of it, each going on to the same result, memory and count, and one position a step; a copy taken at every instruction of a run, at every depth, finished alike, and copies of an idle machine and of one just started running a chain of calls five deep, the original untouched; a budget of zero idle, running and failed; a second start refused while an entry runs inside a call, which goes on undisturbed, and too many or too few arguments, a value of another type and a region of another type refused; slots that are no value of a region's type (a `None` with a payload, no such variant, a second's nanoseconds); a caller's own local written through a reference, and a callee indexing its own array by its own local; a function calling a small then a large callee, both laid out; a local unwritten again at every start and every call; references to a caller's place and to a host's region, and each `HostError`; a structure, an array and an enum built, indexed, switched on and read, and an index past the end; an enum variant's unwritten slots; unreachable code and an unwritten value; three runs alike; every refusal of the loader above, a diamond of calls and a type that reaches itself through a reference loaded; every reason the loader gives met by a test, a scan of its source failing for a reason no test meets; every core operation of a sample refused with each argument and its destination in turn of another type, and with one argument too many; a nesting of 258 types refused and of 200 loaded; a frame, a stack and two regions past the machine's reach; every operation of the list against core's on every integer type and on durations and options, a checked shift's `None` after a `Some` into one place leaving no payload, and each default compared bit for bit; `Ton::scan` and `RTrig::scan` lowered by hand against `orcl-std`'s over 24 000 random scans and every 10-scan trace; an allocator counting each thread's own allocations while 2 000 entries run, 100 fail three calls down and 100 stop midway, and while every kind of instruction and every operation of the list run, in the machine and in a copy of it, zero; the crate's root compared line by line, every source read for an attribute that allows a lint back; and `cargo tree` on the crate's normal and build dependencies, on every target and with every feature, listing the crate alone.
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-153.md
- **Status:** pending

## C-154: orcl-interp's program file — `<name>.orclb`, its header naming its format, its language and its nightly
- **Date:** 2026-11-25
- **Source:** user (docs/orcl/interpreter.md "The program file"; docs/controlador/files.md "The compiled program"; docs/questions.md Q220, Q222, Q224); promoted in part from P-003
- **Description:** `orcl_interp::file` writes a `Program` under a `Header` (the language's version, the nightly that lowered it, the source revision its sender gives) as a program file in a codec of its own, little-endian, leaning on no crate's encoding: the magic `ORCLB\0`, the format's version (1), the language's, the header's two texts, then the program's source files, its types and its functions, every kind of type, projection, operand, scalar, value, terminator, callee, check and core operation of the program model; and `read` gives back the same header and program, which writes again to the same bytes and runs as the one written. A reader refuses a file that does not start with the magic, then one of another format, naming the format it found, or of a language this interpreter does not run (`LANGUAGES`: ORCL 1, which holds the version `orcl-std` states), naming the version it found, before reading anything past those numbers; and a malformed file at the byte where it stops making sense: cut short, a tag or a value no program has (an integer outside its type, a `bool` neither 0 nor 1), a count past the file's end (so a damaged count asks for no more memory than the file holds), text that is not UTF-8, or bytes past the program's end; never with a panic. Every file it accepts writes back to the very same bytes. Two programs' bytes are pinned, a small one written out by hand and one holding every kind against a fixture of the crate's tests, so no code of the format drifts unseen. Format 1 holds no table of entries: a host finds an entry by its function's source path, and its parameters' types are the function's own; a table naming the entries and the functions that build their default and safe values, if the front end needs one, comes with a new format version. The nightly is read and kept, not judged: which nightlies a controller runs (Q222) is controlador's, not yet built. Whether the program read fits its types is the loader's (C-153); a download's integrity is its manifest's (`docs/questions.md` Q36). Distinct from the interpreter's machine (C-153), which loads, checks and runs a `Program` in memory: this clause holds that program's bytes alone, and a kind C-153's model gains comes here with the format's version. Distinct from core's header reader (C-045), which reads the `format` and `version` keys every TOML file of the suite opens with: a program file is binary, not TOML, so outside that reader and law L7 (C-046, `docs/controlador/files.md`); its header is its own, held to the same rule: read before the body, refused by name, never guessed.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/orcl-interp/tests/file.rs, crates/orcl-interp/tests/common/mod.rs | `cargo test -p orcl-interp --test file && cargo clippy -p orcl-interp --all-targets -- -D warnings`, one command whose exit code is the verdict: a program holding every kind of the model (every integer type, a struct, an enum with negative and wide discriminants, an array, references, every projection, every scalar kind including minus zero, a NaN with a payload and the infinities, every binary operation plain and checked, every value, terminator and check, every core operation and every integer type through one of them, names in UTF-8, an empty name) written, read back equal and written again to the same bytes; one small program's bytes pinned to the byte, and the every-kind program's against `tests/every_kind.orclb`, read back to that program; the magic, a format of 2 and languages 0, 2 and 1000 refused as such, the language before the damaged rest, and a format of 2 said whatever its language field holds, and with nothing after it; an integer outside its type and a `bool` of 2 refused at their bytes; an empty program read back; `LANGUAGES` holding `orcl_std::LANGUAGE` and only 1; every truncation of a file refused, at or before its cut, and a byte past its end refused at that byte; every byte damaged three ways either refused or read to a program that writes back to the damaged bytes, both outcomes met; a count past the end and text that is not UTF-8 refused at their bytes; and a program read from its file running to the same results, memory and count as the one written.
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-154.md
- **Status:** pending

## C-130: veredas-aranha — the network scanner
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-aranha: the network scanner"; the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026; promoted from P-005)
- **Description:** `crates/veredas-aranha` tells who is on an industrial Ethernet segment, how each device names itself and who is cabled to whom, with the evidence behind every value, and what changed since a baseline, for setup and fault-finding only (`docs/aranha-plan.md` maps what it absorbs from Veredas 1's `veredas-aranha` and its plan, what it leaves, and the phases: the discovery codecs, the IO codecs, the wire and listening against maquinar's PROFINET device, asking, the topology with the scan file and its diff, the executable, then deep identity, the IP-layer probes, writes and Windows). A library first: cargo, asked what it would build, names exactly one library target (`lib`/`rlib`), at most one binary named `veredas-aranha`, and tests. Light, since the bench depends on it for the codecs: what a crate depending on it compiles (its normal and build dependencies with every feature on, on every platform, transitively, walked by package id) holds no database (`duckdb`, `libduckdb`), no web stack (`axum`, `hyper`, `tower-http`, `warp`, `actix`, `rocket`), no UI crate (`eframe`, `egui`, `winit`, `iced`, `slint`, `gtk`, `cxx-qt`, `qmetaobject`), and no member of the workspace but core (by the workspace's own list, whatever a member's name); its dev-dependencies are its tests' business. Its PROFINET codecs (`src/profinet/`, flat files, no folder and no link, with no `cfg` but `cfg(test)`) build as a crate of their own whose only dependencies are `serde` and `thiserror`, which core already has, every target and its doctests, and the rest of aranha, its tests included (every target being at a path cargo finds by itself, so a copy of its folders holds it), builds on them as that other crate (its dependencies, their kinds and platforms, and its features those of aranha's manifest), in the builds checked: every feature on, the dev and release profiles, x86-64 Linux and x86-64 Windows (GNU). The codecs hold no `cfg` but `cfg(test)`, and alone, their unit tests with them, build for wasm32 (32-bit, another architecture), for powerpc (32-bit, big-endian, without 64-bit atomics) and for i686 (32-bit x86, a 64-bit integer aligned to four bytes), so code bound to an architecture, a pointer width, an endianness, 64-bit atomics or an alignment does not hide in them without a `cfg` either; the rest's side is proven in those builds, a reach behind a `cfg` none of them takes (another architecture, another Windows toolchain, a target feature, a panic strategy) being outside the proof, stated. So their move into core (P-018) is a move. It compiles in no file from outside its folder: every cargo the checks run starts from the workspace's root, as the real build does, and no cargo configuration lies in its folder, under it or between it and the root (which would steer a build from inside and no build from the root); no name under it holds a control character and nothing under it is a link; every file rustc reads for every target, in each build checked, by rustc's own dep-info, lies in it once every link is followed, and is there (a line the dep-info breaks names no file); every target cargo names, by its kind and name, is among those checked; and since an assembler (`.incbin`), a doctest and a proc macro read files that dep-info and the split do not see, no file rustc reads for it, and no file under its folder whatever its name, holds assembly, rustdoc finds no doctest in it in any build checked, and the proc macros its builds and tests use are named, six (displaydoc, serde_derive, thiserror-impl, yoke-derive, zerofrom-derive, zerovec-derive), so a new one is seen and weighed. It names no vendor: every file under its folder is UTF-8 and has a UTF-8 name, and none, by its contents or its name, holds the terms core's reader bans (core's list, held to core's file), read through Unicode's decomposition, with marks (by general category) and default-ignorable code points dropped, and supervisão's table of the Latin letters it leaves whole (held to supervisão's file: every letter of Latin-1 but µ, which NFKD makes Greek's mu, every letter of Latin Extended-A, and three of Extended-B, read as plain letters; other Latin letters, look-alikes from other scripts and small capitals are outside, as for supervisão's reader), or a section sign citing a document, written as the sign, its named entity whatever follows it (HTML reads the legacy name before a letter or digit too), its numeric entity, a Rust, JSON, TOML or byte escape, or percent-encoded (its UTF-8 bytes or its Latin-1 byte), decoded rather than listed. Its own SemVer from 2.0.0-alpha.1 (L3), its plan before its code (L6), core the only member it may use (L5). Each capability is a clause of its own below. No interface of its own: docs/questions.md Q280, ruled 2026-11-25 (a library and one command-line executable; the surfaces are Oficina's). Open: Q45 (where identify, name and address runs; Npcap's licence).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/component.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | `cargo metadata --no-deps` on the scanner and on planted packages: a plain library, a `src/main.rs` and a `[[bin]]` of the package's name accepted; a binary of another name, two binaries, an example, a bench, a build script, a `cdylib`, a library typed `bin`, an empty `crate-type` and a package with no library refused; every target at a path cargo finds by itself, the rule held on fourteen paths and a planted `[[test]]` at a `.txt` path refused; every target cargo names, by kind and name, among those checked, and a planted `test = false` target and a planted `test = false` test named as the library found unchecked; every file rustc's dep-info lists for every target in the dev and release profiles with every feature and for Windows inside the crate, a planted crate pulling in a file by an include in braces behind a comment, a module linked outside, and an include behind `cfg(windows)` and one behind `not(debug_assertions)` each seen in the build that reads it, a cargo configuration in the planted crate's folder setting a `cfg` that leaves an include out read by none of the checks (the include seen) and refused by name, and assembly in a module file not named `.rs` found among the files rustc read; the planted crate's link refused; a planted module whose name holds a line break failing the dep-info reading and refused by name; no cargo configuration, no control character in a name and no link in the scanner's own folder; the dependency walk on the real graph (serde and thiserror reached, nothing banned, no member but core by the workspace's list) and on a planted workspace (normal and build dependencies and theirs, a build-dependency's dependency and a dependency's build-dependency, an optional one behind a feature, one on Windows and one on another architecture reached and named as members, a dev-dependency not), the ban list read by prefix; the split: the codecs as one crate on serde and thiserror, the rest of aranha with its tests as another on them, its dependencies, kinds, platforms and features aranha's own, checked with every target and every feature in the dev and release profiles and for Windows, the codecs' doctests run (they hold none), the codecs alone with their unit tests checked for wasm32, powerpc and i686, and six codecs bound to a platform without a `cfg` (an x86-64 module, a 64-bit assertion, an x86-64 module in a unit test, a little-endian assertion, a 64-bit atomic, an eight-byte alignment of a 64-bit integer) building on x86-64 and failing on the target that shows them, the dependency comparison seeing a kind, a platform and a feature apart on planted manifests, and six poisoned splits failing (a codec test reaching for the wire, a codec reaching for it, the rest giving a codec type a method of its own plainly, behind `cfg(windows)`, behind `not(debug_assertions)`, behind a feature); the codecs' files flat with no `cfg` but `cfg(test)`, and a planted folder's cfg of a platform, cfg_attr, cfg! macro, folder and link each refused by name and a cfg(test) not; no assembly in any file under its folder, the check pinned on five spellings and three innocent words; rustdoc listing no doctest in it in any of the three builds, and listing each of seven a text check would miss (an indented block, a doc attribute after another on its line, a doc block comment mid-line, a fence opened after one left open, a doctest in a module file not named `.rs`, one behind `cfg(windows)` and one behind a feature, these two missed by the host's default build alone) in a planted crate; the six proc macros named, and in a planted workspace a test-only proc macro of the root found and one of a dependency's tests not; every file and name UTF-8, a planted folder's Latin-1 file and non-UTF-8 name refused and a term in a file's name found; every file's contents and name read for core's banned terms and the section sign, the term list held to core's file, the letter table held to supervisão's and every letter of Latin-1 but µ and of Latin Extended-A read as plain letters, the reader pinned on thirty-five planted spellings (each term, across lines, accented, full-width, four Latin letters NFKD leaves whole, three invisible fillers, a mark of combining class 0 and a spacing mark inside a term, a file's name, the sign as itself, its named entity with and without its semicolon, before a digit and at a line's end, numeric entities in decimal and hex with leading zeros, Rust escapes with zeros and an underscore, JSON and TOML escapes of four and eight digits, a byte escape, percent-encoded, as UTF-8 and as Latin-1) and seven innocent texts (a byte escape before a letter past ASCII among them, read without a panic); the laws on the real tree

## C-131: The envelope and the MAC address: Ethernet II with one VLAN tag, the three spellings of a MAC
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", the PROFINET codecs; C-130)
- **Description:** `profinet::ether` reads and writes Ethernet II with at most one 802.1Q tag: destination, source, the tag's priority, drop-eligible bit and VLAN id, the type field, and the payload with whatever pad followed it, however long (each protocol above bounds itself). A second tag, and a tag of 802.1ad (0x88A8) or of 0x9100, is not read as a tag: its type is read as the frame's type and its tag field as the payload's first two bytes, where Wireshark reads a tag. A type field up to 0x05DC is read as an IEEE 802.3 length (`is_length`), from 0x0600 as an EtherType (`is_ethertype`), and between them as neither, as IEEE 802.3 reads it (Wireshark calls it invalid in an untagged frame). A frame is written padded to 60 bytes; a source that is a group address (IEEE 802.3: one station sends), a payload past 1500 bytes, tagged or not, a priority past 7, a VLAN id past 4094 (4095 is reserved by 802.1Q: read, never written), or a type that is no EtherType to write (anything below 0x0600, or a type readers take for a tag: the eight Wireshark 4.6.6 registers as one, 0x8100, 0x88A8 and 0x9100, 802.1ah's I-TAG 0x88E7, 802.1BR's E-TAG 0x893F, 802.1CB's R-TAG 0xF1C1, the VN-Tag 0x8926 and Broadcom's tag 0x8874) is refused, never cut or misread; every other type is written; a frame shorter than 14 bytes (18 tagged) is an error naming its length. `profinet::mac::Mac` reads exactly the spellings `aa:bb:cc:dd:ee:ff`, `aa-bb-cc-dd-ee-ff` and `aabb.ccdd.eeff`, in either case, and refuses every other text with the text it got; it prints the first spelling in lower case, serialises as it prints, and tells a group address (multicast or broadcast) and a locally administered one. Wireshark's dissectors read the frames it writes as they were written, for the EtherTypes the codecs carry (PROFINET's, LLDP's) and a local experimental one, tagged and untagged.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/envelope.rs, crates/veredas-aranha/tests/common/mod.rs | the three spellings in either case and in mixed case, every hex letter in upper and lower case, twenty-five other texts refused (short, long, bare, mixed separators, short and long groups, a sign, a space, a trailing line break, a leading tab, a separator at an end, the dotted form with six groups and the coloned with three, non-ASCII digits and letters, non-ASCII groups exactly a group's width in bytes), the message; the group bit of the DCP, Hello and LLDP group addresses and the broadcast, the local bit on thirteen first octets and two group addresses; serde both ways (read from a string, an owned value, a reader and an escaped string), the twenty-five refused texts refused through it untrimmed; an untagged frame and a tagged one (priority 6, VLAN 0) to the byte, pad included, and read back whole, and tagged ones by their tag bytes and read back (every bit set but the reserved VLAN id's, the drop bit alone, the priority's low bit alone), a tag of nothing (priority 0, VLAN 0) written and read, VLAN id 4095 refused on writing and read, three group sources refused tagged and untagged with the message and such a frame heard read as it is, tags of 802.1ad and 0x9100 read as the frame's type with their tag field the payload's first bytes, the pad to 60 and none past it; 1500 bytes taken, read back whole, and 1501 refused, untagged and tagged (1518 bytes); a 1522-byte frame with two tags and a 9000-byte one read whole; eight types that are lengths, nothing, or read as tags refused both ways, 0x0600, 0x88A9 and 0x9101 taken; a spanning-tree frame's length read as a length and its whole payload, pad included, kept, and seven type fields at the boundaries, tagged and untagged, read as lengths, neither, or EtherTypes (each of the three told apart), each payload whole; every priority from 8 to 255 and every VLAN id from 4095 to 65535 refused by their exact errors, never masked into a written tag, with their messages; payloads of 1501, 65 535, 65 536, 65 537 and 70 000 bytes refused tagged and untagged; every type field from 0 to 0xFFFF tagged and untagged, refused exactly below 0x0600 and for the eight tag types and every other written and read back as itself; the five tag types past VLAN's read by tshark each as its own protocol's tag; 13, 14, 15, 16 and 17 bytes refused naming their length and 18 taken; tshark reading, on each of eight frames (PROFINET's type tagged with priority 6 and untagged, LLDP's untagged and tagged, a tagged 1518-byte frame among them), its source and destination, and its type, or for the five tagged ones the tag's type and its priority, drop bit, VLAN id and inner type, each against what the frame was built with, with no complaint
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-131.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-132: DCP: identify, Hello, Get and Set as the standard writes them, a Set to one device only, damage kept with its reason
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope" and "Rules", the PROFINET codecs; Veredas 1's `dcp.rs`; C-130)
- **Description:** `profinet::dcp` reads and writes PROFINET DCP: Identify requests (every device, one station name, one alias) to the Identify group address `01:0e:cf:00:00:00` with their response delay; Identify answers, and Hellos to the Hello group address `01:0e:cf:00:00:01`, with every identity block (MAC, IP suite with its BlockInfo of set and conflict, device vendor, NameOfStation, vendor and device id, role with its reserved byte (written 0, never read into the role), options, alias, instance, OEM id, device initiative), and the four control blocks that are their qualifier alone (a transaction's start and end, a factory reset, a reset to factory), one holding more kept damaged; Get requests as bare option and suboption pairs, and their answers; Set requests of a name, an IP suite and the identification signal with their BlockQualifier; and Set answers. A block carries a BlockInfo or qualifier exactly where Wireshark's dissector reads one, read and written alike: a control block (option 5, suboptions 1 to 6) a qualifier wherever it is, but the control response (three bytes), which never does; a control option outside those six, the all-selector's option, a manufacturer's own option (0x80 to 0xFE) and an IP option outside its three suboptions none; any other block a BlockInfo in an Identify answer, a Get answer and a Hello request, a qualifier in a Set request, and nothing elsewhere (options the dissector cannot parse, such as 0, 4 and 8, and PDUs of a service or type it decodes no block of, services outside 3 to 6 and types outside 0, 1 and 5, follow that general rule when read, with no reading to hold them to). Text (vendor, station name, alias) travels as ASCII: written only so, heard otherwise kept damaged. A typed block is written only under its own option and suboption, bytes as given only where no typed body is read (any of the twenty typed codes) and the dissector reads them as given: never under the device initiative's option (6) or option 7, which it reads with a layout of its own whatever the block's length says, nor under an option it cannot parse (0, 4 and 8 to 0x7F), which it reads otherwise from three bytes, nor under DHCP's 61 and 255 below three bytes (measured over every untyped code of seventeen options in six kinds of PDU), a damaged block (kept by the decoder to be seen) never, what is written only if it reads back as the PDU written (its service and type as they travel), so trailing bytes only as the decoder would have kept them, a Get request's pairs only in a Get request, a frame id only DCP's; a block written otherwise is refused; an odd block its pad. A Set request and a Hello are known by the bytes they travel as (service 4 with the selection bit clear, service 6), however their service and type are spelled, and each is written only by `frame`, with where it goes (`encode` refuses them): a Hello only to its group address; a Set request gated in the codec for the helpers and for a PDU built, decoded or read from a file alike: only to one device named by its MAC, with no trailing bytes, only of a station name that keeps the standard's rules (1 to 240 characters, labels of 1 to 63 of `a`-`z`, digits and `-`, no hyphen at a label's edge, no port name, no address form), an IP suite (qualifier 0 or 1) or the signal 0x0100 (qualifier 0); nothing else a Set does (a factory reset among it) is expressible, the name never rewritten. A block past its 16-bit length or a PDU past 1500 bytes is refused. Decoding is total: a block that does not fit its kind (its size, an info word missing or cut short, text that is not ASCII) is kept with its bytes and the reason and the next block still decodes; a block running past the data stops decoding there, kept; bytes that make no block are kept; a damaged header is an error naming it; any bytes decode without a panic; a decoded PDU holding no damaged block and no bytes the dissector reads otherwise, written again, reads back the same, and its bytes are the ones heard save a pad byte's value (written 0) and a last odd block's missing pad (written), within what a frame carries; one holding a damaged block or such bytes, and a decoded Set request outside what may be written, decode and are never written again. Wireshark's dissectors read every frame it writes field for field, its destination among them, but for tails kept as heard and written back, which they read as the start of a block and flag (malformed, a parse error), and tell apart Veredas 1's five-byte control response, which this codec reads as damaged and does not write; a damaged block, which the dissector reads its own way (a too-long block's extra bytes as blocks of their own, a too-short one swallowing the next), is never written.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/dcp.rs, crates/veredas-aranha/tests/common/mod.rs | the three Identify requests to the byte (an odd name padded) and read back with no BlockInfo; an answer with every field read back whole, block by block with its info, re-encoded to its own bytes, the smallest answer to the byte, an empty identity and a lone vendor id saying nothing; a Hello to `01:0e:cf:00:00:01`, its header to the byte (0 where a request's delay would be), with its IP BlockInfo for the six states of set and conflict both ways, a missing status read as set, a Hello refused to three other addresses, a Hello answer and an "unsupported" one read with no BlockInfo by this codec and by tshark, an Identify and a Get answer of type 5 read with theirs; a Get request to the byte, an odd last byte kept and written back, a Get answer with a name and a control response to the byte; Set requests of a name (permanent and temporary), an IP suite and the signal to the byte; four group targets refused for each Set helper; Set PDUs built by hand: three good ones taken to one device and refused to four group addresses, sixteen beyond the gate refused by exact error (the two resets and a transaction's start and end, typed, among them), every Set request refused by `encode`, every body of every kind at its own code and at the three allowed codes with five qualifiers each, exactly five shapes written and every other refused by its exact error, five spellings of a Set's service and type under each of DCP's four frame ids gated as a Set, one read from JSON gated, trailing bytes of six and of one, two and three refused, five signals of other values refused, a reset to factory heard on the wire decoded as its typed block and refused when written again, a signal heard with more than its value decoded damaged and refused, a Set answer not gated; info words refused where the PDU carries none and required where it carries one, a damaged block refused (each of four damages, with a word and without, in an answer, a filter and a Hello), a typed block under another code, a pair outside a Get request and a block inside one refused; the dissector's info rule for thirteen blocks with no word (an IP option outside its suboptions, 1/0, 1/4, 1/8 and 1/9; a control option outside its six, 5/0, 5/7 and 5/9; the all-selector's option; four manufacturers' options), with neither the general nor the IP BlockInfo nor a qualifier read for them, and a control block in a Set answer, by this codec and by tshark; the four qualifier-only control blocks to the byte both ways in an answer, read by tshark with their qualifiers (the resets' under their own field) and no complaint, and a reset holding a name's bytes in a Hello kept damaged by this codec, refused when written again, and read as a second name by tshark; unread bytes under a typed code refused (Veredas 1's five-byte control response as bytes among them), empty or not under each of the twenty typed codes (listed apart from the codec's list and held to it); what would read back as another refused (trailing bytes that make a block or a Get request's pair); a Get request's pair with a word and a frame id not DCP's refused; a Hello spelled five ways (its service and its type) under each of the four frame ids refused by `encode` and to three other addresses; the device options and a full IP suite to the byte, read back, and read by tshark in their order; thirty-four names against the rules; a Set answer of two control responses to the byte, and one holding a name block read with no info word, by this codec and by tshark; twenty damaged blocks (three good blocks of the same shapes beside them in the table) each kept with its exact reason beside a good next block, an overrun stopping with what came before kept and no trailing bytes, tails of one, two and three bytes kept (and written back), each read by tshark as the start of a block and flagged (the three bytes malformed, one or two a parse error, a Get request's odd byte a parse error too), the frame's pad left out; header damage named; 1500 bytes written and 1501 refused, a vendor text past the frame, a block past 65535 and a PDU of 80 024 bytes refused; eight written frames decoded and written back to their own bytes, a reserved role byte kept and not read into the role, the identity's role block written with it 0, an RSI properties block to the byte in an answer, read back and read by tshark as 261, the two bytes not kept pinned; twenty thousand noisy PDUs and two seeds (bytes the dissector reads otherwise, text not ASCII) cut at every length with the data length restated, decoding without a panic and writing back to themselves, those holding a damaged block or bytes read otherwise refused for the first of them, gated Sets refused by the gate's errors; tshark reading eleven frames' destinations, services, types, transaction ids, delays, filters, every identity field, the IP BlockInfo and its conflict bit, the qualifiers, the signal, the Get answer's blocks and the control responses' errors with no complaint, and reading a hand-written five-byte control response's error as 2, which this codec, reading it as damaged, refuses to write again (in a Set answer and a Get answer); bytes as given under every untyped code of seventeen options (0 to 9, 0x10, 0x40, 0x7F, 0x80, 0xC0, 0xFE, 0xFF) in six kinds of PDU (an Identify answer and its unsupported one, a Hello, a Get and a Set answer, an Identify request), of 0, 1, 2, 3 and 12 bytes with a name after them: every frame written read by tshark with the codec's names, the refusals exactly options 0, 4, 6 to 9, 0x10, 0x40 and 0x7F at every length and DHCP's 61 and 255 under three bytes, each by its exact error, 3/61 and 3/255 of three and twelve bytes written; vendor, station name and alias text not ASCII refused by their exact error and its message, text not ASCII heard kept damaged, a control byte read as text
