## C-133: LLDP with PROFINET's, 802.1's and 802.3's TLVs: every TLV kept, what a TLV cannot carry refused
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope" and "Rules", the PROFINET codecs; Veredas 1's `lldp.rs`; C-130)
- **Description:** `profinet::lldp` reads and writes LLDP: chassis id, port id and time to live (mandatory, and the first three TLVs in that order, as 802.1AB has a receiver check them: one out of place, missing, or in its place but not fit for its type, is an error naming it; each is read in its place only, never made good from a later TLV, and one of their types later in the PDU is a repeat, kept), port description, system name, description and capabilities, management addresses with their OID, PROFINET's measured delays, port status, MRP port status (the ring's domain and MRRT's status: off, configured, up) and chassis MAC, IEEE 802.1's port VLAN id and IEEE 802.3's MAC/PHY status, and the End TLV as heard (empty, holding bytes, or absent; a PDU with no End keeps the one byte after its last whole TLV). A TLV it does not read, one that repeats what the PDU already said, and one the standard does not let say what it says (an id outside 1 to 255 bytes, a MAC id not of 6 bytes, a network-address id that is not its family byte and an address of its family's length, at least one byte, a management address not of its family's length or whose address string is outside 2 to 32 bytes, an OID past 128 bytes, a text past 255, text that is not UTF-8) is kept with its bytes and written back (but for what the dissector reads its own way, below), so a decoded PDU, written again, reads back the same (the TLVs it reads first in the standard's order, then those it kept); a TLV running past the PDU is an error naming it. What a TLV cannot carry is refused, never cut: an id outside 1 to 255 bytes, a MAC id not of 6 bytes or a network-address id not of its family's length, a description or system name past 255, a management address outside 1 to 31 bytes or not its family's length, an OID past 128 (802.1AB's 167-byte TLV), any value past 511, a kept TLV of type 0 or past 127, a frame past 1500; a kept capabilities TLV past four bytes or a kept management address TLV of any length, which the dissector reads with a layout of its own and so reads what follows otherwise (kept when heard, never written). A time to live of 0 says the sender is leaving, and such a shutdown PDU is written with the three mandatory TLVs alone (802.1AB; the dissector calls any other in it malformed), one heard with more kept; any bytes decode without a panic, and every TLV heard is written back the same bytes, whatever their order, unless it is one of those never written. Errors count TLVs from 1. An absent End survives `encode`; `frame` writes a PDU with no End only when it fills the frame (46 bytes), since a pad would be read as more of it, and refuses it otherwise. Wireshark's dissectors read every TLV this codec reads, field for field, in the frames it writes, with these readings of its own, pinned: text in ASCII, control characters as they are (it stops at a NUL and shows each byte past ASCII as a replacement character), the OID as a BER object identifier (bytes that are not one shown as it reads them), an End holding bytes called an invalid length, and nothing read past a TLV of a reserved type (so neither a kept TLV nor the End after it); what this codec keeps unread it writes back with the bytes it heard, the organisation TLVs first and then the others, each in the order heard, so a reader that stops at one it cannot read (a reserved type, a malformed organisation TLV) reads no further; and it reads a port id of the network kind of a family other than IPv4's and IPv6's as text after the family byte (stopping at a NUL, warning of the rest).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/lldp.rs, crates/veredas-aranha/tests/common/mod.rs | the smallest PDU to the byte, in a padded frame to the LLDP group address; a PROFINET port with every TLV read back whole, its MRP TLV (a domain of sixteen distinct bytes) and chassis MAC TLV to the byte, the MRRT names, the capability names, the short port name and chassis key; a switch's port with a MAC chassis, a name port, an unknown TLV, two unread organisation TLVs and a management address with an OID kept and written back; a second chassis id, time to live, system name and chassis MAC, text that is not UTF-8, a short capabilities TLV, a bad management address and a short organisation TLV kept unread with their bytes; ids of no byte and of 256 bytes in their place the PDU's error, a 256-byte system name and an IPv4 address of one byte kept unread; an End holding three bytes kept and written back to the byte, a 1500-byte PDU with no End written back to the byte and framed to 1514; a short PDU, two overruns (counting TLVs from 1) and three missing mandatory TLVs named, five PDUs whose first three TLVs are out of order refused naming the TLV out of place (one read by tshark, which discards it), a TLV past the End ignored and a PDU with no End read with its lone trailing byte kept and written back (and trailing bytes the writer cannot place refused); every accepted bound (ids of 255 bytes, a network id of an unknown family, three texts of 255, addresses of 1 and 31 bytes, an OID of 128) written and read back the same, a 256-byte port id and address strings of 1 and 33 bytes read as none or kept unread; chassis ids of network, interface-name and other subtypes and port ids of alias, MAC and other subtypes read from their bytes with their keys and short names, MAC ids of bytes past ASCII (chassis and port) and network chassis ids of IPv4 and IPv6 among them; ids of 0, 1, 255 and 256 bytes for chassis and port, an empty network chassis, three texts at 255 and 256, values of 511 and 512 (a plain and an organisation TLV), types 128 and 0, ten management addresses by family and length, and a 1520-byte frame, each refused or taken with its exact error; a leaving sender; for each of eight TLVs it reads, alone at its size read, alone one byte short and long kept unread and written back (the time to live then the PDU's error, a capabilities TLV of five bytes refused on writing), and after a good one a repeat and one byte short and long kept unread and written back; a management address with a byte past its OID and a 129-byte OID kept unread, a 7-byte MAC chassis id first the PDU's error; seven chassis and three port ids by form, refused or taken with the exact error, and OIDs of 128 and 129; text with a NUL, accented text and text with control characters against tshark, each byte past ASCII a replacement character and the control characters as they are; an OID read as 1.3.6.1 and a non-BER one as tshark reads it; an End holding bytes called an invalid length for the End by tshark and a reserved TLV ending tshark's reading; a short PDU with no End through `encode`, refused by `frame` (alone and with each of four trailing bytes, and at 45 bytes) and framed at 46 bytes, 60 on the wire, and read back with no End; network ids of five and three IPv4 bytes, a family byte alone and a short IPv6 address refused for chassis and port with the exact error and, heard in their place, the PDU's error naming them, and one of its family's length read; seven management addresses heard of another length than their family's or with an address string of 1 or 33 bytes kept unread and refused on writing, five at their bounds read, and two (IPv4, then IPv6) read and written back to the byte; the eleven capability bits each alone named as tshark names it; the short port name of a PROFINET port with and without its station, with a dotted station, and of a dotted other one; tshark reading network ids of IPv4 and IPv6, chassis and port, as addresses with no complaint, and a port id of an 802 address family as text with its warning; the kept TLVs written organisation TLVs first, each group in its heard order, and a malformed kept organisation TLV ending tshark's reading; twenty thousand noisy PDUs (organisation TLVs of up to 23 data bytes, repeats of the mandatory three among the rest) cut at every length decoding without a panic, reading back the same, and every TLV heard written back the same bytes, but for more than a thousand holding a kept capabilities or management address TLV, refused for it, and shutdowns with more; tshark reading three frames' every TLV field (subtypes, ids, time to live, descriptions, capabilities, the management addresses, IPv4 and IPv6 with their interface subtypes, numbers and OID lengths, the five delays, both port status words (the RT class 2 one 0x0102), the MRP domain and MRRT status, the chassis MAC, the port VLAN, the MAC/PHY fields, the TLV types in order) with no complaint; the critic's four PDUs (an unfit chassis id, an empty one, an unfit port id, an unfit time to live in its place, a fit one of the type after) the PDU's error by its exact error and message, tshark complaining of each; five repeats of the mandatory three after them (fit or not) kept and written back to the byte, the three read from their place; an organisation TLV with no information string read as one, written before a kept reserved TLV, and read by tshark; three kept TLVs tshark reads its own way (capabilities of five and six bytes, a management address of nine) kept and refused on writing with the exact error and message, tshark not reading the system name after them, and a capabilities TLV of three bytes written back; a shutdown PDU with a system name kept, refused on writing with the exact error and message, tshark calling it malformed, five other extras (one empty) refused, and one of the three alone written to the byte
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-133.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-200: veredas-comissionador — the testing platform
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-comissionador: the testing platform", rules: reads plant data only through portal, drives machinery only through portal's gated writes; decision 6; docs/comissionador-plan.md; docs/questions.md Q314, taken meanwhile); promoted from P-006 (the component)
- **Description:** `crates/veredas-comissionador` runs a plant's acceptance tests through portal (`docs/comissionador-plan.md`: the role nobody covers, the phases, what the engine offers Oficina): a library and one executable named `veredas-comissionador`, both built from its `src/` folder, every file of which is Rust; no build script; at `2.0.0-alpha.1`; its members among its normal and build dependencies core and portal at most (L5), its normal dependencies a reviewed list holding no OPC UA stack and no socket or HTTP crate of its own. It reaches the plant through portal's session alone and arms portal's write switch only as the architect ruled (Q314 (b)): for a run whose test's gated writes the person confirmed, by the session's own arming the executable hands the run, the run disarming it at its end: every file of its `src/`, read as the compiler's tokens (a raw identifier read as its name; a comment is not code), names the arming (`set_writes_armed`) once, in `main.rs`, the session's arming handed to the run, and makes no gate (`WriteGate`), names `writes_armed` only to read the switch's position (`.writes_armed()`), never as a field, a struct literal's key or an assignment, calls nothing named `spawn` (portal's two functions that start a worker that writes, `opc::worker::spawn` and `channel::spawn`, take a gate and are so named, so a gate made by `Default` serves nothing; core's own writers, its telegram channel and its fieldbus masters, are behind the refused `telegram` and `fieldbus` paths; a runtime's own threads are not such a worker), takes no path into core's or portal's protocol layers (`opc`, `telegram`, `fieldbus`) nor portal's log layer (`tail`, `FileTailSource`, `FamilyTailSource`, `SourceSet`, `Source`), names of portal nothing but what the engine takes from it (the session and what it shows, the project's reader, the store's queries, the coercion and clock of portal's serving: every path into portal starts with one of these items, never portal whole, a glob or a module, so its search of logs at rest and its CSV previews stay out), opens no socket (`net`) and no process (`Command`) of its own, names `fs` (its own files: a test and its function files, the runs folder), and a path's own reach to the disk (`read_dir`, `metadata`, `exists` and their kin), in `check.rs`, `main.rs` and `record.rs` alone, a plant's file read there being outside what the reading tells from its own, holds no `unsafe` code (the system's own calls, a socket among them), reads in no Veredas 1 project (`from_v1`, whose files spell the switch their own way), brings in no tokens from a file the reading does not see (`include`, `include_str`, `include_bytes`, a `path` in an attribute), and writes no project text that arms the switch (a literal holding `writes_armed`, `allow_writes` or `allow_dashboard_writes`; a doc comment's text is a comment's). The guard is against accidents, not deliberate hiding (the standard of Q26): a name encoded, split or computed so that it is not read is outside it, as are a dependency's macro and the code it generates; the tests are not read (a test of the engine may arm a session it holds, as portal's do). Its plan came before its code (L6) and it versions on its own from 2.0.0 (L3).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-comissionador/tests/component.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | `cargo metadata --no-deps`: one library `veredas_comissionador`, one binary `veredas-comissionador`, the rest tests, no custom build, the version `2.0.0-alpha.1`; every target but the tests built from a file under `src/`, and every file under `src/` (six at least) Rust; the rule of targets refusing a missing binary, a second binary, a library of another crate type and a build script, each by its text; its members among normal and build dependencies within core and portal, core among them; every normal dependency on the reviewed list; every file under `src/` holding none of the refused forms; `fs` named in `check.rs`, `main.rs` and `record.rs` alone, and found elsewhere as `std::fs`, `tokio::fs` and an alias, a comment passed; the list of refused names held equal to the names the test asserts one by one, portal's log layer among them; the reading finding each form as written plainly, as a raw identifier, inside a macro's body, a gate by `Default` through a spawned worker, a path in a plain attribute, in `cfg_attr` and in an inner attribute, a project text in a literal in each of the three spellings, `writes_armed` as a field, a struct literal's key, an assignment's target and a call given a value, `from_v1`, `unsafe` code and an `unsafe extern` block, and passing a comment and a doc comment, a `path` that is a parameter, a read of the switch's position and a name that holds `opc` within it; the laws on the real tree with comissionador in it; refute round 5's finding: portal's search of logs at rest (`grep::search`, `GrepScope`, `GrepQuery`), its CSV previews (`store::preview_csv`, `sniff_csv_options`) and its families (`tail::FamilySpec`) refused by the list of what the engine takes from portal, however written (a use tree, `self`, a rename, a raw identifier, a macro's body), portal whole, a glob and a module imported refused, an allowed item, its methods and a renamed item passing, a comment and a literal passed over, every item of the list one the sources take today; a folder listed and a file looked at without `fs` (`read_dir`, `exists`, `metadata`, `is_file`) refused outside the three files; the critic's readers put in `plant.rs` failing the sources' test; the architect's Q314 (b): `set_writes_armed` named once across the sources, in `main.rs`, any other file naming it refused
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-200.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-201: The test file and the function file: one plain-text test, read into the model the engine runs and checked whole, every problem by its place
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (decision 6: one test file, two views, script boxes, functions written in text and reused as blocks, branching on any outcome with retries; docs/test-schema.md; docs/veredas-plan.md "Versioned files"; docs/questions.md Q310, Q311, Q317, taken meanwhile; C-045)
- **Description:** A test file (`veredas-test`, version 1) and a function file (`veredas-functions`, version 1) are read into the model of `veredas_comissionador::model` and checked whole (`check_test` with a reader for the files a test uses, `load_test` from disk relative to the test file's folder, `check_functions` for a function file alone), as `docs/test-schema.md` says: the stamp first, the header's lines, TOML, read by core's one header reader (C-045) with the suite's texts, no other key in the header; then the body, the reading of a file stopping at its first error of form, named with its file, line and column; then every problem of the check named the same way. Read: settings with their defaults (both modes, a run's bound of ten minutes) and the rest of a line for `id`, `area`, `portal` and `use`, the roles the test names to sign its reports (`sign tester, witness`, each a role and once, Q312 (e)); steps of one or more of eleven actions, `branch` and `stop` last, `on` routes after them; branch arms by comparison, range or value, `else` last; routes to a step, a counted retry or an end; the layout; functions with typed ports, a description from the comments above them, `reads` and `timeout`; statements of script boxes and functions; expressions by precedence, built-ins by arity, strings with their escapes and `{ }` expressions, durations in whole milliseconds, read exactly, with no loop over an exponent; the language's own words never a name (`max` not among them; a table, a channel or a field so named in backquotes; an input never so named); blocks and expressions nested deeper than 64 levels, counted together, or an expression of more than 1000 operators, counted across the parentheses, the patterns, the ranges and the strings' braces it holds, refused at their place, so no input stops the reader by exhausting its stack; a byte-order mark, a multi-line string, a comment and a single-line string read as TOML reads them in the stamp; every check linear in the file but the search of a cycle's way back, inside its own cycle, a long one named by its first five functions and counted; the inputs a call leaves out are one problem, a long list named by its first five and counted, so a call costs its own arguments; a place's column counted from a mark every 64 bytes of its line, in any order. Checked: every name settled, a variable's `<name>.<column>` its row's column and any other plant data; plant data never in a script box or a `let`, a function's tables declared in its `reads`; a script box neither waiting nor calling; constants and inputs never assigned; calls against the functions of the files a test uses, a function's calls against its own file, none back to itself; routes to steps that exist, retries above 0, each outcome routed once, a branch's pass by its arms; the counts the designer shows (steps, branches, functions called, gated writes). What a run does with any of it is the engine's (phases 2 and 3), not this clause's. Distinct from the suite's TOML files (the channel file C-017 onward, portal's project file C-046, supervisão's files C-048): the body is not TOML (Q310, taken meanwhile), so the reader cuts the header at the first line that opens with `test` or `function` and hands core's reader that alone.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-comissionador/tests/grammar.rs | the worked example loaded from the reference itself: its title and every setting, its eight steps by name and block, each action's parts (the send's channel, message and field; the wait's condition and timeout and its routed timeout; the call's inputs and its outputs on the line after it; the script's two assignments; the branch's comparison, range with its retry, and else; the report's values and its interpolated note in four parts; the two ends), its eight positions, the function's signature, description, reads, timeout and body; checked with the function file, the counts 8, 1, 1 and 3 as the reference's sentence says, a name that is no variable settled as plant data and a variable's as its row's column; from disk beside its function folder, with CRLF line ends alike, and a missing function file named at its `use` with the call it leaves unresolved; the header's five refusals by the suite's texts (no format, no version, another format, another version, the format decided first) and another key by its line, comments in the header, a function file's own format; comments and blank lines anywhere, a `#` in a string kept; the reference's list of words equal to the reader's, each refused as a step's name at its place, a column named `read` after a dot, a table in backquotes, a name starting with a digit, a letter outside ASCII, a blank or unclosed backquote; a line broken inside parentheses and before an arrow, and not elsewhere; every literal of the reference read to its value, and eleven unreadable texts each by its reason and place; the settings' defaults and the rest of a line trimmed, commented and unquoted, and every refusal of a setting the reference lists, by text and place; ## C-290: The release manifest: a set of component versions pinned at one commit, read as versioned TOML and checked against history
- **Date:** 2026-11-22

Checked against the repository's history, never the working tree (a shallow clone, its history cut, and a repository whose objects are not named by SHA-1 refused by name): the commit there, a commit's own id resolving to itself (a tag's id refused by name), and an ancestor of HEAD; every git read made with replace refs and the grafts file turned off, so objects and ancestry are the ones committed; the commit's tree taken out of git's objects as stored (no attribute, export rule or filter applied; a tree holding a link, a submodule, a path that is not UTF-8 or a path with a `..`, `.` or empty part refused by that path, before anything is written; so a setting or a file outside the history changes nothing) into a scratch folder of the call's own under the tool's cache, whatever `TMPDIR` says (behind a link included; a parent holding `*`, `?` or `[`, which cargo would read as a pattern, or not UTF-8, refused by name, judged as its canonical path, links and `..` resolved as the kernel resolves them), cargo run from inside it, and read by cargo itself (`cargo metadata --no-deps --offline`, each member's folder read against the workspace root cargo names), the commit's root manifest a workspace (or cargo would look above the tree; one that does not parse refused with cargo's reason) and everything cargo read inside the tree (its workspace root, every package, every path dependency, each refused by name otherwise), so every member of its workspace under `crates/`, however cargo accepts its spelling (`./crates/x`, `crates/x/`), is named in the manifest at the version cargo reads there, shipping every `bin` target cargo names for it without required features, and of those with required features the ones cargo's own build with the default features makes, as the cut sees it (C-291; alforge models no feature: a manifest may name or leave out such a bin, never one there is not; docs/questions.md Q443, `// DECISION PENDING`; cargo's own discovery for its edition); a workspace cargo cannot read refused with cargo's reason, its causes included; nothing else named; a later commit moving nothing. Whatever fails in reading one commit's tree (its root manifest not UTF-8 among them) is that commit's refusal alone: no component is judged against it, no other release is charged with a difference from it, no error hides another's refusal. Across a releases folder, every file is a manifest named after its own release, and a component's version pinned by two releases names its build's inputs, everything its build reads but the toolchain (docs/questions.md Q441, ruled 2026-11-25) (a release whose commit is no commit, or a pin its commit refutes, left to its own refusal, charging and hiding no other): its folder; the folder of every member and of every package by path outside the members it uses (normal and build dependencies, transitively; read by cargo in the workspace each belongs to, with that workspace's manifest); every file a target of theirs starts from (cargo's `src_path`: a `[lib] path`, a `[[bin]] path` or a build script outside the folder; a library of any crate type) and every file outside them their sources name by a literal `#[path]`, a `path =` inside `#[cfg_attr(…)]`, `include_str!`, `include_bytes!` or `include!` (read from the tokens as rustc lexes them, line endings made LF: char, raw and byte literals, nested comments, tokens apart; the folders read whole, their tests', benches' and examples' sources included: docs/questions.md Q445), every file a target starts from and every file a source names as Rust (a module, an `include!`) read in turn wherever it lies, whatever its name ends with (one climbing out of the tree refused by name); the lock file's entries of its external dependencies, transitively, by source and checksum (a git source as cargo writes it, a `[replace]` followed to its package, a package by path the workspace knows named by its folder); the workspace's manifest (whole, any byte of it charging every component: docs/questions.md Q442, `// DECISION PENDING`) and the root's cargo configuration; each as git stores it at the commit, every input that differs named. Inputs that do not read (no lock file to pin a dependency; a lock file that is not the workspace's, or names a package it does not pin or pins twice; a package the lock pins by path that is no package the workspace knows, as a `[patch]` or a `[replace]` to another folder makes; a replacement the lock does not pin; a root cargo configuration overriding packages by `paths`; a package by path cargo cannot read) are refused by their reason where a version is pinned twice. Limits: what a build script or a procedural macro reads beyond these, a module a file outside the folders declares beside it, and a `#[path]` inside an inline `mod {}` (which rustc resolves from that module's own folder, this scan from its file's) are not read as rustc reads them; the scan reads these forms from the tokens, not the code as the compiler expands it, so a form beyond them (a crate's own macro including a file) is for each build's dep-info to show; the cut holds every component's build (its executables', or its library's when it ships none) to them by cargo's dep-info, each path judged where it lands (C-291). A test-only change in a folder charges it (docs/questions.md Q445, `// DECISION PENDING`). `alforge check` (it takes no file: a manifest is never judged apart from its folder; the repository found wherever it lies, a path ending in a space or not UTF-8 included) runs all of it on a repository's releases, judging every manifest that reads beside any that does not (exit 0 with a line per release, 2 with every refusal said, 1 on a usage error); the repository holds its first release, 2.0.0-alpha.1, and every manifest of it checks against its own history.
Like the project file (C-046) and supervisão's files (C-048), a release manifest is one more versioned TOML file of the suite held to its stamp by core's reader (C-045) and by law L7; it runs nothing and starts nothing, it records what a cut (C-291) built and what the tester machine (C-292) carries.
Its scope: the repository's own record, a manifest the cut wrote, in the suite's repository with its laws green; a hostile history or environment is outside its promise, though each one its refute rounds built is refused by name (.zen/evidence/C-290.md).

## C-291: The cut: a release's executables built on the tester machine's distribution, packed into a bundle with its sums and run together on the plant it ships
- **Date:** 2026-11-22

- **Description:** `alforge cut <release>` cuts HEAD of a clean tree, refusing by name a tree that differs from HEAD (an untracked file included whatever `status.showUntrackedFiles` says, a file ignored by a rule the commit does not hold, by `.git/info/exclude` or an untracked `.gitignore`, a file marked `--assume-unchanged` or `--skip-worktree`, and a mode the commit does not hold whatever `core.fileMode` says, too; a folder of files the commit's own rules ignore one by one no difference) and a release whose manifest exists in `releases/` or where it writes. It reads the components, their versions and executables (C-290; an executable's name two components build refused by name, a bundle and the tester machine's `PATH` holding one executable a name) and the recipe (`tools/alforge/maquina/maquina.toml`, `veredas-machine` v1, read through core's header reader, every key checked, the library limits versions of dotted digits) at the commit, and checks the manifest it would write, before running or building anything, against the repository and, for a real cut, against every release already cut (C-290's checks: a version pinned at other code refused); a trial's manifest, never committed, is checked against the repository alone. A real cut then runs the whole suite there, its default profile, every variable of the caller's shell that would steer cargo, rustc or nextest removed, behind the machine's lock, its temporary files in the cache, and refuses a red one or a tree that moved while it ran (HEAD or any file), where a trial cut (`--trial`) skips it, its manifest says `not run: a trial cut` and goes beside its bundle (never into `releases/`, refused there). It builds every component alone (`cargo build --release --locked --offline -p <component> --bins`, `--lib` for one that ships no executable, so cargo unifies features within its own inputs and no other component's manifest reaches them: docs/questions.md Q444, `// DECISION PENDING`, as the suite tests them built together), inside a builder machine of the tester machine's distribution (Debian 13 under QEMU with KVM, four processors and 6 GB, the build four jobs, holding the host's lock for the database engine's compilation, started only with 12 GB available on the host), from the commit's tree as git stores it and the `cargo vendor` of its lock file, under the `rustc` version the suite runs with (read in the suite's own cleaned environment, so a `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN` or `cargo +toolchain` of the caller's steers neither), installed from rustup's official source in a builder disk cached per toolchain (a builder printing another `rustc --version` refused), a bin cargo did not build with the default features named and refused; holds what each executable's compilation read (cargo's dep-info for it, handed back with it; a library's for a component that ships none) to what its component's version names (C-290), each path judged where it lands (`..` resolved), a file of the tree outside it or a file outside the tree refused by name and a missing dep-info refused, writes the manifest once the build has said which bins with required features cargo made (each it did not make said and not shipped) and checks it against the repository again, and refuses before running anything a component whose build inputs do not read; refuses, by name, an executable whose glibc, libstdc++ or C++ ABI versions (read by `objdump -p`) pass what the recipe says the distribution offers; packs the bundle from git's objects at the commit, as stored (the README, the suite's plan, every file reference, the pinned components' plans (the language's crates' shared `docs/orcl/README.md` among them), the design references, the plant `tools/alforge/planta/`) with the executables, the manifest and `SHA256SUMS` over every other file, in name order, owned by root, dated at the commit, every mode read as 0644 or 0755 whatever the umask or a setgid folder, tar and gzip run without the shell's `TAR_OPTIONS` and `GZIP`, so the same commit and executables pack to the same bytes; extracts it again and refuses a file that does not match its sum; runs the bundle's bench and supervisão together on a copy of its plant where only its ports and supervisão's address change (to free ports on the loopback; the shipped bind to every interface is C-292's to run), and refuses, naming each fault, unless the page is served, every object of the loop is live, both sources are live, both parcels move along the loop, the write the binding offers is refused by portal's switch (403, "writes are turned off for this project"), and both executables exit 0 once their stdin closes; only then writes the manifest. Limits: every refusal the cut makes before its build is shown stopping it, nothing built; two no tree cargo reads can reach (a `rustc --version` naming no version, a manifest that would not read back) are read from the code, and so is what no test reaches without a machine: that the build holds the engine's lock while the builder machine runs and that the machine starts only under the 12 GB rule (the lock and the rule themselves shown), that the builder runs the script shown and its log is judged as shown (a bin cargo did not build refused by name, shown on the host; no member has such a bin), and that the cut stops on a fault of the run together, every fault itself shown named; the run is on the development machine, the tester machine's own run is C-292.

## C-293: The tester machine's update command: a newer release's bundle checked, installed beside the old one and switched to, the plant's files left alone
- **Date:** 2026-11-25
- **Source:** user (docs/questions.md Q437, ruled 2026-11-25 "Update command": "a command in the machine fetches a newer release bundle, checks its sums and manifest, installs it beside the old one and switches to it; the plant's files are left alone"; P-022); ingest (docs/alforge-plan.md "Delivery and updates")
- **Description:** `sudo veredas-atualizar <bundle.tar.gz>`, installed in every tester machine by the provisioning (C-292) at `/usr/local/sbin`, takes a release's bundle by path (nothing is published, Q436, so it fetches from nowhere), holds its lock before reading anything (the installed release and `current` are the ones it replaces), reads the bundle once into its staging folder and prints that copy's SHA-256; and its `SHA256SUMS`' digest (what the boot's check says as `VEREDAS-BUNDLE`); it installs it only if the whole gzip stream reads (its CRC and length judging every byte; any error reading it a refusal, never a traceback), the archive is one folder of files and folders (a link, a hard link, a FIFO, a path out of the folder and a second folder at the top refused by name), the disk has its size and a reserve free, every file matches its `SHA256SUMS` (a changed, an extra and a missing file each named), its `release.toml` reads as `veredas-release` version 1 stating every key of a manifest, of the machine's target and cut for real (a trial's refused), its folder is named after its release, no executable named by two components, no place on the PATH an executable would take held by a file or folder no release made, its release is newer than the installed one by SemVer's precedence (a pre-release below its release), its manifest names exactly the executables in `bin/` and no installed folder has its name, and every executable finds its libraries on the machine (`ldd`); then it moves the folder beside the installed release under `/opt/veredas/`, renames a relative `current` link over the old one, gives the PATH a link through `current` for each of its executables and drops the one for an executable it no longer has (another's links kept), and restarts the bench and supervisão on it. and sees both stay active on one main process at two looks. Anything refused, an I/O error once the folder is moved included (`current`, the links and the folder put back), leaves every file and link of the machine as it was and restarts nothing (exit 2, every reason said); run without the rights to write `/opt/veredas` it says to use sudo, and while another update runs it refuses at once (the folder itself locked, nothing left behind); a restart that fails, or a service that does not stay up (a new release refusing the kept plant), is said with the way back (exit 3). The plant's files in the home folder are never touched. Inside a tester machine, the shipped disk booted copy-on-write on VMware's devices with a newer release's bundle on a disk, the command run at the serial console as the recipe's user with sudo installs it: `current` names it, both releases are in `/opt/veredas`, the bench and supervisão run its executables, the boot's check passes on it, the plant's shipped files are unchanged, the same bundle is refused a second time, the plant is served from the host, and, booted again, the machine's own check passes on the new release and the plant is served from the boot; the shipped disk is unchanged. Limits: the manifest is read by Python's TOML reader for the keys the command uses, not by core's (C-290 judged it at the cut); the bundle's integrity is its own sums, not a signature, and a hostile bundle is outside its promise (`ldd` runs its executables' loader as root; a signature is the suite's security round's, P-014); what a bundle cannot carry (the command itself, the services, the packages, the desktop) still needs a new OVA; the old releases are kept (about 160 MB each on a 16 GB disk) and nothing removes them; going back is the stated link, not a command; the plant's files are left even when a new release's plant would need them changed; how a bundle reaches a machine under VMware (dragged in, a shared folder) is the architect's to try, the proof hands it in on a disk.
It is P-022's update, the release C-290 states and C-291 cuts.
- **Verification:** automated → tools/alforge/tests/atualizar.rs, tools/alforge/tests/maquina.rs | the shipped script run by `python3` against a machine's folders of the test's own (`VEREDAS_ROOT`) with a stand-in `systemctl`, bundles packed by the cut's code: a newer pre-release installed beside the old one, its output line for line with the bundle's SHA-256 and its sums' digest, `current` relative and moved, the old release and the plant's files untouched, the new release's files matching their sums, the PATH's links made, dropped and another's kept, the services restarted, no staging left, and the same bundle refused (not newer, there already); a trial's bundle, a manifest missing keys (`[tested]` among them), an executable two components name, a disk without the room for the bundle's files beside the reserve (a sparse file of 200 MB against the reserve set under the free room), a byte changed anywhere in the compressed stream (forty places, each refused), a path out of the folder, a FIFO and a hard link in the archive, a file on the PATH no release made, and I/O errors once `current` is moved and once a link has changed (all of it put back); ## C-134: The raw link, and the private network a test makes for it: real frames on a veth pair with no privilege on the host
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", the wire, and "Rules", the real wire in tests without privilege; the orchestrator's ask of 2026-11-22 for one helper for the three members that send raw frames, and for a raw-frame test that says by name where it cannot run)
- **Description:** `wire::raw::RawLink` (Linux) opens an `AF_PACKET` link on a named Ethernet interface (a card, a veth end; loopback refused), hearing every EtherType or one (a filter the kernel runs, matching the type behind up to two tags, 802.1Q or 802.1ad in any order, counting a tag the kernel took out of the frame, so on frames heard and sent alike, keeping a frame whole; a tag's own type refused as a filter), joining every group address (promiscuous mode on request), the interface's MAC read from the kernel of its own namespace; it sends a whole frame without ever waiting (a full queue is refused by name) and hands back the next frame heard within a timeout, none before the timeout's end, through signals and frames passed over, to the microsecond (a timeout past what the clock counts waiting for the next frame), as it was on the wire (an 802.1Q tag the kernel took out put back where it was), with the time it was read and whether it left by this interface (this host sent it on it), the sender never hearing its own frame back, and frames this host's other sockets send on it left out on request by the kernel (so a burst of them never fills the link's buffer against a frame from the wire), whatever their length; a frame past what the interface carries (the kernel counts an 802.1ad tag in the 1514, an 802.1Q tag not) or shorter than an Ethernet header is refused by name on sending, and one heard past the link's 65 536-byte buffer (a tag it puts back counted) is refused with its whole length; a missing interface and a name that is not an interface's primary name by the kernel's own byte rule (empty, past 15 bytes, `.`, `..`, with `/`, `:`, NUL or a byte its `isspace` takes, 9 to 13, 32 and 0xA0) are refused by name before anything opens (an alternative name the kernel gives an interface is held to the same rule: one that keeps it opens its interface, one with a space, a colon or past 15 bytes is refused); a lookup that fails for another reason than a missing interface (no descriptor left for it) is named as the lookup's failure, not as a missing interface; without `CAP_NET_RAW` the open is refused with a reason naming the capability and the two ways to it; closing the link leaves the interface's memberships as they were. `netns::isolated` runs a test's body, the calling test's own name given and one private network per test, in a user and network namespace of its own, the test binary run again for that one test, unshared between `fork` and `exec` and mapped to root inside, with loopback and the veth pairs the test names up, their carriers waited for and a numbered probe (EtherType 0x88B6, which no test sends) proven to cross each pair each way before the body (`netns::wait_pairs`, which a test that raises a pair again calls; a pair that carries nothing either way fails it by name after 5 s), and IPv6 off, nothing of the host touched and nothing left behind; it fails, with the child's own output, when the body fails or never ran (a name that runs no test is a failure, never a pass), and where the namespace cannot be made it fails naming why with what the system says of user namespaces (`docs/aranha-plan.md`, "Traps"). Proven here only where the process may make a user namespace with capabilities (the desktop app's sessions on the Linux machine); elsewhere the test is red by name.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/raw_link.rs | in a private network: a DCP Identify-All from one end heard whole on the other, and one sent 400 ms into a wait read at or after its send, seen leaving by a second link on the sending end and not heard back by the sender, an LLDP frame to its group address and a unicast answer the other way; a priority 6 VLAN 0 frame and a 1518-byte tagged frame heard with their tags, to the byte; links of one EtherType each hearing only theirs (an EtherCAT type, and a tagged PROFINET frame with its tag), a watcher leaving out what this host sends seeing none of it and hearing what comes in; on the sending end and the far end, a link of one EtherType hearing it behind an 802.1Q tag, an 802.1ad tag, and the two-tag orders 802.1ad then 802.1Q, 802.1Q then 802.1ad, 802.1ad twice and 802.1Q twice, and on neither end another type behind one tag or behind two in each of the four orders, nor its own type behind three tags, all twelve frames heard whole by a link with no filter; the tags' own types refused as filters; a 1518-byte frame with an 802.1ad tag refused and with an 802.1Q tag sent; a 1514-byte frame crossing, frames of 14 and 59 bytes crossing whole, and 1515 and 13 bytes refused by name; tags of priority 0 and VLAN 0 (802.1Q, 802.1ad and two of them) put back whole by a plain and a filtered link; a tag a bridge adds on its way out of an interface, which the kernel holds beside the frame on the sending end, put back for a watcher and a filtered link there; at MTU 65 535, frames of 65 536 bytes heard whole untagged and tagged by a plain and a filtered link, and of 65 537 (both) and 65 549 refused with their whole length, and one of 65 537 passed over by a link that leaves out this host's frames, which waits its timeout; an idle link's 300 ms timeout honoured, through a signal at 50 ms and one at 280 ms (ending under 480 ms, not a timeout after the signal), under a stream of this host's frames left out (within 295 to 600 ms), and to the microsecond: twenty waits of 100 µs and twenty of 1500 µs each at least what it asks, the shortest under 900 µs and 1.9 ms (what a wait rounded up to the millisecond would pass); a link that leaves out this host's frames hearing a frame from the wire after a burst of three thousand of them it did not read; links opened over and over while two interfaces flood, a filtered one handed no frame of another type and a plain one no frame of the other interface;
