## C-135: The cyclic frame: a frame id by its range, the IO data as bytes, the APDU status with its bits named
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", the frames a PROFINET IO controller and device exchange, phase 3; the briefing's PROFINET IO controller in core on aranha's codecs, P-018)
- **Description:** `profinet::rt::CyclicFrame` reads and writes PROFINET IO's cyclic real-time frame: the frame id, the C_SDU as bytes (40 to 1440; its layout of data and status bytes is the connection's, not the frame's), and the APDU status (cycle counter, data status, transfer status), the status being the last four bytes of a frame that carries no pad; written with the cyclic tag (priority 6, VLAN 0), another tag or none. It reads frames as they travel without PROFINET security: a secured cyclic frame carries nothing that tells it apart from a plain one (only its connection says so), and is read as plain, its security header and checksum as data and the checksum's last four bytes as the status; tshark 4.6.6 reads a cyclic frame as secured when the data's bytes 6 and 7 are its length less 20 (their reserved bits clear), its status then from other bytes (authenticated) or none (encrypted), with no complaint, as pinned; the writer writes the connection's data as given. A frame id is read by its range as Wireshark's dissector reads it (RT class 3 0x0100 to 0x0FFF, RT class 1 0x8000 to 0xBFFF, its legacy range 0xC000 to 0xFBFF, the two alarm ids, DCP's four, everything else not cyclic); a frame id that is not a cyclic frame's, or data outside 40 to 1440 bytes, is refused both ways; any bytes decode or are refused without a panic, and a decoded frame writes back to its own bytes. The data status bits are named as the standard and the dissector read them (primary, redundancy, valid, run, station without problem, ignore at bit 7; bits 3 and 6 reserved), and a provider or consumer status byte's (good, who decided, extension).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/rt.rs, crates/veredas-aranha/tests/common/mod.rs | a frame to the byte both ways, tagged with the cyclic tag (64 bytes) and with others (priority 5, drop eligible, VLAN 100; VLAN 4094), untagged (60), each with its addresses where they go and read back, the longest (1440 bytes of data); twenty-two frame ids against their classes (the alarm ids bounded either side); eight ids that are not cyclic and data of 0 (a frame id and the status alone among them), 39 and 1441 bytes refused both ways, both refusals' messages; twenty thousand noisy payloads of every length up to 1599 bytes decoded or refused without a panic, decoded exactly when the id is a cyclic frame's and the data 40 to 1440 bytes whatever the status (over five thousand), each decoded one written back to its bytes; the ten frames tshark reads (the legacy range, other statuses, the last cycle counter among them) read back by this codec as written; each data status bit alone named, the reserved bits naming nothing, the good status 0x35; each status byte bit alone (good, who decided, extension) and its reserved bits, the good and bad status bytes 0x80 and 0x00; tshark reading four frames' destinations and sources, ids (RT class 1, 3 and legacy), cycle counters, data status bytes and named bits, and transfer status, and six more frames whose statuses set each bit apart, each of the dissector's bits held to this codec's accessor, with another tag, with no complaint; in each of four ranges (RT class 3, RT class 1, its legacy range, its last part) at 40 and 1440 bytes, data whose bytes 6 and 7 are its length less 20 read by this codec as written and by tshark as secured with no complaint: authenticated, its cycle counter from data bytes n-16 and n-15 and its transfer status from n-13, encrypted (bit 0 of byte 0 set), no cycle counter; and four neighbours (the length less 19 and 21, either reserved bit set) read plain by tshark, the status as written (48 frames)
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-135.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-136: The alarm frame: the RTA header, the notification and its acknowledgement, what is not read kept with its reason
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", the alarm frame, phase 3; the briefing's PROFINET IO controller in core on aranha's codecs, P-018)
- **Description:** `profinet::alarm::RtaPdu` reads and writes PROFINET IO's alarm frame, the acyclic real-time protocol on frame id 0xFC01 (high priority, tagged priority 6) and 0xFE01 (low, priority 5): the header (the destination and source alarm endpoints, the PDU type and version nibbles, AddFlags with the window size, TACK at bit 4 and the reserved bits kept as heard, the send and acknowledge sequence numbers, the var part's length) and the var part: a DATA PDU's one block at version 1.0, an alarm notification (0x0001 high, 0x0002 low: alarm type, API, slot, subslot, module and submodule ident, the alarm specifier with its sequence and four diagnosis bits named, and the first item's user structure identifier with every byte after it as data, or none: a maintenance item's data holds the item it qualifies, and an item of several diagnoses under one identifier holds them all as its data) or an alarm acknowledgement (0x8001, 0x8002: the alarm it answers and a PNIO status); an ACK and a NACK carrying nothing; an ERR carrying a PNIO status. Helpers write a notification and an acknowledgement as DATA with TACK, the transport's acknowledgement and an error without; the sequence numbers and the window are carried as numbers, the alarm connection's to keep, and its retries are its alone. Decoding is total: a damaged header (shorter than the header, a frame id not an alarm's, a var part past the bytes or past what a frame carries) is an error naming it, and an alarm frame with PROFINET security (0xFC41, 0xFE41) is refused as one, not read; a var part this codec does not read (a PDU type outside 1 to 4, a var part that does not fit its type, a block of another type, of the other priority than its frame, of a version other than 1.0, or of a length that does not fit) is kept as heard with its reason and written back as heard; bytes after the var part are the frame's pad and are not kept; any bytes decode or are refused without a panic, and a decoded PDU writes back to its own bytes without the pad. A PDU that would be read back as another (a typed body under a PDU type that does not carry it, a type spelled as another's nibble, bytes kept with a reason they would not be read with) is refused, as are a type or version past a nibble and a var part past what a frame carries (1486 bytes). Wireshark's dissector reads field for field, with no complaint, the frames this codec writes of typed parts whose item, if any, is under an identifier the dissector decodes (any below 0x8000, and 0x8000, 0x8002, 0x8003, 0x8100, 0x8300 to 0x8303, 0x8310) and fits its structure (as the dissector reads it, one diagnosis per identifier; the item's data is the device's to shape, not checked here), and whose PNIO status it knows; it reads an item of several diagnoses under one identifier as one diagnosis and takes the next one's channel number for another identifier, silent (or calls it malformed when that number is 0x8000), as pinned; it complains, as pinned, of an item that does not fit (a channel diagnosis with no data), of an item under an identifier it names and does not decode (iParameter's, as undecoded data), of a status it does not know (an ErrorDecode it has no table for, an ErrorCode1 it does not know under PNIO's), and of three var parts kept unread and written as heard (a DATA PDU with no block, a PDU type outside 1 to 4, a block of version 2.0), while it reads others its own way, silent (a notification of the other priority than its frame, as a notification), so it is no oracle for what this codec keeps unread; and it reads TACK as the whole high nibble of AddFlags, so where reserved bits are set its reading and this codec's differ, as pinned.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/alarm.rs, crates/veredas-aranha/tests/common/mod.rs | a notification with a channel diagnosis to the byte both ways, its frame tagged priority 6 (66 bytes) with its addresses where they go and read back; a low-priority notification with no item to the byte, its frame padded to 60 and the pad not kept, untagged 60; an item of an identifier alone; an alarm acknowledgement to the byte, a low one with an error status, the transport's acknowledgement, an error and a NACK to the byte both ways, a pad of 30 bytes not kept; the frame ids, tags, block types, header and longest var part; AddFlags of both helpers and six bytes read (window, TACK, reserved bits); the alarm specifier's bits each alone, the reserved bit 14 naming nothing, the longest sequence; the PNIO status both ways; seven PDU type nibbles read; twenty-six var parts each kept with its exact reason and written back as heard, the reasons' words; header damage named (two short lengths, a frame id not an alarm's, an overrun by one, a var part of 1487 bytes, unread and typed, and of 65 535) with its messages, the two secured alarm frame ids refused as secured with the message and their four neighbours as not alarms', a var part of 1486 bytes read and written back; every cut of four written PDUs; twenty thousand noisy PDUs with a pad, each decoded and written back to its bytes without the pad, more than five hundred reaching a typed body, and two thousand of var parts up to 2199 bytes, each decoded one written back and each other refused as past what a frame carries, more than five hundred of each; eleven typed bodies under a type that does not carry them or a type spelled as another's nibble, and five var parts kept with a reason they would not be read with, refused as not themselves, the same bytes with their own reason written; the nibbles refused with a message, PDU versions 0, 2 and 15 carried; a var part of 1486 bytes written (1500 bytes of payload, 1518 on the wire), 1487 refused typed and not and 70 028 typed, 1487's message checked; a PDU read from JSON gated alike; tshark reading seven frames (two notifications, two acknowledgements, the transport's acknowledgement, an error, a NACK): destination, source, tag priority, frame id, endpoints, PDU type and version, window, TACK, sequence numbers, var part length, the block's type, length and version, every notification and acknowledgement field with the specifier's bits, the PNIO statuses, the channel diagnosis item field by field, with no complaint; and its TACK read as 15 and 14 where this codec reads bit 4 of AddFlags 0xF1 and 0xE1; a maintenance item followed by an extended channel diagnosis read back by this codec as one item of 0x8100 holding the second, and by tshark as both identifiers with no complaint; two channel diagnoses under 0x8000 and two extended ones under 0x8002 read back by this codec as one item each, and by tshark as the identifier and the next diagnosis's channel number taken for another (2), with no complaint, and with that channel number 0x8000 called malformed; a channel diagnosis item with no data called malformed, a DATA PDU with no block a dissector failure, a PDU type 5 malformed, a block of version 2.0 not implemented; an error of ErrorDecode 0x82 and one of ErrorCode1 0 under PNIO's each complained of by name, a notification of the other priority kept unread by this codec and read as a notification by tshark with no complaint; and a seeded batch of three hundred typed frames (notifications with no item, a manufacturer's item of up to 39 bytes or a channel diagnosis, acknowledgements with a good status, transport acknowledgements, either priority) read by tshark with no complaint, their endpoints, sequence numbers and every block field (type, length and version, the alarm's place, the specifier's sequence and four bits, the idents, the item's identifier, an acknowledgement's status) as written; an iParameter item read as undecoded data
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-136.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-137: PNIO over connectionless DCE/RPC: the header and the arguments in either byte order, the UUIDs, the blocks, the record read's headers
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", PNIO over connectionless DCE/RPC, phase 3, and phase 6's implicit read of the port data; the briefing's PROFINET IO controller in core on aranha's codecs, P-018)
- **Description:** `profinet::rpc` reads and writes one connectionless DCE/RPC datagram as PNIO uses it over UDP (port 34964): the 80-byte header (version 4; the packet type, the eleven DCE/RPC defines (0 to 10) named; both flag bytes; the data representation, NDR's format label (an integer and a character format of 0 or 1 each, a float format of 0 to 3), its integer format saying the byte order; a packet type past 10, a bit of the second flags byte but 0 and 1, and a data representation that is not NDR's are refused both ways by name, as Wireshark takes a datagram holding one for no DCE/RPC at all; the serial number's two bytes apart; the object, interface and activity UUIDs; server boot, interface version, sequence, operation, the two hints, the fragment number, the authentication protocol), the body its length counts, and bytes after the body kept. The header's integers, the UUIDs' first three fields, PNIO's argument counts and a response's PNIO status (a 32-bit value, error code to code 2 from its most significant byte) follow the header's byte order; the blocks are big-endian always, their UUIDs in text order. A UUID is its text (8, 4, 4, 4 and 12 hexadecimal digits joined by hyphens, either case read, lower case written); PNIO's four interfaces and the endpoint mapper are named, and a PNIO object UUID is made from and read into its instance, device id and vendor id. PNIO's arguments: a request's maximum, length, maximum count, offset and data, a response's status in place of the maximum, the actual count written as the data's length, bytes after the data kept. Blocks are read in order until one is not whole (a header cut short, a length under 2 or past the data), whose bytes are kept; a block past its 16-bit length is refused. The record read's request and answer headers (blocks 0x0009 and 0x8009, version 1.0, 60 bytes, padding zero) are read only from their own block; a request's target AR travels only under the nil AR (an implicit read), and under another AR its place is padding, so a request naming both is refused. Helpers write an implicit read (operation 5 on the device interface, idempotent; a request under an AR that is not nil refused) and the answer to a read (a response, last fragment, no fragment acknowledgement wanted, in the request's byte order with its object, interface, activity, sequence and operation; the read header echoing the request's sequence, AR, API, slot, subslot and index, then the record). A datagram shorter than the header, past what UDP over IPv4 carries (65 507 bytes), of another version, or whose body runs past it is an error naming it, and so are arguments too short or whose data runs past the body; a datagram past 65 507 bytes is refused on writing too. Any bytes decode or name their damage without a panic, and a decoded datagram writes back to its own bytes. A datagram is one fragment: joining fragments, retrying and following the port an answer comes from are the caller's. Wireshark's dissectors read field for field, with no complaint, the datagrams its helpers write whose record is its index's block and whose status the dissector knows (the record and the status are the device's to shape; a record that is not its index's block the dissector calls malformed, and a status it does not know it warns of, both as pinned), the arguments' counts and the status in the header's byte order, and pair each answer with its request.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/rpc.rs, crates/veredas-aranha/tests/common/mod.rs | a UUID's text both ways and in either case, its bytes in either byte order and back, nine malformed texts refused with a message, serde as its text; PNIO's four interfaces, the endpoint mapper and the nil UUID spelled; a PNIO object UUID made and read back, twelve other UUIDs (ten differing from its prefix in one byte each, in each of its fields) read as none; an implicit read of I&M0 to the byte, little-endian, both ways, its arguments and its one block read back; the same big-endian, its header's integers, UUIDs and counts turned and its block not; its answer to the byte (status, counts, the read header, the I&M0 record after it), read back, split into header and record; a refusal's status in the header's byte order and a header with no record; a big-endian answer in the request's order; header damage named (two short lengths, version 5, a body overrun by one) with its messages; a datagram that is its header alone (80 bytes) written and read back; trailing bytes kept and written back; the serial number's bytes apart; the eleven packet types carried, named, and the other 245 refused both ways with the message; the flag bits and operation numbers; the four format labels' byte order (0x00 and 0x01 big-endian, 0x10 and 0x11 little-endian, a float format of 3 carried), every other first byte of the data representation (252), float formats 4 to 255 and second flags bytes 4 to 255 refused both ways, each value kept read back; tshark reading eight refused values (two packet types, two second flags bytes, three first bytes, a float format) as no DCE/RPC and five kept ones as DCE/RPC; a datagram of 65 507 bytes written and read back, of 65 508 (a trailing byte more) and 65 615 refused on writing and of 65 508 on reading, with its message; every cut of a datagram and twenty thousand noisy ones (most a DCE/RPC header, their arguments' actual count fitting, some a read answer's header heading the data; more than two thousand arguments read and two hundred answers split) decoding or naming their damage, each decoded one, its arguments read either way and its body's and its arguments' data read as blocks (each tried as a read header, the answer's data split), written back to its own bytes, more than two thousand decoded; empty arguments of a request and of a response (20 bytes) written and read back in either byte order; arguments too short or overrun in either byte order, data and trailing bytes kept, a status of four different bytes in each byte order, hand-written counts kept but the actual count, a request's and an answer's written to the byte in either order and read back from their own places; two blocks to the byte, seven tails that are not whole blocks kept (two of a length under 2 with their header whole), a body of 65533 written and 65534 refused, with messages; the read headers built and read back (an implicit read's target AR, an AR and the answer's additional values at their bytes), a target AR under an AR refused, an AR whose first half is zero among them, and three with their second half zero or one byte at either end, each with a target refused, its target's place read only when zero and no implicit read under it, and two target ARs of one byte at either end refused under each, the target's place under an AR read only when zero (two bytes set refused, and one under that half-zero AR), an implicit read under an AR refused, with messages; refused from another type, either header's type, three other versions, a byte short or long (each header both ways), header bodies of 0, 10, 27, 30, 57 and 59 bytes of their own type and version refused with no panic, eight padding bytes set, with messages, and a read answer's data shorter than its header, beginning with a block of another length (named), or a request's header refused, the response's refusal message checked; tshark reading four datagrams in UDP (an implicit read of I&M0 and its answer, little-endian; of the port data and its refusal, big-endian): the version, packet type, flags, byte order, the three UUIDs, interface version, sequence, operation, hints, body length, fragment number, serial bytes, the arguments' maximum, length, maximum count, offset and actual count, the status, the read header's type, length, version, sequence, AR, API, slot, subslot, index, record length, target AR or additional values, the I&M0 record read after it, the refusal's code, each answer paired with its request, with no complaint; and read by tshark besides: a read under an AR (its AR, and no target AR, read) answered with a refusal of four different bytes, little-endian, read whole with the answer echoing the AR, API 5, slot 3 and the read's operation (by tshark too); a datagram with every header field distinct and not zero (flags, serial bytes, server boot, sequence, both hints, fragment, authentication protocol) to the byte and read by tshark; an answer with additional values 0x1122 and 0x3344; and an I&M0 answer whose record is not a block called malformed; an answer with a status the dissector does not know (ErrorDecode 0x82) read as written and warned of; not dissected, an answer to a request on the controller's interface on that interface, its operation echoed and the interface's bytes in the request's order
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-137.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-138: The records a read returns: I&M0 and the port's real data, its peers and line delays, read from their own block
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", the I&M records and the port's neighbour data, phase 3, and phase 6's device-to-device links from the port data; the trap "Passive LLDP gives nodes, not edges")
- **Description:** `profinet::record` reads and writes two records of a PNIO read, each a block of version 1.0 read only from its own block: I&M0 (index 0xAFF0, block 0x0020: vendor id, an order id of 20 and a serial number of 16 visible characters kept as sent with their padding, hardware revision, software revision as its prefix and three numbers, revision counter, profile id and type, I&M version, and the supported I&M records with I&M 1 to 15 named), and the port's real data (index 0x802A, block 0x020F: slot, subslot, the own port id, each peer's port id, chassis id, delay word and MAC, the MAU type, the domain and multicast boundaries, the link state's port and link, the medium, and bytes after the medium kept as heard), its fields aligned to four bytes from the block's start with zero padding, a peer's delay read as a line delay or, with bit 31 set, a cable delay in nanoseconds. Texts are visible characters (space to tilde). A block of another type or version, an I&M0 of another length, a port's data that ends before a field or has padding that is not zero (each named by the field after it: the MAU type after the last peer, or after the count when there is none), or text that is not visible characters, is refused by name; written, an I&M0 text of the wrong length or not visible, a port's text past 255 bytes or not visible, more than 255 peers, or a port's data past what a block's length counts (65 533 bytes), is refused. Wireshark's dissectors read both records inside a read answer field for field with no complaint.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/record.rs, crates/veredas-aranha/tests/common/mod.rs | I&M0 with every two-byte number past a byte (vendor 0x1234, hardware revision 0x0A0B, revision counter 0x0205, profile 0xF600 and type 0x0304, supported 0xF01E), the revision's numbers and the version's two bytes all different (17, 14, 9; 1.3), every number's bytes found once in the body, and texts padded with spaces at either end, to the byte both ways and from a read block sequence, the texts read back as sent; its version's two bytes in their order, a version of 2.1 read back whole; I&M 1 to 4 and 12 to 15 named supported on the block read (bit 0 and past 15 naming nothing); texts with tildes, and a space and a tilde prefix, written and read back; the indexes, block types and text lengths; written with an order id one short, one long, with a tab and with an accented letter, a serial number one short and with a byte under space, and a prefix under space or past tilde, refused with the exact error; read with a byte past tilde and one under space in the order id, a zero in the serial number, a prefix under space or past tilde, another version or type by either byte (2.0, 0.0, 1.1, 0x0120, 0x8020, 0x0021), a byte long or short, refused with the exact error and message; the port's data at slot 0x0102 with one peer and with none to the byte both ways, with two peers of odd lengths (a cable delay of 0x12345 ns and a line delay of 0x10007 ns, a dotted port id, tildes in the chassis ids, a MAU type 0x011E, boundaries 0x0100_0003 and 0x8000_0010, link down, medium 0x0100_0002, four bytes after the medium kept) aligned to the byte at each field and read back, a peer's MAC of six different bytes in order, a peer's delay word read both ways, five delay words at the edges of bits 30 and 31 (0x7FFF_FFFF, 0x8000_0000, 0xC000_0001, 0x4000_0000, 0xFFFF_FFFF) read as their format and nanoseconds and written and read back, the link up for link 1 alone among 0 to 8, a chassis id with spaces at either end read as sent, texts with a tilde, texts of 255 bytes with 100 peers and 255 peers written and read back, a port's data of 65 533 bytes written and read back, one of 65 534 and 255 peers with texts of 255 bytes refused with the error and its message; written with texts of 256 bytes, a character past tilde, under space or accented refused with the exact error, and 256 peers refused with an error of its own and its message; read with another version or type by either byte (2.0, 0.0, 1.1, 0x010F, 0x820F, 0x0240) refused, every cut of a one-peer body naming fourteen fields in their order, of a two-peer body nineteen (the pad between the peers before the next peer) and of a body with no peer nine (the pad after the count before the MAU type), fifteen padding bytes set each naming the field after it (pads of one, two and three bytes, each byte of the three; the MAU type after the last peer and with none), a text byte past ASCII and one under space refused, with messages; every byte 0 to 255 at one place of each of the six texts (order id, serial number, prefix, own port id, a peer's port id and chassis id), written and read, taken exactly when it is a visible character and refused naming its field otherwise; bytes after the medium of every length 1 to 8, zero runs among them, written to the byte at the block's end and read back, two of them (one and four zeros) read by tshark with no complaint; two thousand seeded records of each kind (every number over its whole width, random visible texts, up to three peers, up to eight bytes after the medium) and records of all ones and all zeros written and read back as they were; tshark reading five read answers (I&M0, and the port's data with none, one, two and three peers, the three holding the delay word's edges: the largest line delay, a cable delay of 0, one with bit 30 set) with no complaint: every I&M0 field at its value, and the read header's slot (the request's) and the port's own, the subslot, own port id and its length, the number of peers, each peer's port and chassis ids and their lengths, delay word, its format and its line or cable delay, and MAC, the MAU type, both boundaries, the link state's port and link, and the medium
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-138.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-139: The wire seam: a frame with its interface and stamp; the capture file read and written as tshark reads it, a capture replayed, the memory hub, the raw link behind the same seam
- **Date:** 2026-11-23
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", the wire: one seam every frame passes through, three kinds behind it; phase 4; Veredas 1's `wire.rs` and `pcap.rs`, absorbed)
- **Description:** `wire::Wire` is the seam every frame aranha hears or sends passes through: a name, the interface's MAC (none for a capture), `send` of one whole frame (14 to 65 536 bytes, refused by its length before any kind sees it, never blocking) and `recv`, waiting at most its timeout for the next `Frame`: its bytes, the interface that heard it, its stamp in nanoseconds since the Unix epoch (0 before it, the largest past what 64 bits count) and its length on the wire. A wire hands back the frames that arrived, never one this host sent: the raw link (C-134) behind the seam passes over its host's frames within the same wait (one deadline, however many there are, a flood faster than it reads included) even when opened to hear them, and its own errors (the interface's MTU refusing a frame among them) are carried as they are. `wire::pcap` (a capture of frames: not portal's capture rules of C-038, which read text lines into series, nor the session a project records to the drive, C-046) reads classic pcap as tshark 4.6.6 reads it: either byte order; the microsecond, nanosecond and modified magic numbers (the modified format's record headers eight bytes longer); major version 2 or later (1.x refused); a record's two lengths in version 2.4's order (bytes kept, then length on the wire) from 2.4 on, the other way round before 2.3, and at 2.3 taken so that the bytes kept are the smaller; the nanosecond magic counting nanoseconds from 2.4 on and microseconds before it; AIX's format (version 2.2, the microsecond magic, link type 6) as Ethernet stamped in nanoseconds; the link type Ethernet when its low 16 bits are 1 and its reserved bits (16 to 25) are clear, bits 26 to 31 (a check sequence's flag and length) kept and the frames kept as recorded; the snapshot length not held against the records; a length on the wire under the bytes kept kept as stated. It departs from tshark on purpose in three places: a fraction past a second is carried into the next (tshark keeps it as an invalid fraction and wraps it past 2^31 nanoseconds); a length on the wire past 2 147 483 647 is kept as stated (tshark shows 2 147 483 647); and under the microsecond and modified magic numbers it reads the one layout each names, where tshark also tries the layouts of 1999's patched tcpdumps on the first records (RedHat 6.1's and Nokia's under the microsecond magic, SuSE 6.3's under the modified) and keeps the one that reads best, so a capture in such a layout is read otherwise here (the nanosecond magic, which it writes, has one layout for both). pcapng (named, with the way out), another magic number, a link type with a reserved bit set, another link type and a header under 24 bytes are refused by name; a record header or a record's bytes cut short, or a record keeping more than 262 144 bytes, ends the capture there, with the frames before it and the damage named (the record, its offset, what is wrong). It writes nanosecond little-endian pcap 2.4, snapshot length 262 144, Ethernet, each frame's bytes and length on the wire; a stamp past 2106, a frame over 262 144 bytes or a length on the wire past 32 bits is refused. `pcap::Replay` hands a capture's frames back in order at once whatever the timeout, then says it has ended, keeping what is sent. `wire::hub::Hub` is a segment in memory: every port hears every other port's frames and never its own, stamped with the hub's clock (the test's, from 0) and the hearing port's name; a port waits at most its timeout (a timeout past what the clock counts waits for a frame), keeps at most 4096 frames unread, letting the oldest go and counting them, and leaves the segment when dropped.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/wire.rs, crates/veredas-aranha/tests/common/mod.rs | five frames (a DCP Identify, an LLDP frame, a tagged frame, a frame cut to 60 of 1514 bytes in the last second a capture counts, a 14-byte frame at stamp 0) written to the byte (the file header, a record, the cut frame's record header) and read back whole, from bytes and by path, an empty capture, a missing file named; tshark reading the written file with no complaint, each frame's stamp to the nanosecond, length on the wire, bytes kept and bytes; tshark's own nanosecond and microsecond rewrites of it read back (stamps to the microsecond in the second), its pcapng rewrite refused with the message; files written by hand in both byte orders and both resolutions read as tshark reads them (stamps, lengths, bytes), a snapshot length of 20 not held against a 60-byte frame; sixty files by hand (versions 2.0, 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 2.4, 2.5 and 3.0, the three magic numbers, both byte orders at 2.2, 2.3 and 2.4, each holding two frames cut by their capture, written in either order of the two lengths, and a whole one between) read as tshark reads them, frame for frame (stamp, both lengths, bytes) and damage for damage, the order each version reads, the resolution by magic and version, the magic and byte order kept; versions 2.65535, 3.3 and 65535.0 read, 1.0, 0.9 and 1.65535 refused; ten link types with bits 26 to 31 set or clear read as Ethernet with no complaint, as by tshark (a check sequence kept with its frame, the field whole), four with a reserved bit refused as tshark refuses them, six others (0, 6, 101, 105, 257, 0x8001) refused and none read by tshark as Ethernet; AIX's format in either byte order read in nanoseconds as by tshark, and eight files near it (the nanosecond magic, version 2.4, link type 9, the modified magic, versions 3.2 and 4.2, link type 6 with a check sequence's bits) refused and not read by tshark as Ethernet; tshark's own rewrites in RedHat 6.1's and SuSE 6.3's layouts read whole by tshark and otherwise by this reader, their magic kept (the departure); five other magic numbers refused; headers of 0, 4 and 23 bytes refused, with messages; the departures: four fractions past a second (1 000 000 and 2 147 484 microseconds, 1 500 000 000 and 4 294 967 295 nanoseconds) carried, where tshark's stamp differs, and two under a second read alike, lengths on the wire of 2^31 − 1, 2^31 and 2^32 − 1 kept and written again, where tshark shows 2 147 483 647 for each; a capture cut in a record's header and in its bytes giving the two frames before, its damage and its message, tshark reading the same two and saying the file was cut short; a modified capture cut 20 bytes into a record header of 24 giving the frame before, its bytes as written, and naming 20 of 24, tshark reading one (in SuSE 6.3's layout) and saying it was cut short; a record keeping 262 144 bytes read and one keeping 262 145 ending the capture, with its message; every cut of a written capture reading a prefix of its frames, the damage named unless the cut falls between records; twenty thousand noisy captures read, written and read back; a length on the wire under the bytes kept kept as stated (tshark reading the same lengths), a fraction past a second carried at both resolutions, a last record keeping no bytes read (by tshark too); writing refused past 2106 (and at the largest stamp), past 262 144 bytes and past 32 bits of length, the last second and the largest length written, with messages; a replay handing five frames back in order at once (within a second for five waits of a minute), then ending with its message, keeping two sends and refusing 13 and 65 537 bytes, with messages; a hub of three ports (two sharing a name) carrying each frame to the others only, stamped with the clock and the hearer's name, a clone of it the same segment, a dropped port gone and a new one hearing, lengths refused, 65 536 bytes carried, 4099 frames to a port that does not read keeping the last 4096 and counting 3; a hub port waiting 50 ms for nothing (at least 50 ms, under 500), woken by a frame sent from another thread, and waiting with a timeout past the clock; on a veth pair in a private network of the test's own, the raw link as a `dyn Wire`: its name and MAC, a frame from the far end handed back with its interface, a stamp between two readings of the clock and its length, this host's frames on the interface passed over (the wait ending empty at its timeout, and between 300 ms and 1.5 s for a wait of 300 ms under a stream of them every 20 ms and under a flood from three senders in a tight loop), lengths refused by the seam, and the MTU's refusal carried as the link's own error, with its message; a stamp: the epoch 0, a moment before it 0, nanoseconds to the largest count, past it the largest
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-139.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-140: What a frame says: observations about one device each with their witness, the frame's class, its damage named
- **Date:** 2026-11-24
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", listening: a frame decoded into observations, one thing one frame says about one device, with its witness, and what it cannot decode counted by class, never dropped unseen; "Rules": frames kept verbatim, decoded on demand; phase 4; Veredas 1's `observe.rs`, absorbed)
- **Description:** `observe::decode` reads one kept frame (a `wire::Frame` and its index among the kept frames) into its class, the observations it holds and the damage it holds, each named. Observed, passively: a DCP answer or Hello from a station (heard: the identity its typed blocks give), a DCP request (service type 0 under Identify, Get or Set, a request by its selection bit as the codec reads one: the asker, its identity empty), an LLDP PDU (its source, the port's MAC on a PROFINET device, and the PDU whole), an ARP request or reply of Ethernet and IPv4 (its sender claiming its address; a probe's sender of 0.0.0.0 claims none). Each observation carries its witness: what was heard (`dcp-heard`, `dcp-request-heard`, `lldp-heard`, `arp-heard`), the interface and time the frame was kept with, and the frame's index; and names the MAC of the device it is about (a DCP or ARP sender; an LLDP PDU's chassis MAC TLV, else its chassis id when that is a MAC, else its source). A frame is classed by its EtherType inside one 802.1Q tag (a frame under a second tag by that tag's type; `dcp`, `cyclic` for RT class 1, 3 and the legacy range, `alarm`, `profinet-other`, `lldp`, `arp`, `ipv4`, `ipv6`, `ethercat`, `powerlink`, `mrp`, `ptp`, `other:0x....` for any other type or 802.3 length, `malformed` for no Ethernet frame), its name, exactly as written, read back as the class (an unknown type's only when the type has no class of its own). Cyclic, alarm and every other frame is classed and counted, never observed. Damage is named and never fatal: a frame a capture cut (kept fewer bytes than the wire carried: it says nothing, what the cut left out being maybe what it would say, and is classed still), a DCP block that does not fit its kind and bytes after a DCP PDU's last block (the rest observed), a DCP or LLDP PDU that is none, a DCP PDU of a service DCP does not define (3 to 6) or a service type none of 0, 1 and 5 (as tshark leaves it undecoded), an LLDP PDU naming a group address for its chassis (a group address is never one device) (nothing observed), a DCP or LLDP frame or an ARP sender from a group address (nothing observed), an ARP that is short or not Ethernet's and IPv4's or of another operation, a PROFINET frame too short for its id, no Ethernet frame. Any bytes give a class without a panic.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/observe.rs, crates/veredas-aranha/tests/common/mod.rs | an Identify answer observed with its witness (probe, interface, time, index) and the identity its blocks give (name, vendor id, address), the same untagged and under two single tags, its subject the device; a Hello heard; four requests (Identify all and by name, Get, Set) observed as the asker with an empty identity; a Set answer heard; a damaged IP block named with the name still observed, a data length overrun named with nothing observed, an answer from a group address named with nothing observed; four LLDP PDUs (a PROFINET port's with its chassis MAC, a switch's with a MAC chassis id, a bare one, and one with both differing) observed whole with their source, their subjects the chassis MAC, the MAC chassis id, the port's MAC and the chassis MAC TLV over the chassis id; an unfit chassis id and a group source named with nothing observed; an ARP request and reply observed as their sender, to the byte; a probe claiming nothing and naming no damage, a gratuitous one observed; a cut ARP, another hardware type, another address length, another operation and a group sender named with their messages; fourteen frames of other classes (cyclic in three ranges, alarms of both priorities, two other PROFINET ids, IPv4, IPv6, EtherCAT, POWERLINK, MRP, PTP, an unknown type) classed with nothing observed and no damage, an 802.3 length classed by it, a PROFINET frame too short for its id and a 13-byte frame named; the fifteen class names both ways and four that are none refused; a capture replayed and a hub port observed with the frame's interface, time and index; thirty thousand noisy frames (five frames of the observed kinds with up to three bytes flipped and cut at random, a quarter random bytes under seven types) classed without a panic, observing only unicast stations, over a thousand observations and a thousand damaged frames; every service and service type byte (65 536 each) on a request's shape and an answer's: a service DCP does not define and a service type none of 0, 1 and 5 named with nothing observed, a request its asker with nothing said, the rest heard; the critic's four (a name filter under service type 4, a Set under 2, service 7, a frame id and zeros) named; three bytes after the last block named with the PDU observed; an LLDP frame cut by its capture at 55, 56, 73 and 119 of 120 bytes (its chassis MAC TLV left out) named with nothing observed, through a capture file too, a cut DCP and cyclic frame classed; a group address for the chassis, by the TLV and by a MAC chassis id, named with nothing observed; an answer under two tags classed by the second's type; an unpadded 42-byte ARP observed, an operation of 0, a protocol of 0x86DD and a hardware address length of 8 named; six class names that are none (a plus sign, upper-case digits, four types of their own class) refused and another type's taken
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-140.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-141: Fusion: devices by their MAC, every value kept with its witnesses, conflicts on record
- **Date:** 2026-11-24
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", fusion: observations into devices, the MAC the identity, every field keeping every value it was given with the witnesses for each, a disagreement a conflict on record; "Rules": it never guesses identity, a name is never a key; phase 4; Veredas 1's `fusion.rs`, absorbed)
- **Description:** `fusion::Fusion` applies observations (C-140) to devices keyed by the MAC each names, and to no other key (the devices heard on a segment, kept in memory; not comissionador's run record of C-204): an LLDP PDU naming no MAC for its chassis (neither PROFINET's chassis MAC nor a chassis id of subtype MAC; a shutdown PDU holds the mandatory three alone) is the device's whose port its source is (a MAC any of that port's frames came from, the MAC's own device aside), when exactly one device's is, else its source's, kept there as a port not yet known until a PDU from that MAC names its device, which takes it in; a local chassis id equal to a station name known from DCP joins nothing. A device holds DCP's station name, alias, IP suite and the device's own word on it, vendor and device ids, vendor text, role and instance; LLDP's chassis id (as a key, `mac:...`, `local:...`), system name, description and capabilities, its IPv4 management addresses (each PDU's, a field like any), and its ports by their short name (each with the MAC its frames come from, description, PROFINET port status, delays and MRP status, port VLAN, MAU type, the interfaces it was heard on, its frames and times, and when it said it was leaving, a time to live of 0, unless a later frame came, by stamp then index); the addresses its ARP claimed; what it was heard doing (answering or asking DCP, LLDP, ARP), on which interfaces, its first and last times and frames. A station that asks is on record with nothing said of it. Every field keeps every value it was given, in the order first heard (by its first witness's stamp, then index), each with its first and last witness and its count; its current value is the one heard last (by the frame's stamp, then its index), whatever order the observations are applied in. Its label is its station name, else its LLDP system name, else its MAC, an empty name none; its addresses are its current DCP, ARP and management addresses, 0.0.0.0 none. Conflicts are on record, each once, ordered by kind and value: two devices of one non-empty current station name (`duplicate-name`), two devices holding one address (`duplicate-ip`), a device that has named itself two ways (`name-changed`, its non-empty names in the order heard) or held two DCP addresses (`ip-changed`, the addresses alone, 0.0.0.0 none, in the order heard), a device whose current word flags its own address in conflict (`reports-ip-conflict`, with its address, none for 0.0.0.0). The same observations in any order give the same devices, but where a MAC is a device of its own (it speaks DCP or ARP, or a PDU names it as its chassis) before a PDU names it another device's port, and where two devices' ports share a MAC.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/fusion.rs | a Hello and an Identify answer of one device fused into one, every DCP field current, its name once with count 2 and its first and last witnesses (stamp, index, probe), its times and frames, what it was heard doing and its label; an asking station on record with nothing said of it; a device renamed and readdressed, an older hearing applied last and two hearings of one stamp told apart by their frames, the current value by time, every value with its count and witnesses, the two conflicts with their values, and the same observations in the same order giving an equal fusion; an LLDP PDU's chassis key, system name, description and capabilities, management address, its port by short name with its MAC, description, PROFINET status, VLAN, interfaces, frames and times; a label by system name until a station name is heard; the port heard on a second interface, a shutdown PDU with no chassis MAC fused into the device whose port it comes from (one device, the port leaving), an older frame not bringing it back and a later one doing so, a second port by its short name; a local chassis id equal to a known station name joining nothing (the port's MAC its own device); a port MAC two devices claim belonging to neither; nine observations (a leaving heard before its device, renames, a readdress, management addresses moving, a PDU naming no chassis MAC that is no leaving, three devices) reversed and in twenty shuffles giving a fusion equal to the witness, the port's MAC no device, every field, time, interface and count of the device and its port, its delays, MRP and MAU type, the management addresses with their witnesses, its addresses and the conflicts; a leaving against a return in both orders and within one stamp, two leavings out of order; a leaving heard first taken in by its device and the later ones going to it; a port its device's by an older MAC of its frames; a shared port MAC whose later naming PDU takes in nothing; a MAC that spoke DCP keeping its own LLDP; conflicts read from current words (moved by DCP and by ARP, renamed from a name another holds, the names in the order heard whatever the order applied, a flag cleared, a flag on a second address, a mask, a gateway, 0.0.0.0 and an empty name no change, a management address another's duplicate); a PDU naming its source as its chassis, by both or by the chassis id alone, its source's device; a port known before its device taken in whole, the same in both orders (a later leaving, an earlier return against a leaving naming PDU, times before and after, every field a PDU naming no chassis MAC carries, a port of another short name); a host's ARP interface naming its other interface by a chassis id of subtype MAC a device of its own, its address its own; a MAC named as one device's chassis and another's port: its leaving the port's device's, a PDU of it heard before it was the port's staying its own while a later leaving still goes to the port's device, and its leaving applied before anything named it staying its own (the exception); a device flagging a conflict on 0.0.0.0 naming no address; an empty station name no label; an ARP address as a second witness, one device's two witnesses of one address no conflict, 0.0.0.0 no address and no conflict; two devices of one name, a DCP address held by another's ARP and a device flagging its own conflict, on record in order with their values and MACs; empty names no duplicate; the five kinds' names
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-141.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-142: The listener: a worker per wire, every frame kept and fused, a paced and bounded sender only where it listens
- **Date:** 2026-11-24
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", listening: a worker per interface hearing everything before anything is sent, every frame kept verbatim with its interface and time, decoded into observations, what it cannot decode counted by class; "Rules": it listens before it asks, every active step bounded and paced (20 frames a second), frames kept verbatim; phase 4; Veredas 1's `engine.rs` and `capture.rs`, absorbed)
- **Description:** `listen::Listener::start` runs one worker per wire (C-139: a raw link, a capture replayed, a hub port), two wires of one name refused, a rate outside one to a thousand frames a second or no frame kept refused, a wire of any name (a NUL in it) started. Each worker hears its wire; every frame heard is kept verbatim under its interface's name (the wire's, whatever name its frames carried) with its stamp, in order, numbered from 0, up to a bound (200 000 by default; past it the oldest let go, counted, their numbers no longer kept); each is decoded (C-140), its class counted, its damage kept (the last 50, each with its frame's number and interface; damaged frames counted), its observations fused (C-141) unless the frame was sent from one of this machine's own interfaces or they are about one (the wires' MACs: this machine's own LLDP agent and ARP among them), which are left out and counted. A frame handed to the listener for an interface is queued there and sent by that interface's worker alone, paced (20 frames a second by default; the first within the poll, 20 ms by default and at most half a gap, then one a gap after, no slower, after a quiet spell no burst, a frame the wire refuses keeping the pace too), the queue bounded (100 by default; a frame past it refused, never a flood), refused on an interface no worker hears, on one whose worker has ended (with its reason) and at a length no wire carries (outside 14 to 65 536 bytes); a frame sent is kept too, marked sent, stamped when it went; a frame the wire refuses is counted with its reason kept among the damage; whoever hands a frame over may be told once what became of it (kept as sent with its number and stamp, refused with the reason, or never sent). Each interface's account says its MAC, frames heard, sent and refused, queued, never sent (still queued when its worker ended or the listener stopped: said so, the queue emptied), and why its worker ended (a capture read to its end, the wire's error, after which it reads no more, or a panic of its wire's or its own, said with its message), the others going on. A snapshot gives the devices, their conflicts as fusion has them, the counts, the earliest and latest stamps heard, and the numbers of the first frame kept and of the next. Stopping, or dropping it, ends and joins every worker. A capture replayed gives the same devices every time.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/listen.rs | a device's Hello, LLDP, ARP and cyclic frame on a hub: the four kept in order with their bytes, interface and stamp, unsent, numbers 0 to 3 and none at 4; counted by class; three observations fused into one device with its label, frames and what it was heard doing, the cyclic frame saying nothing; the interface's account; a value's witness naming a kept frame that decodes to it again; a capture replayed twice giving equal devices, its end said, its stamps' first and last, no MAC of its own; two of this machine's interfaces on one segment: a request sent on one heard on the other and left out as this machine's (counted, nothing fused), the device hearing it, kept as sent on its interface, the device's answer heard on both and fused onto one device with both interfaces; ten frames at 20 a second spanning at least nine gaps of 50 ms, heard in order, all counted sent; at one a second the first sent at once and three queued, a fourth refused with its message, an interface not heard and lengths of 13 and 65 537 refused, two wires of one name, a rate of 0 and no frame kept refused; sixty damaged frames under a bound of five: five kept, 55 let go and their numbers gone, the last kept to the byte, sixty damaged and the last fifty kept with their numbers and messages; a wire's error ending its worker with its message while the other hears; a wire refusing a send counted with its reason, nothing kept as sent; the defaults; rates of 1 001 refused and 1 000 taken; at two, twenty and a hundred a second and at twenty after a quiet second, measured where the wire is handed each frame, the first within the poll and 15 ms, every gap at least one, twenty gaps spanning at most 100 ms more than twenty; eleven single frames at a thousand a second handed over at different moments of the worker's wait, their median delay at most 5 ms; three frames the wire refuses handed a gap apart; a tracked frame refused told so with the reason, once; a capture read to its end refusing a send with its reason, its queue empty; a wire failing after its first send with four frames queued at one a second: the four never sent, a tracked one told so with the reason, once, the queue empty, the wire read once after the failure and no more; stopping with two queued: never sent, a tracked one told the listener stopped, the interface not ended; a capture recorded as one name replayed as another, every frame kept and counted under the wire's name, the earliest and latest of out-of-order stamps, one frame of two damages one damaged frame with two lines; frames of 14 and 65 536 bytes sent and arriving, each told once where it was kept (the kept frame's bytes, interface and stamp), the account after sending (nothing heard, two sent, none queued, no stamp heard), each stamped between the clocks read before and after; a wire that panics after its first send: that one told sent, the next told never sent with the panic's message, once, the account ended with it and a send after refused; a wire named with a NUL started, and its listener's stop leaving no worker; this machine's own LLDP agent, from one of its MACs naming another for its chassis, left out and counted; two devices of one name and one address, the snapshot's conflicts fusion's, both on record; stopping within two seconds with its ports gone from the segment, and a listener dropped unstopped joining too
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-142.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-143: The listener against the bench's PROFINET devices on real links
- **Date:** 2026-11-24
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Rules": fixture first, the scanner's clauses proven against maquinar's PROFINET bench device on a real link; the real wire in tests, without privilege; phase 4, the listening proven on veth pairs in a private namespace against maquinar's PROFINET device; P-020)
- **Description:** The listener (C-142) on raw links (C-134) at the near ends of two veth pairs in a private namespace hears maquinar's own executable, built from the workspace's sources into a target folder of its own and run on a bench file of two PROFINET devices (C-112) at the far ends, one station name on both: each device is found by its MAC (the one the test set on its interface, not the code's reading of it) on its own interface alone, from its Hello and its LLDP, then from its answer to an Identify by name and its ARP reply, each sent through the listener on that interface; what each states of itself (its name, role, IP suite and the device's word on it, vendor and device ids, vendor text, its port by short name and its chassis, its ARP address) is its current value, the name's first witness its Hello and its last the Identify answer; the shared name is a duplicate-name conflict, and the only one; each interface's account holds its MAC, two frames sent, none refused or queued, no end; nothing of this machine's own is heard and nothing is damaged, to the bench's stop. The bench stopped as its user stops it (its stdin closed) exits 0, and each device's port has said it is leaving. Every frame kept is read by Wireshark's dissectors as the listener classed it, with no complaint: the frames sent from this machine's interface on it, marked sent, in the order sent on that interface; the frames heard from that segment's device, counted by class as the listener counted them, what each device states read by the dissectors against the bench file (not through the codecs the bench and the listener share): every DCP frame's station name, vendor and device ids, address, mask, gateway, vendor text and role, every LLDP frame's chassis (a local one, the station name) and port id; and every Identify sent read as one by that name.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/bench_device.rs | the bench's executable built offline into its own target folder at the path cargo names; the four MACs set by the test before anything opens an interface, and read back; two devices (ids 42 and 261 with the default vendor text, and the defaults with a vendor text of their own; two addresses, one with a gateway; ports 001 and 002; LLDP every 200 ms) on b0 and b1, the listener on a0 and a1: both Hellos and LLDP heard within 10 s, each device's interface its own; an Identify by name and an ARP request from each interface answered within 10 s; label, name unconflicted, role IO device, IP status set, the three probes heard, the name's last witness the Identify answer, IP suites, ARP addresses, addresses, ids, vendor texts, port names, the port heard on its interface not yet left, chassis keys; the one conflict, duplicate-name with both MACs; both accounts; no own frame, no damage; the bench's exit and its running line; both ports left within 5 s; the kept frames numbered from 0, every one dissected without complaint as DCP, LLDP or ARP, sent from this machine's interface or heard from that segment's device, the sent ones DCP then ARP on each interface, the heard ones counted by class as the listener did, each DCP one's name, ids, address, mask, gateway, vendor text and role and each LLDP one's chassis subtype and id and port id as the bench file states them, the sent DCP ones asking by `bomba-1` with no all-selector, the heard count all but the four sent; nothing own or damaged at the end
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-143.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-144: Asking: a DCP Identify through the listener, its answers matched to it
- **Date:** 2026-11-24
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", asking: DCP Identify, all, by station name, by alias, paced, bounded, and only through a listener already running on that interface; "Rules": it listens before it asks, every active step bounded and paced; phase 5: the bench device's answers fused with what was already heard, each value with its witnesses, a staged duplicate name flagged; Veredas 1's `active.rs` Identify-All, absorbed)
- **Description:** `ask::identify` sends a DCP Identify (every device, a station name, an alias) on an interface through a listener already hearing it (C-142), from that interface's MAC to DCP's group address, under a transaction id this process has not used, with a response delay factor of 1 to 6400. It is refused where no worker hears the interface, where its worker has ended, on a wire with no MAC of its own (a capture replayed), past the interface's queue bound, at a delay outside DCP's or a wait past a minute, and when DCP cannot carry the request. The listener tells it what became of its request (kept as sent, with its index and stamp; refused by the wire, with the reason; or never sent, its worker having ended, said as an ended interface); it waits its turn in the interface's queue at the interface's pace and a second more (else the request was not sent), then the devices' spread (the factor in 10 ms steps) and its wait. Its answers are the frames that listener keeps from that interface after the request, not sent by it, under DCP's Identify answer frame id and an answering service type, addressed to that interface's MAC under the request's transaction id, each with its source MAC, kept frame, stamp and the identity its blocks give; Identify answers there to another id or another station are counted apart, and frames let go past the listener's bound before they were read are counted lost. The listener has fused every answer as it came (C-141): a device silent until asked is found by its answer, and one heard before has its values witnessed by both.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/ask.rs | on a hub, a device of the test's own answering what it matches and sending four frames besides (another id, another station, another frame id, a request's service type), the listener sending an answer of its own meanwhile, a second interface of this machine on the hub: the request kept as sent with its bytes, stamp, source and destination, its one answer from the asking interface with its identity and stamp, two others, none lost, the device fused and heard on both interfaces; by name and by alias answered under new ids, by a name it does not hold none; at one a second behind three queued frames, sent fourth three seconds after the first and returned after the spread and the wait; an answer later than the wait not counted, fused all the same; under a bound of three, twelve frames lost and none read; refused: an interface not heard, delays 0 and 6401, a request past 1 500 bytes, a wait of 61 s, a wire with no MAC, an ended worker at once, a wire refusing the request with its reason, a full queue, a worker that ends while the request waits its turn said at once with its reason and the request counted never sent, a send that stalls past its turn not sent; on a bridge in a private network, maquinar's executable running two devices of one name behind it, one silent: the announced one heard by its Hello alone, an Identify of every device answered by both with their names and addresses, the silent one found by its answer alone, the announced one's name witnessed by its Hello first and its answer last, the duplicate name the one conflict; by name both, by the silent one's alias it alone, by a name nobody holds nobody; the bench's exit; every frame kept dissected without complaint, the four requests sent and every answer named as given
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-144.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-145: The topology: nodes, links by the confidence their evidence earns, rings, components
- **Date:** 2026-11-25
- **Source:** user (docs/aranha-plan.md "Scope", the topology: nodes, links with the confidence their evidence earns, MRP rings, connected components; "Rules": it never guesses identity, a chassis key two devices claim resolves to neither; "Traps": passive LLDP gives nodes, not edges, device-to-device links come from neighbour tables, MRP membership read from the domain; phase 6; Veredas 1's `topology.rs`, absorbed)
- **Description:** `topology::build` makes, from what fusion holds (C-141), this machine's interfaces and the neighbours devices reported (their port data's peers: the reporting device and port, the peer's chassis id, port id and port MAC, and how many reads said it), a topology: nodes for every device (by MAC, its label, its ports by short name, the MRP domains its ports are in), every interface named or heard (with its MAC when known), and every chassis a neighbour names that resolves to no single device (its label saying ambiguous, with how many devices announce it, or not yet identified); links, each stored lesser end first: heard, where exactly one device's port, not leaving, was heard on an interface (its frames the witnesses), none where several were (a mirror port or a hub, said in a note) and none to a port that said it was leaving (said); reported, from a device's word on its port's neighbour, the neighbour found by its port MAC (a device's own MAC, or the one device whose port it is; all zeros none), else by the chassis id it announces in its own LLDP when exactly one device does, never by a name heard by DCP, else a chassis node; the MAC taken over a chassis another device announces, said; confirmed where both ends report each other, the witnesses both sides' reports; port ids read as short names. A ring is an MRP domain and the devices with a port in it, whatever MRRT's port status, closed when the links among its members, three at least, make one cycle through them all. Components join the nodes the links join, largest first, each sorted. The same inputs give the same topology whatever order the neighbours come in. Links inferred from a switch's forwarding table come with the SNMP phase.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-aranha/tests/topology.rs | three devices on three interfaces, one heard three times, two on one interface, a port that left: the nodes in order with their kinds, labels, MACs and ports, the interfaces named and heard; the one heard link with its witnesses; the leaving and the mirror or hub notes; the components; a neighbour named by its port MAC reported back by chassis and confirmed with two witnesses, one by chassis alone reported, the same topology from the neighbours in another order; neighbours found by port MAC, by a device's own MAC, by an announced chassis, an ambiguous chassis and an unknown one and a DCP name each a chassis node with its label, an all-zero MAC none even where a frame made a device of it, the ambiguity said, the links in order of their ends, a MAC against another device's chassis taken and said; three devices of one domain closed by a triangle of reports, open with a link missing, six as two triangles open, a triangle with a tail open, a device alone after a larger component, two members no ring, another domain another ring, MRRT off a member, each device node naming its ring
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-145.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-220: veredas-abaco — the engineering spreadsheet
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-abaco: the engineering spreadsheet"; decision 4; P-007)
- **Description:** `crates/veredas-abaco` computes and edits the engineering workbooks the suite works from, with no Office on the machine (`docs/abaco-plan.md` maps what it absorbs from Veredas 1's Ábaco, what it leaves, and the phases: the engine at parity, byte-faithful edits, the `.abaco` file, Python, the extension point with a small public example; nothing of Veredas 1's grid, its Excel host or acerto carried, Q341 to Q343). A library and nothing else yet: cargo, asked what it would build, names one library target of crate type `lib` and its tests. Its engine is its own fork of IronCalc at `forks/ironcalc`, reached by path, the only copy of the engine in its graph, neither of the fork's crates a member of the workspace as cargo itself reads it (cargo makes a crate beneath a member's folder a member whatever `exclude` says, so the fork sits outside every member's folder); the fork is upstream's `base` and `xlsx` at `15ecfec` as one commit, then every commit that changed them since names one change of the record `forks/ironcalc/VEREDAS-FORK.md`, and one only in any letter case, and the record holds no other row (its tables read as GitHub's Markdown, GFM 0.29, reads them: every table of the section, which ends at the next heading of level 1 or 2, ATX or setext, a header and a delimiter row of as many cells opening one, but a header lazily continuing a list item's or a quote's paragraph, rows with or without a leading pipe, to a blank line or a line that opens another block, none in a fence or an HTML block, an HTML line of kind 7 inside a paragraph the paragraph's; a table in a quote or a list item, which GitHub shows, fails the reading), and none is changed outside a commit (docs/questions.md Q340). It holds no plant connection: its normal dependency graph on every platform is a reviewed list of crates (the engine, its file layer and what they need, and the `.abaco` file's TOML, SHA-256 and unread-key check, C-224; no member, core's header reader compiled from its source by path, Q345), none of them a member of the suite, none of them an OPC UA, database, socket, HTTP, file-watching or async-runtime crate, a crate joining failing until it is reviewed. No crate of the workspace or of the fork's own workspace is patched or replaced, by a manifest, a cargo configuration cargo would read or any other route the locks would show; every crate of its graph but its own three path packages comes from crates.io, and the fork's lock holds from crates.io each the engine uses (its two crates' own graph, read from cargo). ## C-221: A workbook's links to other workbooks, resolved by a policy, and the log of what moved
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the architect's rulings for Veredas 1's Ábaco of 2026-11-03 and 2026-11-04, its AB7 and Q4, carried by docs/abaco-plan.md "Scope"; C-220)
- **Description:** `veredas_abaco::links::open` opens a workbook under a policy and evaluates it, the values shown computed at opening, never the ones the file stored: *always ask* (the default) calls the question once, only for a file that links to other workbooks, shown the books with their targets, sheets and cached cells, and applies its answer; *always use linked spreadsheets' cached values* answers every linked cell from the cache the file carries (Excel's values with the linked book closed); *always recalculate linked spreadsheets* opens each linked workbook from its target as Excel writes it (a URI: percent-encoded, or a `file:` URL) and reads it live, a workbook that is missing or cannot be read reported with its reason and answered from the cache. A file without links never asks, and a DDE or OLE link (the engine keeps it as a placeholder with no target) is no workbook: never asked about, counted or listed, a workbook link beside it keeping its number; a file that cannot be read is an error naming it. Under a linked resolution `link_diffs` writes one line per external cell whose live value differs from its cache (`[1]Sheet1!B2: cached 999 → live 10`, a sheet name quoted when it must be), numbers compared at Excel's 15 significant digits and text exactly, a number never equal to a text, an empty text read as empty on either side, a cell absent from the cache differing only from a non-empty live value, a book that cannot be opened or was left out yielding none, a book that opened without the sheet a formula reads yielding a line that says so (`cached 999 → live (no sheet ZONES)`), and no line under the cache; a number is spelled as Excel turns it into text, a text between double quotes as in a formula, a backslash doubled, its line breaks and control characters escaped, and every space but the plain one, Unicode's line and paragraph separators, the listed invisible format characters (the bidirectional controls among them) and the combining marks Latin, Greek and Cyrillic letters decompose into written `\u{..}`, so the line stays one line; and a line between two texts of which one holds a character beyond ASCII ends with the first character where they differ, by its code point (`(first difference at character 5: U+03A9 against U+2126)`), so two different texts never read the same. Veredas 1's third answer, Excel opening the linked workbooks, is not offered while its host is not carried (docs/questions.md Q342).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-abaco/tests/links.rs | workbooks written to scratch folders by the fork's own writer with their links and caches and opened back: each policy's answer and labels, the default; a file without links under the three policies never asking and evaluated; always ask called once with the book's target, sheet and cached cell, both answers applied; the cached and linked values and strategies; a missing linked workbook reported unavailable with its path's reason and answered from its cache beside a loaded one; a linked file that is no zip reported with a reason naming it, answered from its cache, no log line; targets `sub%20dir/Zone%20list.xlsx` and a `file:` URL opened and read live; a package whose only link is a DDE link (Excel's `ddeLink` part) never asking under the three policies, and one with a DDE link beside a workbook link asking about, counting and listing the workbook alone, its `[2]` read; a linked workbook that opened without the sheet: `#REF!`, two lines naming the sheet (a cached value and an empty cell), none under the cache; a file whose stored values differ from what its cache and formulas give (a host's 555 against a cache of 999, a stale product) shown computed, with and without links; the log under each resolution (one changed cell, an unchanged text, a missing book, a file without links); an absent and an unreadable file named; the log's rules on a model whose live side is the engine's host hook: no change, one change with both values and its position, a cell absent from the cache against a text and an empty text, kinds (a number become text, text, error), a range read cell by cell, a side that cannot answer and a skipped book, a quoted sheet name; the comparison (15 digits both ways, a negative zero, case, an empty text on either side) and the spelling of every kind (`1E+21`, `1E+16`, `0.0000001`, `1.23456789012346E+17`, `0`, a doubled quote, `\n`, `\r`, `\t`, a control character, a composed accent as it is, a no-break space and a decomposed accent escaped, a doubled backslash, `C:\new` and a line feed read apart, the line and paragraph separators and three bidirectional controls escaped, and ten pairs that would look alike (a no-break, thin, narrow and ideographic space, a zero-width space, a soft hyphen, a byte-order mark, a word joiner, a variation selector, a decomposed accent) each spelled apart from its plain twin; the first differing code point on the line for a Greek omega and an ohm sign, a K and a kelvin sign, a hyphen and a non-breaking hyphen, a composed and a decomposed kana, a text and the same with a tag character, never for two ASCII texts, a text against a number or an empty side)
