## C-090: The store kept as a window in memory: the rows that arrived within a span, the older let go, nothing on the drive
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the ruling of 2026-11-19, "Memory window", docs/robustness.md "The store of a controller's embedded portal"; docs/portal-plan.md "Decided in the briefing of November 2026"); promoted in part from P-019
- **Description:** A session's store may be kept as a window (`SessionConfig::store_window`; the project's `store = { window_s = N }`, C-046): in memory, nothing written to the drive, holding the rows that arrived within the span on the arrival clock (the clock health is judged on: a line's stamp, however old, decides nothing), the older let go once a second (`WINDOW_EVERY`) by the pump through the store's ingest connection, so the pass never waits on a reader (C-043) (`DuckStore::evict_before`: every base-table row that arrived before the cutoff and every bare sample indexed before it let go, a row at the cutoff kept, the count returned), so a session that runs for years holds a bounded stretch, every protocol's rows alike (a captured line, a poll, a message), the views and the narrow surface reading the window. The series listing still counts every sample seen (its counts order the frames, C-043); the counters count the rows let go (`evicted`) and the snapshot (C-044) carries the span (`window_ms`, null without a window) beside them. A window on a store on disk is refused by name before anything starts. In memory the engine hands deleted rows' space back, the figure bounded (C-042). Not built: a ceiling in bytes beside the span (docs/questions.md Q41). What a session kept as a window writes to the drive is other clauses': the record (C-091) and the copy at a fault (C-093).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/store_window.rs | on a real in-memory store: an empty store letting nothing go; two base tables and the samples table, stamps running against arrival, a cutoff letting go exactly the rows that arrived before it (three: one of each), the row at the cutoff kept, a view and the narrow surface reading what is left, the same cutoff again letting nothing go, a later one and the end letting go the rest exactly, the store taking rows after; a session over a log kept as a window of three seconds: ten lines read whole and every one still held 2.8 s after the first of them arrived, ten stamped a day earlier arriving after, the first ten let go while the second stay (the temperatures exact), no sooner than three seconds after the last of them arrived and no later than the span, a pass and 1.5 s after the first, the rows let go counted 10 then 20, the listing counting the 20 samples seen while the store holds none, the snapshot's span 3000 and its counts; against the bench's plant with a window of one second: eighty polls and an announcement, at least twenty rows let go, the oldest poll held watched for four seconds never older than the span, a pass and 0.7 s and at every look at least half a second old, the polls' and the announcements' held arrivals within the span, the period and a margin of each other, fewer rows held than polls; the same plant without a window letting nothing go, its snapshot's span null, every poll a row; a session kept as a window of a minute passing at least half its quiet pace over three seconds while an export into a pipe nobody reads holds the store's query connection, the lines written meanwhile stored and none let go (Unix); a window on a disk store refused with its exact text, nothing written; a poll let go never inside the span (the youngest gone at least 1 000 ms old at the look after its going), and the oldest held moving at each pass, at least five times in six seconds and never 1.45 s apart
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-090.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-091: Recording to the drive, the project's choice: every row the store takes written in batches to a record of the run's own, until its size limit
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the ruling of 2026-11-19 and its additions "not objected to", docs/robustness.md "The store of a controller's embedded portal": "the project can turn on recording to the drive, in files with a size limit"); promoted in part from P-019
- **Description:** A session may record to the drive (`SessionConfig::recording`; the project's `record = { dir, max_mb, every_s }`, C-046): every row the store takes is also written to a database file of the run's own (`<name>-<yyyymmdd>-<hhmmss>.record.duckdb` in the folder, created; a database already standing at the file refuses the session by name, as C-041's store does, asked before the store opens), refused by name before anything opens beside a store on the drive (`disk` or `dir`, C-046), which already holds every row, with the store's tables and the project's views, a file any tool reads once the session has stopped; the rows wait in memory and go to the drive in one batch every `every_s` seconds (10 when absent) and at the stop, every table's rows in one transaction, so a batch lands whole or not at all (a write that fails stops the recording, the batch counted unrecorded) and the drive is written seldom whatever the poll rate; with a store kept as a window (C-090) the record holds what the window lets go. When the record's files (the database and its write-ahead log) reach the size limit at a write, the recording stops at that first write: what was written stays, nothing is deleted, the rows after are counted unrecorded and never written, the store goes on. At the stop the record's log is folded into its file and the size published is the one the drive holds (the engine allocates the file in blocks, so a closed record may stand above the size its last write measured). The session publishes the recording's file, limit, size, rows recorded and unrecorded and why it stopped, and the snapshot (C-044) carries them. The size counted is the file's and its write-ahead log's, as the engine keeps them (C-042). What gives way at a limit is docs/questions.md Q41's: recording stopping is the reversible answer taken meanwhile. Distinct from the session's store and its pump (C-043), whose rows it records, from a store on the drive (C-041, C-046's `disk` and `dir`: the session's store itself, unbounded, the one the server reads) from C-041's snapshot (a whole store written once, on request) and from the copy at a fault (C-093, the whole store at one moment, a file per fault): a second C-041 store beside the session's, fed by C-043's pump with the rows it stored and written on the pump's own pass through a connection no reader holds, its last batch in the stop's last passes.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/store_record.rs | against the bench's plant with a window of one second and a record written every second: the record's folder created and its file opened at the start, sixty polls, an announcement and twenty rows let go by the window, the snapshot's file, limit and no stop; after the stop every row the store took recorded and none unrecorded, the size under the limit, every poll on the drive (read by a plain connection) while the window holds fewer, each poll the window still holds the record's in every column, every table of the record together holding exactly the rows the store took, the announcements there and the project's view in the record as a view; a log of 20 000 lines and a limit of 200 KiB: the recording stopped by its limit with the exact words, the size the write reached and the limit named, the size at or past it, fifty lines after it stored but not written, the rows recorded unchanged from the stop, recorded and unrecorded adding to 20 050, the record holding exactly the rows recorded, each line's source, key and state as the rule read them; a second session onto the same file refused by name with the file untouched; against the plant polled every 50 ms, a record written every two seconds changing its rows two to four times in six seconds, never twice within 1.8 s; a log written 500 lines at a time with a write every 200 ms and a limit of 400 KiB: several sizes published under the limit, every one before the stop under it and the one at the stop at or past it and the drive's, the session still running; after the stop the size published equal to the database and its log on the drive, the log folded, the record holding exactly the rows recorded; a log of 3 000 lines recorded every minute: nothing written before the stop and the record refused to another process while the session runs, then at a normal stop all 3 000 written, the size published the drive's, the log folded, and another process opening the record and counting 3 000 rows; a log of 200 000 lines recorded every minute, stopped as soon as the store holds a row, the rows its stop's last passes take recorded too, every one on the drive and none unrecorded; four writes at one every two seconds, each 1.8 to 2.6 s after the one before, the record's file and its log on the drive changing at those writes alone (as many writes, one fewer when the first look met a write midway, steps closer than 300 ms one write and each done within 500 ms) and as far apart; a batch of two tables whose second fails leaving neither, then landing whole (in the crate); the record's rows read back: every poll once and the polls the window still holds the record's own field for field, the limit's record the log's first lines each once with their temperatures exactly (count, first and last seq, minimum, maximum, sum); through the recorder itself, a good batch's very rows and values, a batch whose second table the record lacks landing nothing of either, counted unrecorded (three rows), the recording stopped with its reason, a row after it counted unrecorded and never written, and the failed batch's transaction ended: the record's log folded after it as the stop folds it, the size measured then the drive's once the record is closed, and the file, read by a connection of its own, holding the good batch alone (in the crate); after a stop at the limit, the snapshot's recording equal field for field to the session's, its reason exact; a record beside a store on disk refused by name with neither file made
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-091.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-092: The controller a portal is embedded in, as a source: its state changes and its faults stored like any other data
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 19 November 2026, docs/controlador-plan.md "The engineering link": "State changes and faults are stored like any other data, and the history view reads them"; docs/portal-plan.md "Decided in the briefing of November 2026"); promoted in part from P-019
- **Description:** A session may hold a controller source (`SessionConfig::controller`, its id and display name), fed in process by the embedding through `Session::controller_feed()`: `push_state` hands a state as the controller holds it (core's `ControllerState`, C-070), `push_fault` a fault record (its FAULT state, the download that ran, the handler's outcome), each stamped on the controller's own clock (the store's axis) and on arrival; a push after the session stopped is refused (`FeedClosed`), and a push racing the stop is either stored or refused, never accepted and lost (the feed is closed before the session's last drain). Every state is a row of `controller_states` (its text by core's one writer, its word, its reason's number and word, its health) and every record a row of `controller_faults` (its text, the cause's number and word, the download, the outcome), in the session's sequence; their values are series like any other (listed, counted, the last value the last state's), framed when numeric, recorded and kept in a window as every other row (C-090, C-091). The source's id and its two tables are claimed like any other's, a clash refused by name; a session of a controller source alone starts. Its health reads waiting until a state came, then live however long since (a state stands until it changes, so never idle), ended at the stop.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/controller_state.rs | beside the bench's plant read by the same session: the source listed by kind and display name, waiting; five states and a fault record pushed; the five rows exact (texts as core writes them, words, reasons 104, 201, none, 501, 101 with their words, health function check, normal or failure), their source, no key, sequence 1 to 5; the record's row exact (text, cause 501 and its word, download 7, outcome, sequence 6); the listing's state counted five with the last value `STOP`, the reason counted four (RUN has none) with its last value, the cause listed; the reasons framed 104, 201, 501, 101; the health live after more than five idle windows with its count 6; the plant polled beside it; after the stop the health ended and a push refused; a session of a controller source alone storing a state; no feed without a controller; its id taken by a log and its faults table's name taken by a rule refused with their exact texts; every row's index within a second of the controller's local clock at the push and its arrival within a second of the unix clock, its stamp its index; a pusher racing the stop over ten rounds, every push it saw accepted a row
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-092.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-093: At a fault the store is written to the drive with the fault record
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the ruling of 2026-11-19 and its additions "not objected to", docs/robustness.md "The store of a controller's embedded portal": "at a fault the controller writes the window to the drive with the fault record"); promoted in part from P-019
- **Description:** With a folder for it (`SessionConfig::fault_copy_dir`), every fault record the controller source hands in (C-092) has the session write its store to the drive once the fault's row is stored in it: the whole store as a standalone database (tables as data, views as views, C-041's snapshot), the window's stretch when the store is kept as one (C-090), in a file of its own named for the session and the record's stamp on the controller's clock (C-092) (`<name>-<yyyymmdd>-<hhmmss>.fault.duckdb`, `.fault-1` and on for a second fault in the same second), never over a file, the folder created; through the store's ingest connection, so a reader holding the query connection holds no copy back (C-043). The counters count the copies and name the last, and the snapshot (C-044) carries them; a copy that fails (a folder that cannot be made) is a store error with its text, the fault stored all the same. Without a folder nothing is written. How many copies are kept and how often one may be written are not ruled (docs/questions.md Q41): every fault gets its copy and none is deleted. Distinct from the recording (C-091), every row the store takes written in batches to one record per run until its size limit: a copy is the store as it stands at one fault, written at once and whole to a file of that fault's own, under no limit of the record's and whether or not the session records. It stands on C-092's record (its trigger, its row in the copy), C-041's snapshot (its form), C-090's window (its stretch) and C-043's pump and ingest connection (its path: the pump is held for the copy's length, a window's stretch, or the whole store without one).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/fault_copy.rs | against the bench's plant with a store kept as a window of a second, a controller source and a view: nothing written before a fault; a FAULT state and its record pushed, one copy named for the session and the fault's time read off the copy's own record (`press-<yyyymmdd>-<hhmmss>.fault.duckdb`), the counters naming it; read back by a plain connection: the record's row exact, the FAULT state's row, the window's polls (some, fewer than polled, within three seconds of each other), the view's rows; two faults at once two files more, one named `.fault-…`, holding both records; the first copy unchanged at the end; without a folder no copy; a file where the folder should be: no copy, one store error naming the folder, the fault stored; an export into a pipe nobody reads holding the query connection while a fault's copy is written, the copy holding the ten rows (Unix); three copies each named exactly for its fault's second in the order the faults came, `.fault-1` for the second in a second, the counters and the snapshot naming the newest; a fault accepted while the session stops has its copy (three rounds, every accepted push a file)
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-093.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-111: The EtherNet/IP adapter: a device's assemblies over explicit messages and Class 1 I/O
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: bench devices for the four fieldbuses, an EtherNet/IP adapter among them; P-020; docs/maquinar-plan.md "P-020: the bench devices, as they land")
- **Description:** `ethernetip::start` serves an `EnipConfig` (TOML, every optional key defaulted, a misspelled key refused; a bench file's `[[ethernetip]]`, C-033) as an EtherNet/IP adapter on its own thread and runtime, the bench's own code: an identity, assemblies by instance, the Class 1 connections it accepts by their configuration, consumed and produced assemblies (each direction run/idle or modeless), and named points in the assemblies' bytes, refused by name before anything binds when they cannot serve (a bind that is not a dotted quad among them, trimmed as it is bound, an IPv6 address or a host name refused: its items carry IPv4 addresses alone). On TCP it answers ListIdentity (on UDP too, where a broadcast reaches an adapter bound to the any-address, its item naming the address the reply leaves from), ListServices, ListInterfaces, RegisterSession once per connection (another version 0x0069 with its own, a limit 0x0002), UnRegisterSession by closing, SendRRData only on its session (else 0x0064, items it cannot read 0x0003), any other command 0x0001, NOP not at all; the Identity object's attributes 1 to 7 and all of them, the Assembly object's data read and written whole (a short or long write 0x13 or 0x15, a produced assembly 0x0E, one a connection consumes 0x0C), an unknown class or instance 0x05, attribute 0x14, service 0x08, a path segment it cannot read 0x04 with its word (a port, key or data segment in a request's own path among them, where only logical segments belong); an Unconnected_Send answered with the embedded request's own reply when its route is empty or the port segment 01 00 (the backplane's slot 0 in its short form; any other form of a route refused), refused 0x01/0x0312 otherwise with the route's remaining size in words and a reserved byte, and one nested in another refused 0x08 (the adapter routes one level, so no request nests its stack away). A Forward_Open or Large_Forward_Open is refused 0x01 with the extended status the protocol gives each fault (a triple already open 0x0100, the triple its three fields (connection serial, vendor, originator serial), a transport other than Class 1 cyclic 0x0103, a connection type other than point to point 0x0108, a path segment 0x0315, an electronic key's vendor or product 0x0114, device type 0x0115, revision 0x0116 with the compatibility bit honoured, the configuration, consuming and producing paths 0x0129, 0x012A, 0x012B, a configuration data size 0x0126, an O→T or T→O size 0x0127 or 0x0128 with the size it wants, an RPI outside its range 0x0111, an output owned 0x0106, the connection limit 0x0113), each size counting the data, the 16-bit sequence count and a run/idle header where the direction has one; an accepted one produces the input assembly at its RPI, its first frame at once, to the originator's own host at the port its T→O socket-address item gives (item 0x8001, its address not obeyed; else, or when it gives port 0, port 2222), each frame counted as it goes out and a send that fails taken back, and its reply gives the adapter's I/O address (item 0x8000) when the port is not 2222; a consumed frame's run data lands in the output assembly, an idle frame or a repeated sequence count leaves it, a frame no newer than the last taken (its 32-bit sequence read across the wrap) is stale and dropped, a frame of another size, host or connection is stray; a Forward_Close closes it (none open 0x0107), and a connection whose originator falls silent past its RPI times 4 shifted by its multiplier is closed (the first frame given 10 s). The plant side reads and sets the assemblies by point or as bytes; the knobs stall production, silence the encapsulation and restart the device (every session's connection, one whose answers wait on a client that reads nothing and one still in the listen queue, its connect returned before the call, too, and every Class 1 connection closed, none opened after it by a request a session the restart ended was still serving); stop and drop free every port, a taken port refused naming it. Codecs the bench did not write and Wireshark's dissectors read the conversation as the protocol has it. The scanner that meets it is core's (C-072, the protocol's other end: its test `tools/mestres/tests/enip.rs` runs against this adapter); neither clause holds the other's behaviour.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/ethernetip_adapter.rs | the configuration's defaults and every refusal with its exact message; ListIdentity on TCP and UDP byte for byte, ListServices and ListInterfaces; sessions (before one, another version, a short body, twice, another handle, unknown commands, bad items, NOP, the limit, UnRegisterSession closing and freeing the place); the Identity object (all, each attribute, an unknown one, a set, the class, another instance, another service, another class, a bad segment with its word, a path past the request, the owned bit while a connection is open); the Assembly object (the points' values, the size, the plant side's set read next, a write landing, short, long, to a produced assembly, to an owned one) and the plant side's refusals; Unconnected_Send with an empty route, slot 0 and another route; a Class 1 connection opened with `ethernetip-connections`' Forward_Open encoder, its reply decoded by that crate, produced 45 to 56 times in a second at 20 ms with every frame counted on and decoded by that crate's Class 1 codec, the plant side's value produced, run data consumed, a repeated count and an idle frame kept out, stray frames, closed and closed again; a silent originator's connection closed after its 40 ms and the output free again; every Forward_Open refusal with its extended status and the originator's triple, the key's compatibility accepted, configuration data written; the crate's own scanner refused 0x0127 for leaving the sequence count out; the knobs; stop freeing the TCP and both UDP ports and a taken port named; tshark reading the whole conversation with no complaint and the identity, sizes, RPI and connection id as sent; the reference's I/O block run through the bench file; refute round 1's pins: an Unconnected_Send nested two and a thousand deep refused 0x08 and the adapter answering after, a Large_Forward_Open taken and refused, unequal RPIs (the reply's two, production at the T→O one, that one range-checked), the timeout's multiplier and bound and the first frame's 10 s, the configured headers carrying frames both ways, a late frame (the wrap too) and a frame from another host kept out, produced frames to the originator's host at its item's port and at 2222 with no item, a connection's first frame at once behind one at a 5 s RPI, a Forward_Open inside an Unconnected_Send keeping its items, 0x0315 for a segment and for one connection point, a ListIdentity broadcast to 127.255.255.255 answered with the leaving address, five configuration refusals, a taken Class 1 port, the plant side by bytes, the Identity class's revision, UDP ListIdentity while silent; refute round 2's pins: a multiplier of 2 closing a 10 ms connection no sooner than 160 ms after its last frame and before 300, the multiplier 1 timeout measured from the last frame sent (80 to 200 ms), a Large_Forward_Open of 700 and 600 bytes taken with frames both ways, an O→T RPI alone below the range refused 0x0111, an equal 32-bit sequence stale, the compatibility bit refusing another major 0x0116, the first frame within 100 ms at a 1 s RPI, stale frames alone letting the connection time out, IPv6 binds refused by name, a port and a data segment in a request path refused 0x04 at their words, four nested misspellings refused, six refusals read by tshark with no complaint; round 3's pins: binds of ` ::1`, `::1 `, `ip6-localhost`, `localhost` and `127.0.0.1.5` refused by name by `validate` and `start`, a dotted quad with spaces bound as its address; two originators sharing a connection serial under their own vendor and serial each opened, the same triple again 0x0100, a close naming the serial under another vendor or another originator serial 0x0107 with both left open, the whole triple closing its own alone; a key and an extended port segment leading a request's path refused 0x04 at word 0; a port segment in a connection path 0x0315; a taken encapsulation port over UDP named `(udp)`; the routing refusal `D2 00 01 01 12 03 01 00` read by tshark as a remaining path size of 1 with no complaint; multiplier 3 at a 10 ms RPI closing 320 to 500 ms after the last frame; a restart with 400 unread answers of a 65000-byte assembly waiting returning within a second with no connection or session left; round 4's pins: a session pipelining Forward_Opens restarted 40 times with no Class 1 connection left open, and a scanner reconnecting after each restart opening its connection, never refused as the output's owner; the routing refusal's size for routes of 1, 2, 2 and 6 words read by tshark with no complaint, slot 0 in its long form refused with 2; a reply's items in the protocol's order, the address, the data, then the socket address; ListIdentity's status owned while a connection is open; the Assembly's services 0x01, 0x4B and 0x05 refused 0x08; 60 adapters started on port 0 beside 2000 UDP sockets holding ports on their address, each answering a ListIdentity over UDP on its TCP listener's number; round 5's pins: an electronic key matching the device exactly taken; assemblies 300, 336 and 337 read and connected by 16-bit logical segments (25 00 2C 01, a Forward_Open's 25/2D path), the run data landing; transports 0x21, 0x00 and 0x81 refused 0x0103; a dropped handle closing its session and freeing its TCP, UDP and Class 1 ports; an Unconnected_Send of an 11-byte Set_Attribute_Single answered past its pad, by an empty route and by 01 00; two connections under one serial with ids of their own, each one's run frame landing in its own output; 200 clients whose connect and RegisterSession came before a restart none served after it; round 4's restart under pipelined opens wrapped in an Unconnected_Send leaving none open in 40 rounds; a T->O item of port 0 sending to port 2222.
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-111.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-112: The PROFINET device: discovery and naming on a raw interface
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: bench devices for the four fieldbuses, a PROFINET device among them; P-020; the aranha session's ask, relayed 2026-11-22; docs/maquinar-plan.md "P-020: the bench devices, as they land")
- **Description:** `profinet::start` runs a `ProfinetConfig` (TOML, every optional key defaulted, a misspelled key refused; a bench file's `[[profinet]]`, C-033) as a PROFINET device on a named network interface, raw frames through aranha's link and codecs (Linux; on another system `start` refuses, naming why, which this suite, on Linux, never reaches), its configuration refused by name before the interface opens (an empty interface, a station name the standard refuses, a port not named `port-xyz` or `port-xyz-abcde`, an alias not a port name, a dot and a station name, an LLDP interval past 802.1AB's hour, a vendor text that is not 1 to 255 visible ASCII characters, as DCP carries it; the refusals through a bench file are C-033's): it sends a DCP Hello at start; takes a request only under its service's frame id (Identify 0xFEFE, Get and Set 0xFEFD); answers a DCP Identify whose every filter it matches (all, its station name, which an unnamed device never matches, its alias, its vendor, its vendor and device ids, a role it has, its IP suite, its MAC; a filter of any other kind never matches), to the asker with the request's xid, after a spread within the request's response delay (at once for a factor of 1); applies a DCP Set addressed to it, block by block (its station name, refused 0x05 and kept when the standard refuses the name or the name is not text, an empty name taken as the device unnamed; its IP suite; the identification signal 0x0100, counted, any other value refused 0x05) and answers each block in order with its three-byte control response (an option it lacks 0x01, a suboption it lacks or does not set 0x02, a block of a suboption it holds that it cannot read, a name, an IP suite or a signal of the wrong form, 0x05), to the asker as a success (a Set whose answer would not fit one frame neither applied, answered nor counted); answers a DCP Get with each pair asked in the order asked, a pair it lacks as a control response 0x02 (a Get whose answer would not fit one frame neither answered nor counted); sends LLDP on its port on its interval (its station as the chassis, an unnamed device its MAC, `port-xyz.<station>` as the port, an unnamed device's `port-xyz`, its MAC in PROFINET's chassis-MAC field) and, when it stops, a frame with a TTL of 0 holding the chassis, the port and the TTL alone; the plant side names it, flags an address conflict in its IP status (the address kept set), mutes its DCP (no DCP frame leaving once the mute returns: an Identify answer waiting out its delay dropped, even when the mute is lifted before the device's next pass, an Identify, a Set or a Get read then neither taken, counted nor answered), and reads its identity and its counts (an Identify answer counted as it goes out, one a mute dropped never). Wireshark's dissectors read every frame it sends as the standard has it.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/profinet_device.rs | in a private network of the test's own (aranha's `netns::isolated`, a veth pair): the Hello to the Hello group with its identity; Identify by all, name and alias answered to the asker with its xid, a name and an alias it lacks unanswered, the counts; twelve answers to a factor of 50 within 600 ms and spread, a factor of 1 within 100 ms; an unnamed device unanswered by name, named by a Set and found by the new name, a bad name (written as raw bytes) answered 0x05 and not taken, an IP suite set, the signal counted, a Get answered with the blocks asked, a muted device silent; LLDP's chassis, port and chassis MAC, its interval, a TTL of 0 at stop; tshark reading every frame the device sends with no complaint, the station names, the Set's error, the xid, the vendor id and the TTLs as sent; the reference's unnamed device run through its bench file, found, named, addressed, found again by name, renamed and its conflict flagged from the plant side, its status line; refute round 1's pins: an unnamed device's LLDP (its MAC the chassis) and shutdown, a name that is not text refused 0x05, requests under another service's frame id dropped, a muted device silent after a queued answer, a Get of two lacked pairs answered 0x02 each, an over-long alias and three bad port names and a bad alias refused, an Identify by name and device id answered, tshark reading the unnamed device, the refusals, an eight-block Get and the shutdown frame last with no complaint, the spread over twelve answers at a factor of 50 (wider than 150 ms, within 520 ms) and the factor 1 at once, a four-block Set answered 0, 0, 1, 2 with the name and address applied, a Get's exact pairs under 0xFEFD with its count and the LLDP count, Sets and Gets not addressed to it dropped, the device's MAC the interface's, LLDP's mean gap over five within 170 to 260 ms at 200, a flagged conflict keeping the address set, the signal's other values refused and uncounted; round 2's pins: round 1's mute pin under its seed (the clock's drew under its 30 ms once in 65) with the dropped answer uncounted, an unnamed device's chassis its MAC and its port `port-001`, the shutdown naming the chassis and the port its frames named; every Identify filter kind matched and not (vendor, role, IP suite, MAC) and three of other kinds never; a muted device answering no Set and no Get and applying none; LLDP naming the station after a Set and after a rename by hand; a Get under the Identify's frame id and a Set of service type 1 dropped; an IP suite of 8 bytes or with no qualifier and a signal of 3 bytes refused 0x05 with nothing changed; an empty name answered 0, the device unnamed, found no more by its old name and its chassis its MAC; aliases `x.switch-1`, `port-001.Bad_Name` and `port-001.`, ports `port-000` and `port-256` and an LLDP interval of 3600001 refused by their text, `port-255` and an hour taken; a misspelled key refused by the configuration itself; after the orchestrator's red run at 081ffa70, 100 rounds of two hundred Identify answers falling due around the mute at a factor of 2, the count read when the mute returned equal to every answer received, and the mute cutting a burst in 40 rounds or more (the bare flag failed it within ten rounds in three runs of three); round 3's pins: 150 bursts of 200 Identify, Set and Get requests cut by the mute, no DCP frame stamped by the kernel later than the moment the mute returned; an Identify answer waiting when a mute came and went at once never sent, five rounds of five, one after answered; the shutdown after a rename at 1000 ms naming the chassis and port its first frame named; a Get of 200 pairs it lacks neither answered nor counted, a Get of one answered and counted; a Hello under 0xFEFC, an Identify answer under 0xFEFF, a Set answer under 0xFEFD; a vendor of `Fábrica de bancada`, none, 256 characters or a tab refused by its text, 255 visible characters taken; round 4's pins: bursts of 20 Identify requests no filter matches, the mute set at spread moments into them, none counted passed once `set_muted(true)` returned in 1500 rounds (on the machine's lock); a Set of the name and 185 blocks more answered with 186 control responses in 1500 bytes, one of 186 or 200 more neither applied, answered nor counted; a Get's and a Set's answers from the device to the asker, as a success, with the request's xid; ## C-094: Search over logs at rest: patterns combined per line over a tree, a file list or a family, decoded as the tail decodes them
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 19 November 2026: the grep engine over logs at rest on Oficina's first-release list; docs/portal-plan.md "Decided in the briefing of November 2026"; Veredas 1's `veredas-core` `grep`, docs/portal-plan.md "What it absorbs"); promoted in part from P-019
- **Description:** `grep::search` (in the light build too) runs a query on a worker thread and streams its events: a query of one or more patterns, each a `regex` (the suite's one syntax; Veredas 1's `fancy-regex` fallback not carried, lookaround refused by the engine's words) or literal, case-insensitive or whole-word as asked (a whole word standing apart from word characters on both sides, whatever the pattern's own ends: `-5` alone, not in `a-5 b`; the pattern checked bare first, so a refusal speaks of the operator's own text and no group of it escapes the wrapper), combined per line (every test, any one, or an odd number holding, each test negatable); a scope of a folder tree in name order, filtered by case-insensitive file-name masks (`*`, `?`, matched by character, so `?` is one accented letter too), recursive or not, a link to a folder or a file not followed (Veredas 1's walk), a list of files in its order, or a log family resolved in its rotation order (`GrepScope::from_family`). Every file is opened for reading alone, read and decoded in the tail's own 64 KiB slices and scanned as it is decoded, so a search holds a slice and a line whatever the file's size (a line past 4 MiB searched in its first 4 MiB, the rest of it read past, wherever the slices fall; a limit: the patterns see the cut as the line's end, so a word cut there stands apart and `$` holds there), as a tail reading it whole from its start decodes it (UTF-8 with or without its mark, UTF-16 in either order with a mark or by its zero pattern, CP1252; C-035; a live tail that followed the file as it was written, or attached at its end, settles UTF-8 or CP1252 on what it read first, and may have read a file mixing the two otherwise: a limit, stated), the mark dropped (a second one text, as the tail keeps it), CRLF and LF lines alike. A matching line comes with its number and its spans (every match of a pattern that is not negated, tagged by the pattern, sorted, an overlap dropped), its context lines before and after as asked and a break between two blocks of a file when context is asked; a file with matches ends with its count, whatever stopped it (its end, a cap, a cancel); a file past the size limit is skipped and named, one with a NUL in its head (a UTF-16 file aside) skipped and counted unless asked for, a file or folder that cannot be read an error in its place, the search going on; a cap per file ends that file and says so, a total cap ends the search (a cap of 0 gives no line, as grep's `-m 0`); a cancel is heard inside a file, at every slice it reads, its lines scanned as they are read (so within every 64 KiB of them, or once the line a slice completes is scanned: a limit, a line is scanned whole, so one of up to 4 MiB under a slow pattern holds the cancel for its scan, about two seconds in round 4's case); the last event gives the totals and why the search stopped. An empty pattern, a pattern that is no `regex`, a query with no pattern and a tree with no root are refused before anything is read.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/grep.rs | the bench's emitter writing a family rotating every 25 lines in UTF-16 with its mark, searched as a family: exactly the files and line numbers a reading of the files finds, in rotation order, decoded with the mark gone, the span exact, the totals; matching lines with one line of context before and after, CRLF and LF alike, the exact sequence with one break, the file's done event with three matches, no break without context and nothing for a file with none; and, not, or, xor and two negations each on exact lines, the spans tagged by pattern with an overlap dropped and none for a negated pattern; a regex against a literal, case and whole word in each combination; one line found in UTF-8 with and without its mark, UTF-16 in either order with its mark and without one, and CP1252, the decoded text exact, none taken for binary; two roots with nested folders by masks recursive and not, every file and a mask with `?`, in name order, a root that is not there an error in its place; a file past the size limit skipped with its size, a binary file skipped and counted and searched when asked, a cap per file twice capped, a total cap stopping at seven, a missing file an error with the next searched; a cancel heard inside a two-million-line file; every refusal's text, the engine's own words in a pattern's (an unclosed group, look-around not supported); a whole word `-5` and `°C` found standing apart and not glued to a word; ## C-202: The engine, live: a checked test bound to a portal session and run step by step against the plant, every outcome routed, every run ending passed, failed or aborted
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (decision 6: branching on any outcome, with retries; docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-comissionador", rules: reads plant data only through portal, drives machinery only through portal's gated writes; docs/test-schema.md "Steps", "Expressions", "Time"; docs/comissionador-plan.md "How a run meets the plant"; docs/questions.md Q313, Q314, Q316, taken meanwhile)
- **Description:** `run::run` runs a test that passed its check (C-201) against a `Plant`, the engine's one door to portal (`plant::LivePlant` over a session's `Live` in this process, Q313): first bound, nothing run and nothing written when refused (the session's name against `portal`, the mode against `modes`, every table and column the test and its functions read, a column read through a name no table it may hold a row of has among them (an `await`'s table, and through every copy of the name, `min` and `max` of it among them, in any order), every `write` an OPC UA tag a rule of the session marks writable, every `send` a channel and a message of the session, and, live, when the test writes, the switch armed, or the person's confirmation of the test's gated writes (`run::gated_writes`, each as written, confirmed by `run::writes_fingerprint` of the list) with the embedder's way to arm it (`LivePlant::with_arming`): the run then arms it before its first step and disarms it at its end, whichever way it ends, a switch it finds armed left as found (Q314 (b), Q320 taken); every refusal named); then step by step on a clock on the store's axis, as `docs/test-schema.md` says: each action's outcome (`pass`, `fail`, `timeout`, `error`), the first that does not pass ending its step; a gated write or send through the session's handle, portal's answer or refusal kept word for word, the clock moved to the answer; a wait judged over every row of the tables its condition reads, from the latest values at the clock, at each stamp once every row of it is applied, a stamp two rows of a table share judged by the values a read there finds, holding at once when it holds as the step starts; an await taking the first row stamped after the clock whose condition holds, or, at the stamp of the row the last await of its table took, a row of that stamp after it, the rows of a stamp in one order whatever the store's (a view's rows, with no source or sequence, among them); a read, a check (a criterion with the values it read), a call (inputs checked against their types, an int taken for a float, outputs into the names, an output its path leaves unassigned an error, its own bound never past its caller's), a script box (the test's variables, what it set its detail; a box or a step's call at most 10 000 000 statements, a turn of a loop one, the tester's stop ending it, and the run's bound once a wait or a call inside it has moved the clock past it), a branch (the first arm that matches, a criterion with the arm taken), a report (an item that cannot be evaluated recorded with its reason), a stop; routes by an arm, an `on` line or the default, a retry counted per route per run (Q316) and spent into a failure; the verdict passed, failed or aborted with the step and the reason, a step the run stopped inside (the tester's stop, the run's bound) recorded with no outcome; a stream of events (each step's start and end, criteria, reports, writes, the end) to a listener; a live read made only once its moment is 250 ms past, so the rows stamped by then are stored. The engine sets portal's switch only so (C-200). The values a test computes with and their comparisons across types are the reference's, a whole number, a duration or a moment past its 64 bits an error, whole numbers and durations never through a float (a duration times a number exact, rounded once). Live, a wait for a duration lasts until its end or its bound has passed, and the run's bound passing inside a wait moves the clock to it, never back. The run's bound is the run's clock's in both modes (the plant's time, which a replay reproduces, never the machine's work), judged at each step and between its actions; a whole number compared with a float or a numeric text exactly. What a run keeps on disk is C-204's. Distinct from replay (C-203), the same `run::run` over a stored window: this clause's test alone proves the binding and every action, route, outcome and verdict, run as rows arrive, a write sent through portal's handles; C-203 proves what replay changes. Limits: a row stored more than 250 ms after its stamp (a late log line, a polled share, P-013) is missed by a read of that moment; when the plant's clock changes (summer time) the store's axis repeats or skips an hour (as C-052 states), and a live run's bound and timeouts move with it. It stands on portal's session and store (C-043: the switch, the handles; C-041: the store's windows) and runs C-201's model; its writes go through the same gated handles as portal's own (C-022, C-039), in process, never through the server's write surface (C-044); distinct from supervisão's binding file (C-048), checked whole against what a project yields before any session starts, and from its gated writes (C-054), a person's one write at a time: here a checked test is bound, as its run starts, to a running session's own catalog, rules and switch, and a test file drives the writes.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-comissionador/tests/live.rs | against a portal session started from a project text by `project::load` and `Session::start`, over the bench's OPC UA server (a speed set 100 ms after `run` is written 1) and the controller reference's machine over a real socket: the reference's worked example passing at `summary` with its seven steps' details, `rerouted` 20, its criterion, its two sends answered and the machine stopped after, seven step starts and ends then the verdict to the listener, the clock running forward from step to step; the example with replies to a free destination failing at `fail_log` with its note; a gated write answered `Good` and seen at the plant, its step ending at the answer at least the read's settle after it began, a wait from there holding at the stamp of the first row the reaction stored, a condition holding at once in 0s, a read, a check passing with its measured values and one failing with them; the binding's nine refusals in order and nothing at the plant, a test that only reads running with the switch off; a retry taken twice then the else, a retry spent failing the step with its reason and routed by `on fail`, no arm matching, `on pass` skipping, a script's `fail`, a fault aborting and routed by `on error`, the first failing action ending a multi-action step, a report of a name its path never assigned, a run with no end aborted after its step budget; a timeout unrouted and routed, the clock at the bound, the run's bound, the tester's stop with the step cut short, a wait for a duration; an event moving the clock to its row with its columns and fields, an await timing out, a read of a table portal never fills an error; a write the switch refuses mid-run and one the plant refuses once the server is gone, each an error kept with portal's words; a function's typed inputs, outputs, loops, a call inside it, an empty range, a refused input type, an unassigned output, its await's and its wait's timeouts within its bound; the reference's arithmetic, comparisons and built-ins over numbers, texts, truth values, moments and durations, and their faults; refute round 1's findings: a column read through an awaited row in a step, a script box (through a name assigned it) and a function refused at binding, the rows' own fields bound, and an armed test so refused writing nothing; a call inside a call ending at its caller's 300 ms, not the run's bound; a loop of no statement ended by the tester's stop and by the run's bound of 1 s; durations and moments past their range, `-` and `abs` at the negative end, `round` past the range, a duration times 1e300 each an error, a loop to the largest whole number ending there; a stamp at which one table's column turns as another's turns back judged with both, the wait timing out; three awaits taking two rows of one stamp and the next, and a function's loop counting four rows three of which share a stamp; every criterion, value reported and write to the listener inside its step, equal to the run's; refute round 2's findings: a copy of an awaited row standing before its await in the text, reached by the routes, refused with nothing written, and in a function through two copies in a loop; a name rebound by an await of another table bound and run, refused only when no table it may hold has the column (both named); three awaits over a view's rows, two sharing a stamp, taking each once, and a function's loop of awaits over the view counting 10; a wait over two keys of one table at one stamp agreeing with the check after it, the stamp's rows in either order; a function's wait past its bound of 1 s timing out once the machine has passed it, the write after it and its reaction after, and a step's wait of 5min under a run's bound of 500 ms ending at the bound once it has passed; the run's bound passing inside a wait leaving the clock at it, its own window replayed naming the same bound; `round` and `int` of whole numbers past 2^53, `int` past 64 bits an error, a duration times 1.0, 2.0, 0.5, 0.1 and a number times a duration exact; refute round 3's findings: three runs whose settle or script box puts the machine past their bound (a wait of 800 ms then a check under 1 s, a read under 200 ms, a box of 3 000 000 turns under 300 ms) passing live and replayed alike; a step past the bound running no further action (a write after a wait past it never sent), and a write's answer past it ending the step with the clock left at the answer; a loop of no statement ended by its budget of 10 000 000 statements; a row copied through `min` and `max` refused with nothing written, in a step and in a function; whole numbers past 2^53 compared with floats and numeric texts exactly; the architect's Q314 (b): a writing test on a session whose switch is off refused unconfirmed and confirmed for another list, the fingerprint named and nothing written; confirmed, the switch armed at its write and off after the run, the write landed; a run that aborts disarming too; a switch found armed left armed; a plant handed no arming refused as before, confirmed or not
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-202.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-203: Replay: a test run over a stored window of a portal session on the rows' own stamps, writes noted and never sent, a live run's own window replayed to its verdict and values
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-comissionador": checks run in real time or replay historical windows; docs/design/comissionador/README.md "Live or replay", "A failed live run can be replayed from its own window"; docs/test-schema.md "Time"; docs/questions.md Q315, taken meanwhile)
- **Description:** `run::run` with `RunOptions::replay(from, to)` runs a test over the stored window `[from, to]` of a session's store with the same engine as a live run (C-202): the clock starts at the window's start and moves only by the rows' stamps, a wait's or a call's bound and a wait for a duration, never by the machine's clock, so a replay waits for nothing; values stored before the window's start are the latest at it; a write and a send are noted "not sent: replay" and never reach the plant, whatever the switch (Q315); a step the window ends inside is cut short and the run aborted with the window's end named; a test whose `modes` lack `replay`, or a window that ends before it starts, is refused before anything runs. A live run's own window, replayed, reaches the live run's verdict at its step, its reported values, its criteria and its events' stamps when no step right after a write reads plant data (a read, a check, a report, a wait, an await) before the time the live run took to send it and have it answered has passed (at least the read's settle, 250 ms, after a step that read the plant): a replay's write moves no clock, where the live one moved it to the plant's answer, so such a step finds the plant as it stood before the write, a wait may time out where it held live and an await take an earlier row (Q318, the claim narrowed meanwhile); the same window replayed twice gives the same run to the millisecond. The tester's stop ends a replay as it ends a live run, looked at in every step, wait and write; the run's bound, on the run's clock in both modes, replays to the same step. The clock never passes the window: a wait for a duration, a function's included, that the window ends inside is cut short with the window's end named, before the run's bound or a call's. Replay over a window a session no longer holds (a snapshot read only) is later work, named in the plan. Distinct from the live run (C-202), whose test alone proves the binding and every action, route, outcome and verdict: this clause proves what the mode changes (the clock moved by stored stamps alone, the window's end, writes never sent, a live-only test and a reversed window refused) and that a live run's window replays to its verdict and values, twice alike. Distinct from portal's backfill (C-037), which replays a log's history into the store: a replay here runs a test over rows already stored, a backfilled window among them. Limits: a row stored after a replay but stamped inside its window (a backfill still running, C-037; a late line) changes the next replay, and rows a store kept as a window has dropped (C-090), or a window ending past the store's now, are not detected.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-comissionador/tests/replay.rs | against a portal session over the bench (its OPC UA server, the controller reference's machine): the worked example run live, then its window replayed in under three seconds to the same verdict, step and reason, the same steps once the lengths of the waits are set aside, the same reported values and criteria, the two sends noted not sent, the function's twenty outcomes ending at the live run's stamp, the window's start the live run's, the same window replayed again equal in every field; a write and a wait on its reaction run live, the plant put back and the switch turned off, the window replayed passing with the write not sent, the reaction met at the live run's stamp, the speed read 2, nothing at the plant; over a window of the session's past: a condition true at its start holding at once, a timeout of 500 ms reached at once on the replay's clock with the clock at its bound, a wait the window ends inside cut short and aborted at the window's end by its text, a wait for a duration inside the window and past its end, an event at its own stamp, a test that runs live only and a window ending before its start refused; refute round 1's findings: a wait for 5 s under a run's bound of 2 s over a window of 900 ms, and a function's under a call bound of 2 s, each cut short at the window's end by its text, the clock at it; a write after a step that read the plant, live moving the clock by at least the settle and the wait after it passing, replayed moving nothing and the wait timing out, the limit Q318 names; refute round 2's finding: an await right after a write with a loose bound taking a row stamped before the write when replayed, where live it took one after the plant's answer, and a read after it finding the plant before the write (`run` 0), pinned as the narrowed claim's limit; refute round 3's findings: a replay of a step routed back to itself, or retried 1000 times, stopped by the tester before it starts and 200 ms in; a live run's bound met after a settle replaying to the same verdict
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-203.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-223: Byte-faithful edits of a workbook Ábaco did not write
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-abaco", scope: "byte-faithful `.xlsx` edits with no Office on the machine"; Veredas 1's AB9, the architect's ruling of 2026-11-04; docs/abaco-plan.md phase 2; P-007)
- **Description:** `veredas_abaco::patch::patch` applies a list of edits (values, formulas, clears, rows and columns inserted or deleted, a new sheet, defined names set or deleted, column widths, row and column outlines, fills, borders), each seeing the workbook as the edits before it left it, to a workbook Ábaco did not write, and splices the result into the original package: every part no edit touched is copied as it was, byte for byte; inside a touched sheet a `<c>` element changes only when an edit wrote it, a move rewrote its formula or position, its shared group had to be expanded (a move reaching any member's formula), or the edits moved its value: formulas and the cells an array spills into, evaluated before and after alike (one seed, one time), values told apart as Excel tells them (an empty cell is neither 0 nor `""`, numbers to 15 significant digits); a formula calling a volatile function, directly or through defined names resolved by scope (a LAMBDA name called included), the cells it spills into, and a cell whose value moves with them both before the edits and after them never counted as moved (one the edits made deterministic gets its value); an array that shrinks leaves no constant behind, one that grows is written where it now spills (the empty cells the file holds there included), one a move cuts into stays whole as Excel spills it again, its anchor's `ref` following the engine where the engine and the file agreed on its size, and a cell no array reaches any more goes, volatile or not; a row's missing `r` never a reason to touch a sheet. References are re-spelled wherever they point (every sheet, defined names, validations, conditional formats, comments, threaded comments, drawings and their shapes' text links, charts and chartEx charts on worksheets and on chart sheets with their data label ranges and their user shapes' text links, form controls' linked cells and ranges, tables with their totals rows and sorts, an autoFilter's filtered columns and its sort; a column width or outline keeping what survives of its columns), a range a deletion cuts into shrinking, a cell of another workbook keeping its address, and a format's or validation's formula re-anchored when its range loses its top-left cell, as in Excel; a move that cuts into a table, or an edit of its header row, is refused, a table moved whole moves in the file and in the engine; a formula is written in the file's spelling (`_xlfn.`, `_xlpm.`, every function as Excel spells it, fork change 25) whatever spelling it came in, a structured reference in one an edit writes as the cells it reads, and one that only an array formula computes as the engine does (a part that may not be single under an operator, or one the engine evaluates as an array) written as Excel 365's dynamic array of one cell, with its metadata; a text reads back as written, carriage returns and control characters included; a refreshed cache drops the metadata that marked an error it no longer is; a chart sheet's part is left as it is and the sheet counted in a defined name's `localSheetId`, a name local to one kept as the file's, its references moved; a package without a styles part gets one only when an edit makes a format; an edit may state what it expects to find (applied, already there, or a conflict and skipped), a plain formula of the file being the array an edit writes only where Excel 365 shows it with no implicit intersection, as the edits left it; a clear leaves the cells an array spills into from outside it to the array, as Excel does, and a cell an edit clears that an array spills into then is that array's; a new sheet takes no name a sheet of the file has, chart sheets included, nor `History`; a name reading as a cell in any case is refused; a value edit takes only the errors a cell holds as a value; a new cell takes its row's format, else its column's; a formula or a name calling a function the engine does not compute is refused; a `#SPILL!` or `#CALC!` is cached as the `#VALUE!` every reader takes; SpreadsheetML written under a namespace prefix is refused (its namespace read as XML reads an attribute), and so is a chart's, a chartEx's, a drawing's (transitional or strict), an extension's, a threaded comment's or a VML element under another prefix than Excel's own, a part declaring another encoding than UTF-8, and a part holding a CDATA section, an XML comment, a processing instruction or an end tag with white space before its `>`; a structural edit deleting a scenario's changing cell, a data consolidation's source area or a published range is refused, naming it; an attribute is read as XML reads it (a raw tab a space, a character reference its character, `A&#53;` the cell A5), and so is the text of an element holding a number or an area (a drawing's anchor, a note's row, a sparkline's place, a moved cell's cached value), a shared group's members (a child written with an end tag is a child) and the dynamic-array entry of the metadata part; a watched cell, a cell's smart tags, a scenario's changing cells and selection, a consolidation's source area, a custom view's pane and selection and a published range follow their cells; the workbook is computed as the destination it is written to (the engine spelling its file `.xlsx` in `CELL("filename")` whatever its kind, which Excel recomputes on opening). The new file is, verification being on unless the caller turns it off, re-read through the engine from a copy at a short path under the system's temporary folder, named as the destination (every cell as the model left it, a formula as the file spells it, every moved value's written cache the engine's, and a value an edit wrote where an array spills), then written beside the destination under a name of its own and renamed over it only if the source still holds what was read, the previous version kept as `.bak` (a previous one replaced), the destination's permissions kept; a failed verification, a moved source or a refused edit leaves the destination as it was. On every workbook among the fork's test files (266), a patch with nothing to do leaves every part as it was; on each of the 234 holding a plain number, changing it rewrites only worksheets, and in them only that cell and cells whose value moved, their formulas unchanged, every computed cell whose value moved holding the new value in the file; rows and columns inserted and deleted and names set on each are verified or refused with nothing written, the 13 refusals pinned with their reasons (tables, pivot tables, the engine's own array and sheet-edge refusals, a value hidden under a malformed merge). Excel's acceptance of a written file is owed on a machine with Excel (Veredas 1 had it on the Windows desk).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-abaco/tests/patch.rs, crates/veredas-abaco/tests/patch_untouched.rs, crates/veredas-abaco/tests/patch_no_styles.rs, crates/veredas-abaco/tests/patch_shapes.rs, crates/veredas-abaco/tests/patch_corpus.rs, crates/veredas-abaco/src/patch/mod.rs (unit tests) | Veredas 1's patcher tests carried: a hand-built package with shared formulas, a merge, a conditional format, a validation, a note, defined names, custom properties, printer settings and a calculation chain, each edit kind's exact written XML and every other part byte-identical; untouched cells byte for byte beside formulas the engine reads otherwise than their cache; a package with no styles part. ## C-224: The `.abaco` file: a workbook born in Ábaco and its workspace, as stamped TOML parts in a zip

## C-072: The EtherNet/IP scanner: one Class 1 connection, every step bounded, the adapter's refusals by their status
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: the four fieldbus masters in core, the EtherNet/IP scanner on a young Rust crate; docs/controlador-plan.md "Networks and devices"); promoted in part from P-018
- **Description:** `veredas_core::fieldbus::enip::Scanner` (behind core's feature `enip`, off by default) opens one Class 1 connection to an adapter: the encapsulation session, then a Forward_Open naming the configuration, output and input assemblies, each direction's size its data with the 16-bit sequence count and the run/idle header where it has one, the Sockaddr Info items by the standard's numbers (0x8001 where the inputs go, 0x8000 where the outputs go); then it produces its outputs at the granted interval, run or idle, and consumes the adapter's inputs from the adapter alone (the address its session reached, the stream's peer, or the one its reply names for the outputs), this connection's id and size, a repeated sequence count no new data and nothing else keeping the connection alive. A connection outside what the 16-bit Forward_Open carries, or with an IPv6 address for the adapter or the I/O socket (the Sockaddr Info items carry IPv4 alone), is refused before anything is sent; connecting, the session and every request are bounded in time; an adapter's refusal comes back with its general and extended status; the outputs go at the granted O→T interval from the open on; the first input is awaited the standard's initial watchdog (10 s, or the timeout when longer) from the end of the open, and an adapter silent for the granted T→O interval times 4 << the multiplier after an input has lost the connection, nothing more produced once that time has passed; a close or a drop sends the Forward_Close and unregisters whatever its answer, bounded (`docs/core-plan.md`, "The fieldbus masters"; the scanner is core's on the `ethernetip-*` crates' codecs, `docs/questions.md` Q102).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/enip_scanner.rs, tools/mestres/tests/enip.rs | against a scripted adapter over real TCP and UDP: RegisterSession, the Forward_Open, the Forward_Close and UnRegisterSession byte for byte as the standard lays them out by hand, every output frame's items, counts and run/idle header, about 50 frames a second each way at 20 ms, a repeated count no update; Wireshark's dissectors reading the whole conversation with no complaint, the sizes, intervals and serial as sent and the Sockaddr items 0x8001 and 0x8000 each with its port; every bound refused before a connection with its exact reason (an IPv6 I/O socket and an IPv6 adapter among them, a listening IPv6 adapter reached by no connection) and the limits taken, 16-bit instances in the path; the adapter's refusal with its extended words, a refused session, silence and a wrong context, encapsulation status, service, originator or a granted interval of 0 each named, a refused port and a full listen queue bounded, an I/O port taken; inputs from another host, id or size or unreadable counted stray and not taken, from the session's address and from the address the reply named both taken and from a third not, an unspecified address in the reply read as the session's, an adapter named by the unspecified address judged by the peer its session reached (Linux), its input from that peer taken when its reply names another I/O address and a third host's stray, run and idle inputs read, modeless outputs carrying the count and data alone; a silent adapter lost at its timeout, stray frames of every kind sprayed meanwhile (another host, another id, this id at another size, no frame at all) keeping nothing alive, the multiplier 1 and 7 carried by the Forward_Open, an adapter that never sends lost at the timeout from the open with its outputs gone from the open on, an open slower than the timeout running and a silent one lost at its first-input window after the open returned, the window the longer of the two as the grant makes it (a slower grant at multiplier 1 giving 800 ms over a 100 ms window; multiplier 7 giving 10.24 s over the standard's 10 s), strays of every kind ending no first-input window and an input inside it taken, a window without end (`Duration::MAX`) still running and producing past the timeout and past the standard's 10 s, then closing as any other, a first input taken whatever its count (0 included), a flood of stray datagrams from another host holding no output back (40 to 60 a second produced and received, the link up at multiplier 7, long enough that the kernel's own drops of the adapter's inputs under the flood do not end it), nothing produced after, its close asking nothing; each direction at its own granted interval (10 ms out, 100 ms in, a 400 ms timeout held), a granted interval of 0 either way refused; a refused or unanswered close said and still unregistered; a drop closing bounded, even against a silent close. Against the bench's EtherNet/IP adapter (C-111), a device written apart: outputs landing in its output assembly only while run, the plant side's inputs reaching the scanner, its own record of the connection what the scanner asked (assemblies, ids, the originator's triple, intervals, where it sends), about 50 frames a second both ways, a close freeing the output at once; its refusals 0x0127, 0x0128, 0x012A, 0x012B, 0x0111 and 0x0106 come back by status; a stalled adapter lost within its timeout and timing the scanner out in turn; a restart lost and a new connection opened; a silent session bounding the open; a stopped adapter not connected
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-072.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-113: The emulated EtherCAT segment: SubDevices answering a master's frames on a raw interface
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: an emulated EtherCAT device answering on a raw network card; P-020; docs/briefing/review.md entry 27; docs/maquinar-plan.md "P-020: the bench devices, as they land")
- **Description:** `ethercat::start` runs an `EthercatConfig` (TOML, every optional key defaulted, a misspelled key refused; a bench file's `[[ethercat]]`, C-033) as a segment of SubDevices on a named network interface, raw frames through aranha's link (Linux; elsewhere refused by name), its configuration refused by name before the interface opens (an empty interface, no device, a name empty, padded or twice, a product name not 1 to 255 ASCII characters, an entry of 0 or over 64 bits, more than 255 entries or 1024 bytes a way): each frame of commands (type 1; any other unanswered, counted unreadable) passes through the devices in order and goes back on the same wire, its source's locally administered bit set as a first device sets it; each device an ESC of registers and 8 KB of process RAM answering every register as stored (a write running past its end storing the bytes inside it), those EtherCAT only reads kept (the type to the features, the alias, the DL and AL status and code, each SyncManager's status and PDI control, the receive times, the system time difference), with the working counter the standard gives each command (auto-increment by position, the address back three more than sent; configured address, or the alias once DL control's bit 24 is set; broadcast ORed and counted by every device, a BRW writing each device the data as it reaches it, its position field advanced by each device as an ESC does; logical through its FMMUs bit by bit, an FMMU that reads and writes writing what arrived and returning what it held; read-multiple-write; a read counted 1, a write 1, a read-write 3, a logical write 1 and an LRW's write 2), its AL state machine (a transition it refuses flagged with code 0x0011, an unknown state 0x0012, BOOT never taken since its EEPROM states no bootstrap mailbox 0x0013, held until acknowledged, INIT clearing it), its SII EEPROM read through the registers (identity, name, General with ports 0 and 1 MII, FMMU, SyncManager and PDO categories built from the file, its size stated, no mailbox), its distributed clock (receive times latched by a write to port 0's, port 1 40 ns later, the written data unkept; system time live with the master's offset; a written system time, by ARMW, FRMW or a plain write covering its first byte (0x0910, as the ESC datasheet has it), compared and its difference kept in sign and magnitude, held to 31 bits, a 32-bit one the short way round across its wrap). Functional, never exact in time, and no more: no SyncManager watchdog (a silent master leaves its devices in OP), no SyncManager direction or buffer rules, the IRQ field never set, an EEPROM write or reload answered as done with no effect. The plant side reads the outputs a master wrote whatever the AL state, sets the inputs it reads, faults a device (from OP to SAFE-OP, below it keeping its state, the error flagged with a code) and silences the segment. An EtherCAT MainDevice the bench did not write brings it to OP and exchanges its process data.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/ethercat_segment.rs | in a private network of the test's own (aranha's `netns::isolated`, a veth pair): ethercrab 0.7.1 finding three devices in order with their EEPROM names, vendors, products, revisions and serials, giving them the addresses the segment then holds, PRE-OP after its init, OP after its transition, outputs written by it read in the device and inputs set on the plant side read by it, a faulted device reported by it with its code and the others still in OP, a silent segment failing its exchange and answering again; hand-built frames: BRD counted 3 with the type, the source's bit set on the way back, APWR by position and none past the last device, FPRD of the alias and none for an address no device holds, FPRW's old bytes and counter 3, INIT to OP refused with the error flag and code 0x0011 and PRE-OP taken with the acknowledge, LRW through two devices' FMMUs written and read with counter 3, LRD and LWR alone counted 1, the EEPROM's words through the SII registers, ARMW of the system time counted 3, a device faulted in INIT and one in PRE-OP each keeping its state with the code read back, the plant side's unknown device and wrong input size by their texts, a silent segment returning nothing then answering; tshark reading the frame as sent and as returned (counters 0, then 3, 1, 1) with no complaint; the reference's bench-file block started through `Bench::start`, found by ethercrab by name, its address the part's, taken to OP; an interface that does not exist named; every configuration refusal by its exact text from `validate` and from `start`, and the bounds accepted (255 entries, 1024 bytes, 64 bits, 255 characters); the ESC core called directly with its clock handed in: BRD ORed and counted 3, BWR 3, BRW 9 written as it arrives, APRD's address back three more, APRW's old bytes and 3, configured commands 1, 1, 3 and 0, a frame of another type not read; two 1-bit inputs at bits 0 and 1 of one logical byte gathered with the byte's other bits kept, a write of logical bit 1 onto physical bit 0 setting and clearing it alone, a span from bit 4 of one byte to bit 3 of the next, a read-write FMMU under an LRW, an LWR through two devices counted 2 and an LRW reading one and writing two counted 5; receive times latched per device to the nanosecond with port 1 40 ns later, an ARMW handing a 1 ms offset down with differences 0x80000000 | 1 001 000 and 1 002 000, a device ahead with bit 31 clear, a low 32-bit write compared, the system time read live, a difference over 31 bits held at its most; eleven read-only registers unchanged by writes, the station and the offset written, 0x0006 reading 8 and RAM kept at 0x2100 and 0x2FFE; the AL machine's refusal held against a request without the acknowledge, an unknown state 0x0012, the transitions both ways, INIT clearing an error; the alias answering only once enabled; the EEPROM's General port bytes and its size for a small and a large image; round 2's pins: a type-4 frame holding a good APRD unanswered and counted unreadable, and the core refusing a whole type-2 payload; PRE-OP to OP and PRE-OP, SAFE-OP and OP to BOOT refused 0x0011, INIT to BOOT refused 0x0013 and cleared by INIT; logical bit 0 read from physical bit 3 and logical bit 8 written to physical bit 5; the EEPROM's word 4 the alias, General's order and name indexes, SM2's direction written and SM3's read; all eight SyncManagers' status and PDI control kept; a difference ahead over 31 bits held at 0x7FFFFFFF; a BRD of the system time read live; BRD, BWR and BRW back with their position advanced 3; round 3's pins: a low 32-bit write 512 ns either way across the wrap read as 0x200 and 0x80000200, halfway round as the most behind, a 4-byte FRMW across the wrap read as an 8-byte one is; a write of 4 bytes at 0x2FFE and an FMMU of 2 at 0x2FFF storing the bytes inside the RAM, zeros read past it; an EEPROM write and a reload done, not busy, no error, the words unchanged; the IRQ field back as each datagram carried it; the EEPROM's FMMU usage (outputs, inputs) and its PDO entries' index, subindex and bits as the file gives them; ethercrab's segment in OP after 300 ms of silence and answering; a device faulted in SAFE-OP keeping SAFE-OP flagged; ports 2 and 3's receive times kept; the missing interface's refusal by its whole text. round 4's pins: an LWR, an LRD and an LRW through two FMMUs of one direction counted once each way (1, 1, 3); an FMMU never activated or deactivated by its activation byte alone mapping nothing and counted 0; a datagram starting inside an FMMU (logical 0x102 and 0x202) and one ending inside it mapping the FMMU's bytes there, a bit-wise span read from its second logical byte; ethercrab bringing a device of 1024 bytes each way to OP and exchanging every byte; OP to SAFE-OP and SAFE-OP to PRE-OP taken, by hand and by ethercrab's `into_safe_op` and `into_pre_op`; a device faulted in OP acknowledged in SAFE-OP (0x14) and back in OP, faulted in PRE-OP acknowledged there (0x12); a write of the high half alone compared not at all, a 12-byte write from 0x0910 compared; an FRMW addressed to the second device written by the first. The test runs on Linux; the refusal elsewhere is not run here; round 5's pins: INIT taken from PRE-OP and SAFE-OP, and an error held in PRE-OP, SAFE-OP and OP cleared by INIT without the acknowledge, a plant fault of a device in OP too; the EEPROM's mailbox words (0x14 to 0x1C) all 0; a write from 0x090C covering 0x0910 compared over 64 bits (12 bytes) and over the low 32 (8 bytes), a broadcast blank of the DC block from 0x0900 latching every device and comparing a system time of 0; outputs a master wrote read in SAFE-OP and in INIT.
