## C-114: `fisica`: a plant's scene, its bodies' motion at a fixed step and what its sensors see
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: `fisica`, bodies, motion, which sensor an object reached, headless at a fixed step; P-020; docs/questions.md Q160, decided 2026-11-25: Rapier; docs/briefing/review.md entry 31; docs/maquinar-plan.md "`fisica`, as designed before its code")
- **Description:** `fisica::load` reads a scene file (`veredas-cena` version 1, docs/cena-schema.md: the header by core's reader, every key the reference lists with its default, a misspelled key in any table refused, every range and name rule refused by its place) and `World::new` builds it on Rapier 0.36.1 in double precision (`rapier3d-f64`) with `enhanced-determinism`, named only in fisica's world module, its contacts worked out anew at every step, so a part far from the origin (within the reader's 10000 m) moves as one near it: fixed boxes; belts whose top face (their own +z) carries what lies on it toward the belt's speed along the belt's own x, whichever collider of the contact pair the belt is, a part at its side or under it not carried, its speed changed while the scene runs, every body touching a running belt woken before each step (the engine puts a slow body to sleep); bodies falling along -z with their mass and friction and coming to rest on what they fall on at any step, a contact's friction the mean of its two, a body resting on a fixed part 0.07 mm deep at 9.81 m/s² whatever its mass (from a milligram), size (from a millimetre, the least the reader takes), the step or how far from the origin, the depth growing with gravity (0.7 mm at 100), and on a lighter body sinking into it as the engine solves a stack (2.6 mm at ten times its mass at 9.81, more the heavier); movers reaching each placed pose at the next step and pushing without being pushed; sensors, a box or a ball, holding the bodies and movers inside them, never a fixed shape or a belt, each change an event of the step that sees it, one step after the first step whose poses overlap (the engine detects before it moves), within a step by sensor then body, and a body removed leaving every sensor at once, its leavings after the step's events in the order of removal. A pose's angles are any finite degrees, each reduced to a turn before use; read back, yaw and roll within ±180° and pitch within ±90°. `World::step` advances one fixed step; the world reads its steps, its time (`fisica::time_at` of the step and the steps, both counted in 64 bits; `time_at` pinned past 2^32 steps, the world's own count read), every body's and mover's pose (centre, rotation, velocity, its angles back), every sensor's contents and the events (taken when read, past 65 536 unread the oldest dropped and counted); a change is refused by name and by kind, or out of its range, leaving the world as it was. The same scene and the same inputs give the same run bit for bit, and two worlds share nothing. `fisica::start` runs a world on a thread of its own at one step per step of real time, a step begun more than one step after its time (judged once it has the world, whatever held it: an oversleep, the handle, the step before) taken at once and counted late, never skipped; its handle reads and changes the world between two steps and takes hooks run on the scene's thread before each step, the world in hand (C-115's models); `fisica::paused` builds a scene that runs only once `run` is called, so what is added to it first (a hook) is there before its first step, and a paused scene stopped never runs; stop and drop end the thread, the last state kept.

## C-115: `reflexo`'s first half: drive, conveyor, sensor and actuator models behind a device's points
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (the briefing of 13 to 16 November 2026: `reflexo`, ready-made models for drive, sensor, conveyor and actuator; P-020; docs/maquinar-plan.md "`reflexo`'s first half, as designed before its code")
- **Description:** A bench file's `[[reflexo]]` part (docs/bench-schema.md, its sixth example and its keys) holds models that make a device's points behave as the machine behind them, reading and writing `<part>.<point>` of the file's Modbus servers and EtherNet/IP adapters (a bool as 0 or 1, a number converted to the point's type: an integer rounded and held to its range, NaN written as 0 whatever the type) and moving the scene of its `[[fisica]]` part: a drive ramps its speed toward its setpoint while it runs (negated while reversed) at its acceleration away from 0 and its deceleration toward 0 or a smaller setpoint, through 0 when the sign changes in one ramp (the step that passes 0 carrying what is left of it at the acceleration, so a reversal takes the sum of its two times, a step at its zero's time within the slack on 0 exactly), landing on the setpoint exactly at the first step at or after its time however long the ramp (its steps worked out once, with a slack relative to their count and a billionth of a step besides, `reflexo::ramp_steps`, a count past 64 bits, an infinite setpoint's among them, held at the largest and the speed going on at its rate), and writes its speed, running and at-speed points (running while the speed is not 0; at speed while its run is on and the speed within its tolerance of the setpoint as negated, a setpoint of 0 included); a conveyor's drive sets its belt's speed (its ratio, held to the scene's 100 m/s); a sensor model writes its scene sensor's contents to a bool point (inverted when normally closed) once the scene has held them without a break for its on or off delay, at the first step at or after it; an actuator moves its mover between two poses along the straight line at an even speed over its stroke, at an end at the first step at or after its time, and writes its end switches. A part's models step together on the scene's thread before each of its steps from its first, at the scene's step (the bench builds every scene before any runs), in this order: drives, conveyors, actuators, sensors, each kind in the file's order, two parts moving one scene one after the other in the file's order; without a scene, every 4 ms on a thread of their own, a step never before its time; a model that cannot read or write a point is skipped for the rest of the step, and a step in which any model failed is counted once, its last error named. Refused by place when the bench file loads: a scene, belt, sensor, mover, part or point not there, a reference not `<part>.<point>`, a point of the other kind, a point written by two models of one part or of two, a name empty, padded or twice, a conveyor, sensor or actuator without a scene, a ramp, tolerance or ratio not finite or out of its range, a stroke or delay out of its range, a pose a scene file refuses.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-maquinar/tests/reflexo.rs | stepped by the test: a drive stopped with its points written, then at 0.4 a 4 ms step to exactly 50 at the 125th step with at-speed once within its tolerance, down to a smaller setpoint at its deceleration, stopped at 0 on the 62nd step, reversed to -50 and forward again through 0; a conveyor setting its belt at its speed times its ratio at every step, the carton carried 0.5 m in a second at speed, a belt held to -100 m/s; a sensor model on at the third 4 ms step of a 10 ms delay and off at the fifth of 20 ms, its normally closed twin inverted at once, a visit shorter than the delay never seen; an actuator's 100 ms stroke in 25 steps of 0.04, the mover on the line and turning with it, its switches at each end only, retracting from mid-stroke; every Modbus and adapter point type written and read back through the conversion (a bool from 2 and NaN, u16 held at 65535 and 0, 2.5 rounded to 3, i16, u32, i32, f32, u8 and i8 held); a refused write counted and named with the other writes done; 38 refusals through the bench file by their exact text and a scene file that does not load, or is missing, by its path; the reference's sixth example driven by a Modbus client the bench did not write: stopped with the beam clear and the pusher back, the conveyor at its setpoint after its half-second ramp (124 of the scene's 4 ms steps at least) and running, the beam broken by the carton and clear again, the pusher out after its half-second stroke (125 steps at least) and not back, the status lines, no error; drives without a scene on a thread of their own reaching their setpoint, no step after the stop; refute round 1's pins: every actuator stroke from 1 ms to 1 s at each end at the first step at or after its time both ways, a 300 ms stroke in 75 steps out and 75 back with the mover, a 40 ms one in 10; ramps of up to 107 143 steps landing exactly at their step and not before, up and down; a drive run by a sensor model's point starting the step after the point turns on, a drive run by another's running point starting in the same step; a reversed drive at speed at -50; three visits each shorter than the on delay never seen; two models failing in one step counted once with the last error; models on a scene of 10 ms steps gaining 1 unit a scene step at 100 a second; a second scene of 10 000 fixed parts built after the line's, the line's models stepped before each of its steps and the carton not moved; drives without a scene never ahead of their 4 ms steps and at most 200 ms behind over a second; an f32 point's NaN written as 0; round 2's pins: conveyors stepping after drives, actuators after conveyors and sensors after actuators, each kind in the file's order (a conveyor run by another's running point, an actuator by another's end switch, the last of two failing sensors named); every adapter point type converted (a bool from NaN, u16 300 and 65535 from 70000, i16 -5, u32 2.6 to 3, i32 -7, f32 NaN as 0 and 1.25); an actuator whose switch write fails skipped for the rest of its step; ramps of 21 262 500 steps and more counted at their step, and short ones as before; a stop waiting for a scene's step held inside a point's read, no step after it; round 3's pins: an infinite f32 setpoint a Modbus client wrote ramped at 0.4 a step for 200 steps and left at the deceleration onto 50 at the 75th, a conveyor's belt 0.004 m/s after a step at it, `ramp_steps` at its largest for an infinite count; two parts on one scene, the conveyor's first a step behind the next part's drive, the drive's first in its step; at speed with the run on at a setpoint of 0 (not running) and not with it off; every integer type of both devices rounding half away from 0 and an unsigned one holding at 0; a belt held to +100 and -100 m/s without an error; a conveyor whose write fails leaving its belt and an actuator whose read fails neither moving nor writing a switch, both named; an off delay outlasting three 8 ms absences and passed by 12 ms; round 4's pins: a drive whose setpoint read fails keeping its speed and writing none of its points, and one whose running write is refused writing no at-speed after it; an actuator between poses differing in x, y, z, yaw, pitch and roll on the line at each of its 25 steps; nine refusals of a point not there or of the other kind at an actuator's extend and retracted switch, a sensor model's point and a drive's speed and at-speed; a drive on an adapter's points through the bench reaching its setpoint; a part without a scene through the bench, its plan line every 4 ms, a step of 1 unit at 250 a second and never ahead of its time; reversals whose deceleration ends exactly on a step on 0 there, not running.

## C-204: The run record and the acceptance report: every run kept with the exact text that ran, the rows it read and its report, read back, listed, noted and signed
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-comissionador": an acceptance report for every run; docs/design/comissionador/README.md "Runs are kept"; docs/report-schema.md; docs/questions.md Q312, (a) and (d) taken meanwhile; C-045)
- **Description:** `record::keep` keeps a run of C-202 or C-203 under `runs/<id, lowercased>/<nnnn>/` (`10000` after `9999`), the next number of the test's folder taken by creating its folder (one already there passed over, a number past the last a run can take refused by name, and a run whose moments a report cannot hold refused before any folder is made): the exact text of the test that ran (`test.test`), every function file it used (`functions/<n>.fn`, its `use` path in the report), the rows of every table the test reads stamped in the run's window, oldest first, read once the window's end is 250 ms past for a live run (C-202's settle), as CSV (`evidence/<table>.csv`: `time`, `arrival`, `source`, `key`, `seq` and the table's columns, the stamp and the arrival both on the store's axis, a cell quoted when it must be, a hole empty), and `report.toml` (`veredas-report`, version 1, `docs/report-schema.md`), written last and moved into place, so a folder holding a report holds a whole run: the test, the run (number, mode, session, window, verdict, the step that decided it, the reason), every step with its attempt, outcome (none for a step cut short), detail and times, every criterion, value reported and gated write, the evidence's files and counts, the tester's note and the signatures. `record::read` reads a report back, its stamp by core's header reader, a key the format does not know refused, and every value the reference gives a set or a form judged (the mode, the verdict, the outcomes, the roles, the moments, the attempts and the number from 1, the files where the folder keeps them, each table's evidence and each file once, in any case, by its name when plain and by its place among the evidence otherwise, a moment's form judged to the byte); `record::list` lists a test's runs newest first, each with its report or why it cannot be read, a folder without a report passed over, a test's folder, or a run's, that cannot be read named, never passed over; `record::note` sets the note; `record::sign` signs as `tester` or `witness` by a name holding a letter or a digit, at the plant's clock. A note or a signature reads, judges and writes the report under an operating system's lock on the folder's `report.lock`, so two changes at once never cross. A report keeps the roles its test names to sign (`test.sign`, Q312 (e)): each of them signs once, a role it does not name refused, and the report stands `awaiting <roles>` (`Report::standing`) until all have, then `signed`; with none named, the first signature signs it. A note on a report anyone has signed is refused, and so is a signature once it is `signed`, a correction being a new run; the refusal is the engine's, nothing sealed in the file, and `read` refuses a report written otherwise (a role the format does not know or named twice, a signature as a role not named or twice, two signatures where none is named). It keeps what C-201 read and what C-202 and C-203 did: the test's text as checked, and the run's result. Distinct from the store's own exports (C-041: a table, the whole database, a snapshot) and from the store written at a fault (C-093): the evidence here is a run's window of the tables its test reads, kept with the run.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-comissionador/tests/record.rs | the reference's worked example read as a report with every part as written, and refused with a key the format does not know, another format, another version and a key missing; the reference's worked example run live against a portal session over the bench and kept: its folder `runs/chk-012/0001` holding exactly the test's copy, the function file's copy, the two tables' evidence and the report, the copies byte for byte, the report read back with the run's number, mode, session, window, verdict, step and reason, the steps with their attempts, outcomes and details as the run gave them, the criterion, the three values reported, the two sends, no note and no signature; the function file's `use` path and copy, every step's moments (the next starting where the last ended), the criterion's text and measured value, the sends' answers; each table's evidence with its exact header, a line per row of the window counted as the report says and as the store holds, every cell of every row as the store holds it, the arrival on the store's axis; once noted and signed, the report's keys exactly the reference's; signed, a note and a second signature refused by their text and the file unchanged; the window replayed and kept as run 2, its writes not sent; a folder without a report passed over by the listing, newest first, and the next run kept past it as run 4; a run kept past `9999` as `10000` and listed, a report that does not read listed with its reason and hiding no other; a signature of no role the reference names or of no name, and a report changed by hand refused; refute round 1's findings: the reference's worked example refused with a value outside its key's set (mode, verdict, outcome, role, a blank or zero-width name), a moment out of its form, an attempt and a number of 0, a test, a function and an evidence file named otherwise than the folder keeps them; two signatures, or a note and a signature, at once on one report 60 times, one signature always kept and the other change refused as frozen or made before it; the executable run under a zone five hours east of UTC, the channel's rows read one moment in `time` and in `arrival`; CSV cells quoted for a comma, a quote and a line end; the reference's text held as a copy; refute round 2's findings: an evidence file out of the run's folder through its table's name (`../../0001/evidence/outcome`, `/abs`, `../../../../../../etc/passwd`), a plain table at `table-<n>`, a table of another name at another place, a table given twice, each refused with the file the folder keeps; eleven moments chrono read but the reference's form does not (a tab or a line end for the space, a blank for a digit, a signed year, a leap second, the 30th of February, a comma) refused; a `0000` folder no run; a test's folder that cannot be read, and one that is a file, named by `list`; a folder `4294967295` already there refusing the next run by name; refute round 3's findings: a run's folder that cannot be looked into listed with why among readable ones, and every run's folder so, never a list of no run; two entries at one file (a plain `table-1` beside a table placed first, `table-2` beside one placed second) and one table in two cases refused; a run starting in the year 10000 refused before any folder is made; the architect's Q312 (e): a report naming both roles awaiting both, then the one left, then signed, each role once, notes frozen at the first signature; one named refusing the other; none named signed and frozen by its first signature; reports written otherwise refused by `read` with their reasons; a run kept from a test that names roles carrying them, awaiting them

## C-095: Portal to portal, the reading side: another portal's HTTP interface read over a socket
- **Date:** 2026-11-22
- **Source:** user (docs/portal-plan.md "Decided in the briefing of November 2026": two portals speak portal's own HTTP interface, the event stream, the windowed reads, the gated write surface; docs/oficina-plan.md "Portal to portal"; P-019)
- **Description:** Under the feature `peer` (no store needed), `peer::PeerClient` reads another portal at an address over its own interface (C-044): the snapshot typed (the session's name, whether it runs, its clocks, its window, the switch, its sources) with the whole as the peer served it; the series listing; a series window (inclusive bounds, a key, a point budget the peer clamps) and a table's chart; the catalog; a page of a table's typed rows (a window, a limit, an offset); a tag read on demand, the one call of the gated surface that writes nothing; and the event stream, its hello, its data events as typed samples (source, series, key, stamp, value, count; a value, as a window's point, none where the peer served no finite number, NaN or an infinity, which JSON carries as `null`, the stream going on) and its lag events with their counts, an event of another kind skipped, read by the stream's own rules whatever the reads split. Every call is a connection of its own speaking HTTP/1.1 itself over a tokio socket (a GET closing its connection, the answer framed by chunks, extensions and trailers read past, by its length, or by the connection's end; no crate of the workspace given a new feature), every answer's JSON read by the reader itself, each number as its text says to the last bit, nesting refused where serde_json refuses it (the 128th level) (serde_json's default reading takes about one double in seven a unit off, and its exact reading is a feature of a crate the database engine builds with), its query values percent-encoded (a `+` and a `%` among them) and the peer's address its host, bounded: an answer within ten seconds (or the time given), else a timeout naming the address; an answer past 32 MiB refused before it is whole, a head past 64 KiB refused; a framing broken (a chunk size, a status line or a length that is no number) refused by shape, an answer cut short a broken link; a stream silent for 15 seconds (three of its server's keep-alives) taken for dead, the silence running from the last byte it gave across every call, so a caller dropping its calls more often still learns the link is gone, and a caller away past the silence on a stream that kept talking reads on; a silence set past the clock's reach is none, the stream waiting for its peer; a peer not there named by its address; an answer out of the shape refused, never guessed (a key the interface always writes, missing, refused; its `null` read as none); a refusal the peer's own text with its status. A stream that ends (the peer gone, a broken link, a silence) stays ended; the reader opens a new one. Reading it is cancel-safe: a call the caller drops while it waits (a timeout, a `select!`) loses no byte, the next call returning what was in flight, never a refusal of a peer that sent valid HTTP. No command: what alters a controller waits for docs/questions.md Q37.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/peer.rs | a portal serving a session over the bench's plant read by another over a real socket: the snapshot's name, running flag, start, no window, the switch off and its three sources by kind; the log's temperature listed as a float counted 40 with three keys and its last stamp, the listing sorted; a window of 40 downsampled to 10 with its exact endpoints, a narrower one whole with its exact points, a key giving 13, a budget of three clamped to ten, a series with nothing empty; the catalog's base tables, samples table and view, the readings' 40 rows and DOUBLE temperature, the view with no count; pages of five newest first and the last five ending on the oldest, the view's 19, a column charted to ten with its last point, an unknown table refused 404 by the peer's text, an unknown column refused; a tag read through the peer with its value and an unknown tag refused 404 by text; stopped, the peer read not running; the stream's hello, five samples with their exact stamps, values and counts, a reader that does not read lagging with the frames read and the counts its lag events carry summing to the 400 sent, the marker after them; the serving portal gone, the stream ended and ended again, a fresh stream from the portal served again opening with its hello; a fake peer's request with every value percent-encoded, its host and JSON asked for; a peer not there named; a peer that never answers a timeout of the time given; a stream silent for its silence, then ended; an answer past the limit refused; no JSON and JSON without the field refused by shape; a plain-text 503 as its text; a stream answered as JSON refused; a chunked answer split every seven bytes with an extension on each size line and a trailer, and an answer running to the connection's end, each read whole; a chunk size, a status line and a content length that are no number refused by shape with their exact text; an answer closed 91 bytes short of its length a broken link saying so; a head past 64 KiB refused; a log carrying NaN and an infinity between finite readings, served and read through the stream as none, the stream going on to the next finite one and the window holding all six in order; the defaults, a call to a peer that never answers ending at ten seconds and a silent stream at fifteen, while a real portal's idle stream, kept by its comments, stays open past seventeen; an answer past 32 MiB in chunks and one running to the connection's end refused, a head of two 40 KiB headers refused, a snapshot missing `window_ms` refused; each clock the one the peer named and the arrival this machine's own within five seconds; a page windowed on its time column (ten rows, newest first); the timeout naming the address; the request closing its connection. a stream read through a caller's 300 ms timeouts, a chunk's line end held back and the next size line cut in two, giving its two events in order and then its end; the stream's opening a timeout of the time given naming the address, and a 503 on it the peer's `busy`; a chart's window (ten exact points) and its key (13); a data event's sample missing its value or its key, a listing missing `last_ms`, a catalog missing `rows` and a tag read missing `value`, each refused by the field's name. a stream that goes quiet, read through 300 ms timeouts with a silence of 800 ms, `Silent` 0.7 to 1.6 s after its last byte and then closed; a chunked stream's trailers, their blank line held back 700 ms across dropped calls, ending it cleanly after its event; a snapshot missing `start_ms` refused by name; a data event cut inside its chunk, its second half 700 ms later, read through 300 ms timeouts whole and in order, then the end; a body stalling after its head a timeout of 500 ms naming the address, under 3 s; a snapshot with every clock, the window and the switch set apart read as served; a stream's event of an unknown kind and an unnamed one between two data events skipped, the stream going on to its end; ## C-096: A log says when its start's history is in the store
- **Date:** 2026-11-25
- **Source:** the architect (docs/questions.md Q319, decided 2026-11-25: "Portal says so", that portal publishes per log that its start's read-in has reached the end of every file as it was at the start, a replay waiting for that); docs/portal-plan.md "Decided in the briefing of November 2026"
- **Description:** Every log source of a session (C-043) says, in its health (`SourceHealth::history_read`, the snapshot's `history_read`, C-044), whether its start's history is in the store. Its worker (`FileTailSource`, `FamilyTailSource`, `with_history_read`) counts the lines it has sent once it has read to the end of every file as it stood when the start opened it: a family's backfill (C-037) at its seam, every member before it read whole, an empty member among them; a file read from its start at its end; a start at the end at once, its history skipped; a family not there, born after the start or with no active member yet, and a file not there, at once, whatever appears being new content. The session says the history read once that many of the log's lines are stored (its refresh comes after its flush, so the count it compares is of lines in the store), and a reader that waits for the flag finds the start's history whole, never part of it. Set once: lines that arrive after it are new. A source with no history to read (an OPC source, a channel, the controller) reads as read.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-portal/tests/session.rs | a family of eight members, the second to the sixth empty, 60 000 lines read from a start in the past: never said read while fewer than 60 000 rows are stored, then said with all of them; a file of 40 000 lines read from its start alike; a log started at its end over that file and a log not there each said read at once, no row stored
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-096.md
- **Status:** pending verification

## C-073: Core's layers by feature: the plant-facing ones by default, the header reader alone without them
- **Date:** 2026-11-23
- **Source:** ábaco's ask through the orchestrator (2026-11-23: the `.abaco` file read through core's one header reader, C-045, without the OPC UA client and a runtime); docs/core-plan.md "Features"
- **Description:** `veredas-core`'s feature `net`, on by default, holds the value model, the worker plumbing, the telegram layer and the OPC UA client layer and every crate they stand on, so every component keeping the default sees no change; without its default features core is the header reader (`schema`), the PCG32 (`rng`) and the state text (`state`), its normal dependencies serde, toml and thiserror alone and no crate of a plant's stack in its graph. The demo plant needs `net`; each fieldbus master (`modbus`, `enip`) stands alone on its framing and a runtime. Off by default all three, as C-071 and C-072 state. The promise holds for a crate built alone (its own `cargo build -p`, or a crate whose graph is resolved apart from the others), which is what a form here means: in a build of the whole workspace cargo unifies features across members, so a member asking for core without its default features gets the plant-facing layers there too when another member keeps them (cargo's doing, ábaco's Q345).
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-core/tests/features.rs | asked of cargo: the manifest's features as cargo reads them (`default` is `net`, `net`'s six crates, the demo plant on `net`, each master on its framing and tokio) and every normal or build dependency optional but serde, thiserror and toml; the graph cargo resolves without the default features (normal and build edges, every target), its direct dependencies exactly those three and none of the OPC UA crates, tokio, mio, socket2, regex or chrono anywhere in it; the default graph holding the plant-facing layers' six crates (the OPC UA client and types, tokio, socket2, regex, chrono) and not the demo plant's server; each master alone bringing its own framing and tokio and not the other's, nor OPC UA, regex or chrono; the library compiling in the default form alone (core's own tests and the workspace's own build never give core `net` alone; a member built alone does, portal in the releases), without the default features, with each master alone and with both; a consumer crate of the test's own, asking for core without its default features, reading a stamp and refusing three, writing and reading a state back and drawing the same numbers twice; and a consumer for each other form a member may ask for, alone, naming what it promises (the default: the crate root's re-exports the members import, the value model, the worker handle, the telegram channel and catalog, the OPC browser and worker; the default with the demo plant: the plant and its relay; each master alone: its client or scanner), each in a build folder of the test's own; every feature gate in core's source, read without its comments and with each attribute on one line, exactly the expected list, each gate counted (one written twice with one key counted twice) (`net` on the four plant-facing modules and the root's two re-exports, `demo-server` on the demo plant, each master on its module), and nothing that makes rustdoc see an item otherwise than the build (`doc(hidden)`, a cfg on `doc`); and, read in the code with its comments and literals blanked, the identifier `feature` nowhere but in a gate's `feature = ..` and `doc` nowhere but right after `#[`, so no macro carries a gate's name to a cfg it builds, and no cfg of the build profile (`debug_assertions`, `overflow_checks`, a cfg on `panic`) written anywhere, nor a `macro_rules!` whose body names `cfg`; nothing but the expected gates then depends on a feature, so what depends on the profile (a `debug_assert!`, an overflow) depends on it alike in every form, and the forms relate in a release build as in the development build every measurement here is made in; and, each form built alone as a crate built alone gets it (the workspace's unified build being none of them), the compiler's own view of each of the twenty forms (the plant-facing layer off, on, or on with the demo plant, each with `net` with the feature `default` or without it, a member keeping the default getting `default` too; each master off or on): rustdoc's pages of core with private items, read from core's source, and the pages of its public items as the build holds them, from a facade crate asking for core in that form and re-exporting all of it inlined, which rustdoc renders from core's compiled metadata where `cfg(doc)` was never set, every item's declaration and every anchor kept apart (an id of a doc comment's heading taking no other key's place), every item anchor with what it renders (a header, a field's type, a variant, a re-export, each link's title kept, so two types of one name are told apart, a dependency's type linked by a folder of its crate's name in the doc folder and its title checked on `SourceError::Pattern`) and every row of a module's table with its visibility mark, and every other line of a table (a glob's re-export) by its text, a page with an id twice or the blanket impls' heading out of rustdoc's place refused (an id in a doc comment); every step that adds `default` adds nothing, and every step that adds another feature keeps every key of the smaller form rendered the same and adds only where that feature's layer lies, and a form holds nothing in the layer of a feature it lacks (`net` under its four modules and the root's re-exports and rows to them, the demo plant under `opc::demo`, each master under its module and its line in `fieldbus`), however the gate is written; and for each form a consumer crate naming every public path the facade's pages show, checked by cargo against that form alone. The blanket impls of other crates and rustdoc's tooltip data are left out. The default form's consumer also names every path written from `veredas_core::` into the default form's layers in the other members' sources, read from their files at each run (`use` trees expanded, an expression's path up to its type, comments and literals left out). And the slim layer behaves the same in every form: a probe crate asking for each of the twenty forms alone, run, calls every public function and reads every public table of `schema`, `rng` and `state` (bar the PCG32's seeding from entropy) on the inputs the test lists: every stamp and check of 22 headers with the refusals' texts, every state line of core's own state-text tests, plain or raw literals (163 today, the reader taking 46 of them), read, written back and read again, and asked its state, reasons, health and conditions, every health and maintenance request, and PCG32 draws from five seeds with the edges of each range. ## C-250: veredas-oficina — the workshop: a model with no Qt, its window and the tests' driver behind features
- **Date:** 2026-11-25
- **Source:** user (docs/veredas-plan.md "veredas-oficina: the workshop"; docs/oficina-plan.md "Foundations", "The crate" and the own-shell spike's verdict; the architect's "start the oficina crate", 2026-11-25; docs/questions.md Q370, taken meanwhile); promoted from P-015 (the component)
- **Description:** `crates/veredas-oficina` is a library and one executable named `veredas-oficina`, at `2.0.0-alpha.1`, with a build script and its tests `component`, `tree` and `tree_model`. Its library's model (the project tree, the formats' components) builds with no Qt. Its window, Qt Quick with its logic in Rust through cxx-qt and KDDockWidgets docking, is built only with its feature `shell`, which the executable requires; the tests' driver (Spix's server inside the window, started when `OFICINA_SPIX_PORT` names a port) only with `driver`, which brings `shell` and which the window's tests require; nothing is on by default (Q370 taken meanwhile), so neither the suite's whole default build (`--workspace`) nor its test build (the tests' own dependencies with it) nor Oficina's own holds cxx, cxx-qt, cxx-qt-lib or cxx-qt-build on any target, while the shell's build holds all four, and the model and its tests build and link with no feature as a machine without Qt builds them (a fresh build folder, an empty home folder, no qmake on `PATH`, no library path, `QMAKE` naming none). The bridge is one private seam: cxx and cxx-qt are named only in the files under `src/shell/`, which hold its two bridges (the start and the tree); the library declares that module without `pub`, no file outside the seam names it but the library's one `pub use shell::run;`, and the seam re-exports nothing and declares its modules without `pub`, so its public items are the model's and `run` (a reading of the sources, against accidents). What ships carries no test server: the window built as the release tool builds it (`--release --locked --offline`, `shell` without `driver`), even with the environment set against it (cargo's variable for the driver feature and the driver's port), holds no name of Spix's or AnyRPC's where the driver's executable does, and started with a port named it stays up and nothing answers on that port; the build script reads its environment only by name (Qt's qmake, the C++ libraries' place, the home folder, the target's system) and binds `driver` once, to the compiler's word on the feature, naming it elsewhere only in the blocks it opens (a reading, against accidents). Its members among its normal and build dependencies are core alone, without core's default features: the one header reader (C-045) as C-073 offers it, so no crate of a plant's stack (tokio, the OPC UA crates, socket2, regex, chrono, duckdb) is in its graph, with the driver or without (L5: a component outside the map uses core alone; its `MAP` row comes with its first edge to portal). Its window opens titled "Oficina" with the project pane docked in it (the dock `project` open and not floating, the pane inside the docking area `workbench`, its parents reaching it, and drawn in the main window; closing the dock takes the pane from view and opening it brings the pane back), empty when no folder is named and saying nothing went wrong. The same kind of clause as the other components' own (C-200, C-220), for this crate. Its plan came before its code (L6) and it versions on its own from 2.0.0-alpha.1 (L3); no licence is stated (Q35), in its manifest or as a licence file it names.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-oficina/tests/component.rs, tools/leis/tests/real_tree.rs | `cargo metadata --no-deps`: the name and version, no licence and no licence file, its targets exactly the library, the executable requiring `shell`, the build script and the three tests (`component` and `tree` requiring `driver`), its features exactly `default` (empty), `shell` (the four optional crates) and `driver` (`shell`); `cargo tree --offline --locked --target all`: none of the four in the suite's default build with its dev edges (`--workspace -e normal,build,dev`) or Oficina's, all four with `--features shell`; `-p veredas-oficina -e normal,build` without features and with `driver`: core and none of the plant's crates; `cargo build -p veredas-oficina --lib --tests` with no feature into a fresh build folder, `HOME` and `USERPROFILE` an empty folder (cargo's and rustup's homes and rustc named), no folder holding a qmake on `PATH`, no `LIBRARY_PATH` or `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, `QMAKE` naming no qmake, `OFICINA_DEPS` unset: success; its path dependencies outside the dev table exactly core, no other member named; the sources read: the bridge named only under `src/shell/`, two bridges there, `mod shell;` the library's declaration of it, `pub use shell::run;` the only line outside the seam naming it, no `pub use` and no `pub` module in the seam; `build.rs` read with its comments set aside: every environment variable it reads named by a literal among `QMAKE`, `OFICINA_DEPS`, `HOME`, `USERPROFILE` and `CARGO_CFG_TARGET_OS`, none read through `vars`, `env!` or `option_env!`, `let driver = cfg!(feature = "driver");` once and the word `driver` nowhere else but `if driver {`; `cargo build --release --locked --offline --features shell --bin veredas-oficina` into a build folder of its own with `CARGO_FEATURE_DRIVER` and `OFICINA_SPIX_PORT` set: the executable's bytes holding none of `spix`, `Spix`, `AnyRpc` and `OFICINA_SPIX_PORT` (the driver's executable holding them), started on the test's platform with a free port named: running after 3 s and that port refusing a connection; the window through Spix on Qt's `offscreen` platform: its title, shown, the project pane drawn, the docking area `workbench` describing its dock `project` open, not floating, the pane inside the area and drawn in the main window, the dock closed and the pane no longer visible, opened and the pane visible again, the description the same, no row, its error line empty, Spix's own list of errors empty; the laws on the real tree
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-250.md
- **Status:** verified

## C-251: The project tree: a folder named on the command line drawn row for row, each folder read when it opens, a refresh that keeps what is open and selected
- **Date:** 2026-11-25
- **Source:** user (docs/oficina-plan.md "The first release": one project tree covering every component; the own-shell spike's point 1, `.zen/evidence/P-015.md`); promoted from P-015
- **Description:** Oficina started with a project folder (the first argument that does not begin with `--`, such an option before it passed over; Qt takes its own options away first) shows it in its project pane: the folder's first level, its folders first, then its files, each group by name with case folded, past ASCII too (an accented capital beside its lower case); a TOML file's row names its stamp as core's one header reader reads it (C-045, the same reader the project file's check holds every file of the suite to, C-046) and the component whose reader states the format (`veredas-bench 1 · Maquinar`), or for a suite release's files the tool that reads them (`veredas-release 1 · Alforge`), a format nobody states with its stamp alone (`veredas-panel 3`), a refusal in the reader's own words, character for character (a file with no `format`, a text that does not parse), a file over 1 MiB unread with its size while a stamped file of exactly 1 MiB is read, `Cargo.toml` as a cargo manifest, any other file by its name alone. A folder is read when a person opens it and not before, once (the window counts every folder its projects list from the disk: the root's reading alone when the project opens, one more when a folder opens); a file written into it after the folder above was read appears when it opens, and one written after it opened stays unshown, and nothing is read again, until Refresh, the project pane's dock closed and opened again meanwhile. Refresh reads again, in place, every folder read so far at any depth, those the person closed since among them (what the tree holds of a closed folder stays current for when it opens again), and leaves a closed folder closed. Through a refresh the open folders stay open and the current row stays current, by name, while a folder arrives before the open one, files arrive and one leaves before the current row (so it moves), one gains a stamp and a file arrives two levels down. Nothing reads the disk again but the Refresh button (a reading of the window's QML and the bridge, against accidents: the tree opened in one place, from the command line, and refreshed from the button alone, no timer; a folder read only where the view fetches it and where Refresh reads it). A name that is markup is drawn as its characters (plain text, the review's entry 34). The tree only shows what the reader says and decides nothing about a file: a row's stamp is the file's header, its `format` and `version` (C-045), not the time stamp portal's capture rules parse from a log line by ordered formats (C-038). Distinct from comissionador's command line (C-205), which names a project file to start a session from and run a test against: this one names the folder the tree shows.
- **Verification:** automated → crates/veredas-oficina/tests/tree.rs | the window through Spix on Qt's `offscreen` platform (`xcb` on X11 by `OFICINA_TEST_PLATFORM`), a planted project of thirteen first-level entries: the rows exactly, labels and order, the two refusals compared whole with what core's reader says of the same texts; no error shown; one read; a second project started after an option: `alpha`, `Zeta`, `A.txt`, `b.txt`, a stamped `exact.toml` of 1 048 576 bytes named by its stamp, a release file named `· Alforge`, `ábaco.txt`, `Ágape.txt`, in that order; the root read alone before `alpha` opens though a file was written into it, `alpha` read once when opened and showing that file; a file written after it opened, then the dock closed and opened: unshown, `alpha` still open, no read since; shown after Refresh with the root and `alpha` read again; `alpha` then closed and Refresh pressed: `alpha` still closed, both read again (six reads); with `alpha` and `alpha/inner` open and `project.toml` current, a folder added before `alpha`, two files added and one removed before the current row, one stamped and one added in `inner`, then Refresh: the rows exactly, `inner`'s new file under it, `project.toml` current three rows down and no other row current; the QML read: its only `open(` calls the tree's from the command line's argument and a dock's, `refreshOpen` once from the Refresh button, no `Timer`; the bridge read: `read_folder(` twice (the view's fetch, Refresh), `refresh_folder(` defined and called inside `refresh_open` alone, `refresh_open(` declared and defined, `Tree::open(` once, no `.expand(` or `.refresh(`
- **Evidence:** .zen/evidence/C-251.md
- **Status:** verified

### P-014: The suite's security round — requests a page of another site makes
- **Date:** 2026-11-16

- **Description:** The open items the architect left to the suite's security round, nothing built before it. Neither supervisão's host nor portal's server reads a request's `Host` or `Origin`, so a name re-pointed at the address (DNS rebinding) is the page's own origin to a browser, whose script reads every session's data and presses an armed write; and portal's write and send routes parse a body as JSON whatever its `Content-Type`, so a page of any site posts a write as `text/plain` while writes are armed, the site never asked first (read from the code, not yet shown by a test). Both hold for portal nested in supervisão and for portal on its own. Exit: the round's decisions, each item a C-clause proven by a test that sends the request and sees it refused, or a limit the architect states; C-049's limit then points at that clause. ### P-017: The suite's robustness round
- **Date:** 2026-11-19
- **Source:** user (the architect's call of 2026-11-16 for "a whole new spec definition round: robustness (overall, concerning all the suite's critical conponents)"); ingest (docs/robustness.md)
- **Description:** What a machine built on the suite needs to run unattended for decades, as ruled so far: every request that alters a controller is robust and a download carries a hash; what must survive is the data RUN needs; the program stored twice with a hash per block, rechecked at power-on and on a schedule; a cycle's data permanent only after a successful cycle; 64-bit targets only; the date unconfirmed until NTP or the clock chip confirms it, time-of-day blocks waiting, the clock battery's and NTP's maintenance requests by the architect's table; certificates ending on the last 32-bit date of 2038 with a request a year before; a maximum and a warning cycle time; retained variables by what the target can do (every cycle, at power loss, or the last save flagged), a write from outside saved at once; the embedded portal's store a window in memory (built under P-019: the window C-090, recording to the drive as the project's choice C-091, the store's copy at a fault C-093, each on an answer taken meanwhile, `docs/questions.md` Q41). It rules integrity; the authenticity of the same downloads and requests, a signature and a keyed check, is the security round's (P-014). The round is paused: five areas are not opened and the study of Linux for real time and recovery is owed (docs/questions.md Q36 to Q42).
- **Proposed verification:** the round finished with the architect, then each ruling a C-clause of the component it binds (controlador first, portal where embedded), proven by injecting the fault on the bench, or a limit the architect states.

### P-022: The tester machine — a suite release on a light Linux, as a VMware virtual machine
- **Date:** 2026-11-22

- **Description:** A virtual machine for VMware on a light Linux distribution holding a whole suite release (C-290's manifest, P-011's, every component at the version it pins), for testers and designers who run the suite without building it. It ships what P-011 cuts and adds no component. Amended 2026-11-22: planned in docs/alforge-plan.md (part two: who uses it, what it holds beside the executables, a bench-fed plant from the first boot, Chromium, a desktop, the docs; the image built without sudo by `tools/alforge` from a release bundle, a distribution's cloud image provisioned unattended under QEMU with KVM, flattened to a stream-optimized VMDK in an OVA; proven by booting the shipped disk under QEMU on VMware's device models with the plant reached from the host); its clause is C-292, built from the bundle C-291 cuts, verified 2026-11-23 (the OVA built by the recipe, its shipped disk booted under QEMU on VMware's device models, the plant reached from the host); P-022 stays pending until the architect has tried the OVA in VMware, C-292's stated remainder: the first release's machine, rebuilt by today's recipe from its own bundle and proven (2026-11-23), is `/home/thebdr/veredas-entrega/veredas-2.0.0-alpha.1.ova` with its SHA-256 beside it, the trial's steps in docs/alforge-plan.md "The architect's trial". Ruled 2026-11-25 (docs/questions.md): Debian 13 (Q433), Xfce (Q434), hardware version 14 on x86_64 (Q435), an OVA published nowhere yet (Q436), veredas/veredas (Q438), every interface (Q439), its own command (Q440), as taken; and an update command in the machine (Q437), its clause C-293. Exit: its plan, then the image built from a suite release by a recipe the repository holds.
- **Proposed verification:** the image built by the repository's recipe from a suite release and booted, the suite inside it run against the bench; each claim a C-clause.
